# Recent-Year AI Security Code-Audit Papers: Deep Comprehensive Ranking and Per-Paper Reviews

> Conclusion date: 2026-08-20
> Main search window: 2025-08-04 to 2026-08-04
> Research subject: 328 key papers within the window targeting "project-level AI security code-audit Agents", plus 1 out-of-window historical comparison paper.

## 1. The Bottom Line

### Overall Ranking

| Rank | Paper | Content value | Value to project | Positioning |
|---:|---|---:|---:|---|
| 1 | [2605.21824 QuartetFuzz](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21824) | **9.0/10** | **9.8/10** | Four Principles, controlled fuzzing, production-harness auditing, and real vulnerabilities form the most complete closed loop; the public artifacts cover only some experiments |
| 2 | [2606.04460 CyberGym-E2E](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04460) | **8.9/10** | **9.8/10** | 920 real vulnerabilities, 139 projects, and a four-stage execution oracle make this one of the strongest end-to-end security Agent benchmarks; the target-vulnerability capability must be judged by S4, not the higher S3 |
| 3 | [2607.12316 Antiproof](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12316) | **8.9/10**; fully confirmed only after public implementation | **9.8/10** | Method, scale, and real disclosure impact are the strongest; 510 PoE must not be treated as 510 confirmed vulnerabilities |
| 4 | [2605.17450 ContraFix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17450) | **8.9/10**; more stable once the reproduction package is accessible | **9.6/10** | Contrasting runtime evidence, RepairSpec, and empirical correction are the strongest; the semantic repair rate corresponding to 92% oracle pass is only 53.5% |
| 5 | [2608.00692 Vul4Py](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00692) | **8.8/10** | **9.8/10** | 100 real Python vulnerabilities simultaneously bound to three layers of oracle — exploit, functional, and human-semantic; the CVE/patch location is given and the public result file is missing |
| 6 | [2606.17283 ARVO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17283) | **8.8/10** | **9.8/10** | The largest-scale reconstructable, triggerable, modifiable real-vulnerability dataset; full reproduction is 6,138/8,921, with a small number mismatch between the paper text and public logs |
| 7 | [2607.18575 RECEIPT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18575) | **8.8/10** | **9.8/10** | Clean snapshot, role separation, PoC permissions, and browser sentinel form the hardest XSS validation protocol; 30/30 holds only within the authors' threat model and the artifacts are not public |
| 8 | [2605.05000 SLYP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05000) | **8.8/10** | **9.6/10** | Closed-source Windows binary discovery—debug—PoC and real disclosure are extremely strong; post-run ground truth and best-of-run inflate absolute effect |
| 9 | [2606.26216 CyberChainBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26216) | **8.8/10**; more stable once the paper's evaluation snapshot is public | **9.5/10** | The strongest real on-chain detect/exploit/patch execution benchmark; the currently public patch subset is inconsistent with the paper |
| 10 | [2607.04729 RustMizan](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04729) | **8.7/10** | **9.7/10** | Compilable, multi-context, contamination-aware, with 16 public Inspect logs being the most complete; 42 CVEs and single-run limits constrain extrapolation |
| 11 | [2601.19239 Project-Level Vulnerability Detection Empirical Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19239) | **8.7/10** | **9.7/10** | The most solid wide-domain real-world examination; the most worth following when designing project-level evaluation |
| 12 | [2604.01645 GONDAR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01645) | **8.7/10** | **9.5/10** | The hardest dynamic evidence chain; self-built benchmark and harness dependence slightly reduce external validity |
| 13 | [2510.14700 Web Vulnerability Reproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14700) | **8.7/10**; more stable once the reproduction package is accessible | **9.4/10** | The real-world examination over 80 real Web CVEs is solid; the best Success@3 is only 22.5%, and SQLi is 0% |
| 14 | [2509.01835 CVE-GENIE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.01835) | **8.6/10** | **9.6/10** | Executed reproduction of 428 of 841 CVEs; together with per-example logs and five OSF experiment packages this forms the most complete CVE-to-exploit artifact chain |
| 15 | [2510.23101 Staczzer / TDGF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.23101) | **8.6/10** | **9.5/10** | 41 real vulnerabilities at 40 runs per configuration, 58.4 CPU-years, 10 published CVEs, and 2 incomplete fixes make the goal-directed dynamic validation evidence very hard; no public core artifacts |
| 16 | [2606.26933 Chai](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26933) | **8.6/10** | **9.8/10** | Deterministic protocol differencing, Agent explanation, and downstream dependency tracking form a new crypto-auditing closed loop; two wolfSSL high-severity CVEs are confirmed, but the "100+" mixes in incomplete triage and there are no public artifacts |
| 17 | [2606.18619 Code-Augur](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18619) | **8.6/10** | **9.8/10** | specification-first and guided-fuzzing methodological innovation is the strongest, with real disclosures signaling maintainer/CVE/bounty; no source, data, or raw runs |
| 18 | [2510.21401 FLAMES](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21401) | **8.6/10** | **9.6/10** | Compile, semantic, functional, and exploit multi-layer validation is the most complete; the best 22/108 is confirmed, while some multi-round numbers disagree with the current raw CSV |
| 19 | [2607.00555 PHOENIX / SBIR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00555) | **8.6/10** | **9.8/10** | 26 maintainer confirmations and 20 merged patches make the real-world impact very hard; the SBIR cross-layer semantic bridge is extremely strong, but complete findings, prompts, and raw runs are not public |
| 20 | [2607.00107 VULBENCH-CPP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00107) | **8.5/10** | **9.1/10** | The multi-layer verifier and cluster-aware statistics over 8,918 C++ programs are very hard; ESBMC analyzability and the correct-code subset must be separated out |
| 21 | [2605.04251 Kumushi](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04251) | **8.5/10** | **9.7/10** | Blind review of 178 C/C++ vulnerabilities and 305 plausible patches proves that oracle pass does not equal root-cause repair; the paper claims open source but no dedicated public artifacts were found |
| 22 | [2606.13037 DIG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13037) | **8.5/10** | **9.8/10** | oracle synthesis, constraint inference, generator evolution, and low-level directed mutation form a complete one-day-vulnerability trigger closed loop; 138 Magma CVEs, 80 triggers, and 18-vulnerability ablation are strong, but there is no public implementation, the 6 new vulnerabilities lack per-item evidence, and the cost figure conflicts |
| 23 | [2607.25225 SecDrift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25225) | **8.4/10** | **9.0/10** | The statistics and public artifacts for industry-prompt security drift are the most solid; 14.0% vs. 11.4% is the SAST alert rate and is driven by CWE-502/CWE-22 composition effects |
| 24 | [2511.08462 QLCoder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08462) | **8.4/10** | **9.6/10** | The strongest CVE-to-CodeQL detector synthesis; given patch ground truth, it is not an open-ended finder |
| 25 | [2509.22097 SecureVibeBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22097) | **8.4/10** | **9.2/10** | The strongest real-vulnerability-introduction-scenario security benchmark; the PoV is very hard, while single-run and high-false-positive SAST make C-SEC a conservative lower bound |
| 26 | [2606.22263 REVELIO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22263) | **8.4/10** | **9.8/10** | High-recall hypothesis, strong-model PoV, and sanitizer gate form a hard validation chain; 10 maintainer fixes and 4 verifiable CVEs confirm real value; the main benchmark gives the vulnerable file and lacks complete artifacts |
| 27 | [2604.13611 V2E](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13611) | **8.4/10** | **9.4/10** | The PoC, trigger, and profit validation chain is very hard; it covers only 5 types of contract vulnerabilities, and 28.4% of samples are still MC |
| 28 | [2607.21676 KLEECopilot](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21676) | **8.4/10** | **9.8/10** | The large-scale real C/C++ directed symbolic-execution empirical study and artifacts are the hardest; among 87 independent sanitizer locations, random-path already covers 81, so the true increment should be written as 6, not 87 vulnerabilities |
| 29 | [2605.03952 MOSAIC-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03952) | **8.4/10** | **9.5/10** | One of the most worth adopting composable security regression benchmarks; 199 three-stage tasks and an execution oracle are strong; exact ASR, reviewer mean, FP, and tier must be corrected per artifact |
| 30 | [2605.10834 EthiBench / Real-World Pentesting Agents Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10834) | **8.3/10** | **9.6/10** | 108 expert ground truths, 27 setup-target-run combinations, and complete finding/matching artifacts form one of the strongest real-Agent evaluation protocols; only 3 public targets, and the LLM matcher's manual verification is still small |
| 31 | [2607.01764 Mastermind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01764) | **8.3/10** | **9.7/10** | strategy planner, task-local Curator, and dual-build verifier are strong; 84.5% is at most 8 runs after training, and the artifact is currently 404 |
| 32 | [2511.11019 PATCHEVAL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11019) | **8.3/10** | **9.7/10** | 1,000 CVEs and 230 dynamic dual-oracle repair tasks are strong; the original oracle has been updated, so the paper's 23% and the current 80%+ leaderboard are not the same snapshot |
| 33 | [2604.20179 LLMVD.js](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20179) | **8.3/10** | **9.6/10** | 433/517 public-benchmark and 244/259 NodeMedic PoCs are effective, and the Finder-Judge-execution funnel is strong; of the 36 Wild ones, only 3 got maintainer responses and there are no public core artifacts |
| 34 | [2604.10767 VulWeaver](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10767) | **8.3/10** | **9.4/10** | The neuro-symbolic UDG and holistic context are strong; open-ended whole-repository search capability is not yet proven |
| 35 | [2603.26270 KNOWDIT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26270) | **8.3/10**; fully confirmed only after public artifacts | **9.9/10** | The DeFi semantic knowledge graph and the specification-PoC-reflection closed loop are the strongest; 21/57/0 is the five-run union, and the artifacts are still redacted |
| 36 | [2601.22952 Sifting the Noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22952) | **8.2/10** | **9.4/10** | The most direct false-positive-reduction study, but the 22.25% real-vulnerability deletion must not be masked by denoising numbers |
| 37 | [2603.06858 PVBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06858) | **8.2/10** | **9.7/10** | 209 real vulnerabilities and PoC+ prove that basic validation produces 40%+ false success; public raw data still reproduces 41.26% FDR, but the paper/artifact snapshot and human labels are not fully aligned |
| 38 | [2602.14012 OpenVul / From SFT to RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14012) | **8.2/10** | **9.4/10** | pair-aware post-training, fine-grained reward, and complete open artifacts are strong; the judge reads CVE/patch, projects cross splits, and the test set is repeatedly used for model selection |
| 39 | [2605.21779 FuzzingBrain V2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21779) | **8.2/10**; the current repository strengthens engineering credibility | **9.7/10** | LLM-directed analysis, multi-fuzzer, sanitizer, and real disclosure chain are strong; the real-vulnerability numbers in the abstract and body drift severely |
| 40 | [2604.07624 PAGENT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07624) | **8.2/10** | **9.8/10** | Static rules, program analysis, coverage, and profiler feedback jointly guide the PoC Agent; 130 successes out of 203 vulnerabilities are strong; non-zero exit gate, attribution of 32 post-patch crashes, and the budget ledger still need hardening |
| 41 | [2605.15097 Veritas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15097) | **8.2/10**; more stable after scaling up and opening the core implementation | **9.6/10** | witness-backed static slicing, LLM claim, and runtime confirmation chain are strong; 20 cases, `-O0`, sampled FP, and the closed-source core limit extrapolation |
| 42 | [2511.12993 SmartPoC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.12993) | **8.2/10** | **9.6/10** | BCE, generate—repair—execute, and Action-State differential validation are complete; "64 bugs" are manually sampled TPs, not fully confirmed count |
| 43 | [2606.30587 Words Speak Louder Than Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30587) | **8.2/10** | **9.5/10** | The most systematic experiment on audit cognitive bias and metadata attacks; it mainly changes vulnerability-reporting propensity, not discrimination ability |
| 44 | [2607.24964 ALIBI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24964) | **8.2/10** | **9.8/10** | First systematic malicious-comment attack on a project-level audit Agent; the 90%+ ASR signal is strong, but ground truth, denominators, and defense evaluation have several hard flaws |
| 45 | [2604.06506 Sailor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06506) | **8.2/10**; more stable once the finding manifest and reproducer are public | **9.9/10** | CodeQL specifications, LLM path priority, and KLEE/ASan form the most complete static–symbolic-execution combination; 421 crashes are not 379 new vulnerabilities and the cost is about 2.29B tokens |
| 46 | [2603.04915 EVMbench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04915) | **8.2/10** | **9.4/10** | Detect/Patch/Exploit three-mode real-repository benchmark and grader are the strongest; Detect counts only historically known vulnerability recall and does not penalize extra false positives, and CI is not clustered by repository |
| 47 | [2605.21615 ASSEMBLAGE-DEEPHISTORY](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21615) | **8.2/10** | **9.2/10** | 73,610 cross-build/cross-version binaries, 248 projects, and 329 CVEs are a strong data asset; the public Eval configuration conflicts with the paper's final setup, and production raw outputs are missing |
| 48 | [2602.14345 AXE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14345) | **8.1/10** | **9.2/10** | The most reliable general post-alert dynamic exploitability confirmer; the task boundary is clear and the artifacts are complete |
| 49 | [2603.18740 Contextual Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18740) | **8.1/10** | **9.3/10** | PR metadata is an audit supply-chain attack surface; the 17/17 results need cross-model and public-artifact rechecking |
| 50 | [2601.13933 VulnResolver](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13933) | **8.1/10** | **9.4/10** | Repository context, safety-property instrumentation, and dynamic candidate selection are strong; 75% is SEC-bench verifier resolution |
| 51 | [2601.17833 Heimdallr](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17833) | **8.1/10**; confirmed only after core-system reproduction | **9.1/10** | Context clustering, knowledge reminders, and Verifier composition are complete; FPR convention, ground-truth fairness, and the closed-source core are penalized |
| 52 | [2606.01494 ClawHub Security Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01494) | **8.1/10** | **9.2/10** | 67,453 agent skills and three types of raw scanner signals confirm single-scanner blind spots; the registry verdict is only an automatic silver label, and SkillSpector and the verdict still have LLM circularity |
| 53 | [2605.26548 SEC-bench Pro](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26548) | **8.1/10** | **9.6/10** | Three-mirror execution scoring and the long-cycle PoC benchmark are strong; the tasks already give the target path/vulnerability type, and the manual judgment should be 480/3477/3985, while the original page's 469/3486/3983 does not close |
| 54 | [2606.31159 Security Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31159) | **8.0/10** | **9.5/10** | High confidence does not equal security; False Trust is extremely important, but some ECE conventions are affected by functional failure and filtering |
| 55 | [2604.20801 AgentFlow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20801) | **8.0/10** | **9.6/10** | typed harness IR, joint structure/prompt/tool search, and Chrome external confirmation are strong; the same 89 problems participate in both search and final reporting, so 84.3% is not a held-out generalization score |
| 56 | [2607.08949 SeedSmith](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08949) | **8.0/10** | **9.5/10** | 23 Magma, 115 ARVO, and 10 fuzzing runs prove that structured seeds can unlock hard-to-reach crashes; the 11–14x headline is inflated by timeout/one-shot and there are no public raw runs |
| 57 | [2508.21634 Human-Written vs. AI-Generated Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21634) | **8.0/10** | **8.5/10** | 507,044 paired tasks and public 1.16GB data form strong descriptive evidence; Semgrep rule hits cannot be directly interpreted as real vulnerability rates |
| 58 | [2607.27030 HoF-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27030) | **8.0/10** | **9.4/10** | 95 real AI-discovered CVEs, four repetitions, and strict identity adjudication are solid; the target file is given, all are positive examples, and precision/FPR are not measured |
| 59 | [2602.15485 SecCodeBench-V2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15485) | **8.0/10** | **9.5/10** | 98 industrial security generation/repair tasks, five languages, dual test oracle, Docker, and per-round raw results are strong; the paper lacks the model main experiment, and the 22/14 CWE and severity version drift need to be separated |
| 60 | [2607.26656 VulAgentRL / Graph Is the Verifier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26656) | **8.0/10**; more stable after public artifacts and fair reproduction | **9.7/10** | Using CPG as both investigation tool and evidence-reward verifier is novel; the teacher node is not human root-cause ground truth, and the frontier baseline lacks comparable CPG permissions |
| 61 | [2512.04611 PBFuzz](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04611) | **8.0/10**; more stable after public raw experiment artifacts | **9.7/10** | PIER, persistent memory, program analysis, typed parameter space, and property-based testing form the most complete PoV generation loop; 57 Magma vulnerabilities are strong, but the main experiment is single-run, the cost table is wrong, and there are no per-target raw results |
| 62 | [2508.21302 Locus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21302) | **8.0/10** | **9.1/10** | progress predicate and counterexample-validation method are strong; 41.6x is dominated by the SelectFuzz extreme value, the public table is about 40.80x, and KLEE timeout is fail-open in the current code |
| 63 | [2605.31135 R+R](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31135) | **8.0/10** | **9.0/10** | This batch's control experiment and main ledger are the most solid; known negative constraints can reduce Java secure-API misuse; it cannot be generalized to unknown vulnerabilities or zero-misuse real repositories |
| 64 | [2602.03012 CVE-Factory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03012) | **7.9/10** | **8.9/10** | High value for benchmark and training-data production; the 66.2% core number has a statistical-convention crack |
| 65 | [2607.13439 DREA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13439) | **7.9/10** | **9.4/10** | Planner/local-Explorer decoupling and Lucky Hit evaluation are strong; scale and cost are still limited |
| 66 | [2605.30105 EvoRepair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30105) | **7.9/10**; more stable after public code and order-isolation | **9.5/10** | Experience base, own-failure memory, and cross-vulnerability retrieval suit repair Agents; 90.46% is the cumulative result over up to 15 rounds, and in-test-set experience accumulation has order dependence |
| 67 | [2512.07533 VulnLLM-R](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07533) | **7.9/10**; confirmed only after Agent and zero-day reproduction | **9.6/10** | Dedicated security reasoning model with strong cross-linguistic OOD; the current artifacts lack the paper's Agent, five-project raw runs, and 15 zero-day evidence |
| 68 | [2607.05474 ShadowProbe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05474) | **7.9/10** | **9.5/10** | Static filtering, project dependency recovery, worst-case input, and dynamic-growth inference form a strong complexity evidence chain; real-world findings, raw timing, and cost are still unreviewable |
| 69 | [2601.17471 PatchIsland](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17471) | **7.9/10** | **9.7/10** | Five repair-Agent types, best-of-N, and failure-aware scheduling are strong; the official AIxCC human confirmation provides real external evidence; 84/92 are only plausible patches, and the official correct patch is 31/42 |
| 70 | [2509.11787 CodeCureAgent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11787) | **7.8/10** | **8.9/10** | Artifact and engineering completeness are extremely strong; 96.8% is the plausible disposition rate, and security types are only 31/1000 |
