- **Batch 48 has the most complete fuzz-driver method**: MASFuzzer; multi-dimensional API sequences and an adaptive scheduler are worth borrowing; 261 tests pass but raw runs, fixed versions, and some Docker inputs are missing, and the 16 vulnerabilities must be listed separately as 12 fixed, 2 confirmed-only, 2 reported.
- **Batch 48's false-positive-governance core**: FalseCrashReducer; caller/entry constraints are worth directly implementing, but the 15% headline and the 57%-65% model self-rating cannot be cited as real filtering rate.
- **Batch 48's repair-validation core**: TerraProbe and Automated V&V; the former provides a five-layer oracle and the latter provides container execution, and both must add independent oracle, A/B differential, and real artifact closure.
- **Batch 48's Agent architecture core**: FORGE handles the task forest, and Hound handles the relational graph and long-term hypotheses; same-source LLM validation cannot replace heterogeneous tools or dynamic evidence.
- **Batch 48's product blueprint**: Orion; seed-then-harness, triage, reproducer, and patch handoff are clear, but currently there is no code, raw benchmark, or disclosed-vulnerability evidence.
- **Batch 48 OCR recheck conclusion**: 8 PDFs, 147 pages total; 77%, 15%, 72.3%, 31.64/1.36, Qwen 9 lines, Hound's missing percent sign, and 92/204x were all verified against original pages, LaTeX, artifacts, or external evidence, not OCR misreads.

- **Batch 49 has the strongest data asset**: ASSEMBLAGE-DEEPHISTORY; the 73,610/248/329 scale broadly closes, but the paper's final 3600s/no-cap drifts from the public 1800s/1000-turn configuration, and raw outputs are missing.
- **Batch 49 has the strongest method blueprint**: PCodeTrans; in-place function replacement and differential trajectories suit binary-audit Agents, but BE greater than CS, Table 1/2 non-closure, and no artifacts mean the 99% headline is not citable.
- **Batch 49's context-defense core**: Semantic Consensus Decoding; the dual-flow idea is worth copying, and the CodeLlama-SCD missing samples and pseudo-adaptive attack must be redone.
- **Batch 49's examiner paradigm**: ConneX; GT participating in out-of-scope and the scorer pollutes deployment evaluation, and the full micro-F1 is 0.969270, not the headline 0.9746.
- **Batch 49's knowledge infrastructure**: SecretLoc and PatchSeeker; the former needs candidate/confirmation/prevalence separated, and the latter needs multi-positive qrels, output coverage, and training config unified.
- **Batch 49's weakest evidence**: Solidity Zero-Shot Benchmark and TaintSentinel; the former's 45/48 rows of discrete metrics have no integer solution, and the latter's data, ROC, labels, and code are severely distorted.
- **Batch 49 OCR recheck conclusion**: 8 PDFs, 125 pages total; BE/CS, Recall/ME, 0.817/0.825, 37/5/4/20, AUC plot, 503,627/469,554, and 45/48 no-solution were all verified against original pages, LaTeX, code, or raw artifacts, not OCR misreads.

- **Batch 50 has the strongest method system**: AutoDecompiler; the feedback classification and multi-round counterexample correction are worth directly transferring, but the test oracle and extra inference budget must be separated from model capability.
- **Batch 50 has the strongest historical evidence chain**: MAS-SZZ; the Java-SZZ headline can be partially recomputed, but the formula, experimental unit, and fail-open stopping rule still need fixing.
- **Batch 50 has the strongest product deployment**: Cross-Domain Vulnerability Detection; the multi-domain/PR/developer loop is valuable, but it currently only proves SAST label transfer and the data/F1/dedup ledger does not close.
- **Batch 50's multilingual-repair warning**: Multilingual Vulnerability Repair; same CVE/URL cross-split exceeds 80%, GPT-4-0613 is mislabeled as GPT-4o, and text matching cannot replace dynamic repair correctness.
- **Batch 50's training-signal core**: Hierarchy-Aware CWE Training; keep the default 8.3% directional result, rejecting the test-selected 27.9%, the pseudo-duplicate p=0.005, and the non-isolated hierarchy-causal conclusion.
- **Batch 50's weakest evidence**: SDLLMFuzz and Attack-to-Vulnerability Mapping; the former has budget/plot/ablation conflicts and zero artifacts, and the latter's ground-truth chain is itself wrong.
- **Batch 50 OCR recheck conclusion**: 8 PDFs, 137 pages total; the Precision/Recall denominators, APA 93.33%, cosine 0.9934, 1w-1M TTB, 47/60 bug count, Welch numbers, and split ratios were all re-checked against original pages/LaTeX/artifacts, not OCR misreads.

- **Batch 51 has the highest project value**: PSSec; the analyzer-in-the-loop and repair loop are almost directly transferable, but FSucRate is only the analyzer-clean rate.
- **Batch 51 has the strongest multilingual architecture**: MulVuln; the language parameter pool is worth copying, and the near-97% CVE/commit cross-split overlap means the 72.20% can only serve as an overlap-benchmark result.
- **Batch 51 has the most complete training system**: SmartCoder-R1; the public 756 results close, but the FuncRate similarity oracle and same-source security rules mean the headline must be retested.
- **Batch 51's retrieval/adaptation is most worth reproducing**: Llama Prompt vs Fine-tuning; prefer the classification-head QLoRA + RAG, and Double tuning's 12 updates per sample is not the same budget as a normal baseline.
- **Batch 51's weakest evidence**: HYDRA and Agentic Supply Chain Defense; the former's interface dimension does not hold and has no ground truth, and the latter's main numbers are just ledger-less TikZ coordinates.
- **Batch 51 OCR recheck conclusion**: 8 PDFs, 126 pages total; 0.9330/0.9930, 773/768, 0.970/0.997, Table 7's ms/seconds, 24.89/9.96, and all split ratios were re-checked against original pages/LaTeX/vector plots/artifacts, not OCR misreads.

- **Batch 52 has the highest project value**: MOSAIC-Bench; composable tickets, final-state PoC, and reviewer comparison are best-suited for directly becoming the audit Agent regression benchmark.
- **Batch 52 has the most credible experiment ledger**: R+R; the main count and stress test close best, but zero-misuse is limited to the known taxonomy, valid subset, and current benchmark.
- **Batch 52 has the strongest architecture inspiration but the weakest evidence**: MARD; tool forensics and verifier are worth copying, but 7,825 APKs, time generalization, and cost headline are unreliable.
- **Batch 52 has the strongest artifacts**: MOSAIC-Bench; 199 chains and the execution oracle are very complete, but Kimi, reviewer mean, Gemma FP, and tier still need correction per the artifacts.
- **Batch 52's most typical mathematical degradation**: FusionVul; the single-vector attention is constant 1, and the dataset-fixed mu1/mu2 is not sample-aware.
- **Batch 52 OCR recheck conclusion**: 8 PDFs, 164 pages total; 44/44 vs. .82/.96/.88, 232-233 vs. 228, 50/1/6/67, 136 vs. CSV 137, 24.8 vs. 25.8, 28/608 vs. valid 28/533, MOCHA open/gated, and all formulas were re-checked against original pages/LaTeX/artifacts, not OCR misreads.

### Four Ranking Lists

#### 1. Experimental Credibility

| Rank | Paper | Credibility | Core reason |
|---:|---|---:|---|
| 1 | QuartetFuzz | **9.2/10** | 100-case repeated fuzzing, Wilcoxon, ablation, 586 production harnesses, and maintainer confirmation support each other; full raw runs and RQ1/RQ5 artifacts are still missing |
| 2 | 2601.19239 | **9.0/10** | Real tools, real projects, known vulnerabilities, manual alert verification, and cost data form complementary evidence |
| 3 | GONDAR | **9.0/10** | 54 vulnerabilities, multiple strong baselines, ablation, cost, and dynamic sanitizer; the self-built benchmark limits extrapolation |
| 4 | ContraFix | **8.8/10** | 425 instances, same-backbone ablation, three runs, an execution oracle, and two-person semantic audit; Figshare could not be verified this time |
| 5 | VULBENCH-CPP | **8.8/10** | 8,918 programs, a four-layer verifier, task-level mixed-effects, and a complete reproduction package are strong; a large number of ESBMC-unanalyzable cases limit the formal conclusion |
| 6 | SecDrift | **8.8/10** | 5,355 runs, matched baseline, placebo, mixed-effects, CWE sensitivity, a 90-case two-person missed-detection audit, and public raw artifacts support each other; the core outcome is still the SAST flag |
| 7 | CyberGym-E2E | **8.7/10** | 920 real vulnerabilities and 139 projects, a four-stage oracle, and task-level scripts are strong; S3 can fix non-target vulnerabilities, so target capability should be judged by the hidden-PoC S4 |
| 8 | Vul4Py | **8.7/10** | 100 real vulnerabilities, dual oracle, human-semantic audit, and cost reinforce each other; the task gives the CVE/patch location and the public result file is missing |
| 9 | Web Vulnerability Reproduction | **8.7/10** | 80 real CVEs, container/PoC/oracle, three runs, and staged verification are complete; the public package is currently 403 |
| 10 | CyberChainBench | **8.6/10** | 541 real events, historical mainnet fork, and executable exploit/patch oracle; the paper's evaluation snapshot is not fully consistent with the current public subset |
| 11 | CVE-GENIE | **8.6/10** | 841 CVEs, execution verifier, strong ablation, and per-item logs for 428 examples; the current repository's 429 directory and the paper's 428 are not yet aligned |
| 12 | ARVO | **8.6/10** | 8,921 full candidates, 6,138 successful reproductions, and public images/metadata/manual logs are strong; there is a per-example-level difference between body and logs, and the full cost is very high |
| 13 | RustMizan | **8.5/10** | 173 compilable variants, vulnerable/safe pair, context ladder, and 16 public logs are strong; 42 CVEs and each setting run only once |
| 14 | AXE | **8.5/10** | 40 containerized targets, complete logs, 12 PoCs, and fresh-environment replay correspond to each other |
| 15 | EthiBench | **8.5/10** | 108 ground truths, three independent runs, and the full public chain of raw findings/matchings/results/cost; Claude Code 58.22% F1 is reproducible; the matcher is validated on only 50 balanced samples, and only 3 targets |
| 16 | Sifting the Noise | **8.4/10** | Simultaneously measures FP identification and TP retention, with a post-cutoff two-person annotation set and a complete recomputation package |
| 17 | FLAMES | **8.4/10** | 5,000 compile results, 108 real heists, functional tests, and raw CSV cross-verification; split is not isolated by contract family, and some multi-round triples cannot be fully recomputed |
| 18 | Staczzer | **8.4/10** | 41 real vulnerabilities × 40 runs, 58.4 CPU-years, a unified 24h cap, sign test, and 10 published CVEs are hard; no raw run/core artifacts is penalized |
| 19 | RECEIPT | **8.4/10** | 95 real applications, 30 browser-replayed TPs, 45 known targets, and cumulative-verifier ablation support each other; single-model single-run and complete artifacts not public |
| 20 | 2606.04739 Revisiting Vul-RAG | **8.3/10** | Independent team, open weights, local deployment, repeated runs, public raw results, and proactive reporting of reproduction failure |
| 21 | Kumushi | **8.3/10** | 178 cases, 305 plausible-patch blind review, root-cause labels, pairwise sign test, and κ are supported; no public patch/code artifacts |
| 22 | SecCodeBench-V2 | **8.3/10** | 98 tasks, 90 dual-single-test cases, Docker, per-round code and test logs are genuinely public; the internal raw cases cannot be checked, 8 tasks contain an LLM judge, and the paper/artifact versions drift |
| 23 | ClawHub Security Signals | **8.3/10** | 67,453 lines of full signals, fixed data SHA, per-scanner status, and set metrics are reproducible; the registry verdict is still an automatic silver label and the current repository lacks analysis scripts |
| 24 | SecureVibeBench | **8.2/10** | 105 real-repository tasks, functional tests, and PoV are hard; single-run and about half of Semgrep suspicious alerts not being vulnerabilities limit the absolute security rate |
| 25 | SLYP | **8.2/10** | Real CVE/bounty and debugger PoC are hard; post-run constructed ground truth, selecting the strongest of three runs per object, and no artifacts reduce controlled-experiment credibility |
| 26 | Words Speak Louder Than Code | **8.2/10** | 8 models, three heuristics, very strong correlation, and confidence intervals support "modulating vulnerability-reporting propensity"; single-run, ASR-conditional denominator, and no artifacts limit extrapolation |
| 27 | Chai | **8.2/10** | 47 libraries, three-protocol differencing, and two official wolfSSL CVEs are hard; "100+" mixes untriaged PoCs, no public artifacts, and protocol-dedicated harnesses are down-weighted |
| 28 | KLEECopilot | **8.2/10** | 12 real programs, 13 baselines, five 10-hour runs per group, public heavy artifacts, and 12/18 developer confirmations support each other; only 6 of the 87 locations exceed random-path |
| 29 | LLMVD.js | **8.1/10** | Public benchmark 433/517, NodeMedic 244/259, and the Wild funnel give scale; 69 automatic-oracle false successes, only 3 maintainer responses, and no public artifacts |
| 30 | HoF-Bench | **8.1/10** | 95 real CVEs, fixed vulnerable commits, four repetitions, and 7,600 pass records are strong; given target files, all positives, and LLM-judge calibration still limit extrapolation |
| 31 | Phoenix | **8.0/10** | Public raw outputs and evaluation scripts reproduce 0.8246 F1; the pair denominator and original PrimeVul data are still incomplete |
| 32 | VulWeaver | **8.0/10** | Public code, RQ1-RQ7 data, and real-project manual verification are relatively complete; the paper and README have slight convention drift |
| 33 | Code-Augur | **8.0/10** | Two benchmarks, real disclosures, CVE/bounty, and specification-lane analysis support each other; no source, manifest, PoV, or raw runs limit independent recheck |
| 34 | Hybrid-CASR | **8.0/10** | 42 time-forward windows, dedup, Wilcoxon, and effect-size reporting are restrained; the co-success window adds only 0.016 F1, and Phi-2 may have seen historical vulnerabilities |
| 35 | CodeCureAgent | **7.9/10** | 1,000 inputs, logs, diffs, CSV, and recomputation scripts are complete; the 291 manual cases are stratified by rule rather than random, and security samples are only 31 |
| 36 | Antiproof | **7.9/10** | Scale, ablation, and real-disclosure signals are strong, but the core implementation and most raw results are not yet public |
| 37 | V2E | **7.8/10** | 264 expert annotations, PoC, and execution feedback are relatively hard; 28.4% MC, the profit threat model, and unpublished paper data limit extrapolation |
| 38 | Contextual Bias | **7.8/10** | Includes 14,910 controlled experiments and 17 real ACR attacks; cross-tool extrapolation and public artifacts are still insufficient |
| 39 | PHOENIX / SBIR | **7.8/10** | Of 31 reports, 26 got maintainer confirmation and 20 patches were merged; three representative patches are verifiable in the PyTorch main line; missing complete manifest, prompts, SBIR, and raw runs |
| 40 | EduPluginBench | **7.8/10** | 120 clean, 1,440 mutants, clustered CI, held-out/operator stress, and full-denominator reporting are complete; the construct oracle and detector share specification vocabulary, and the complete-artifact entry is missing |
| 41 | IPv6 Periphery / HLEV | **7.8/10** | 73 regions, 164 ISPs, 281.92M devices, and active ICMPv6 validation give large scale; RGPS and historical single-prefix comparison have selection bias, and version mapping is not exploitability ground truth |
| 42 | Mastermind | **7.7/10** | 1,507 CyberGym tasks and multi-round strategy experiments are strong; 84.5% is at most 8 runs after training, split isolation is not proven, and the code link is 404 |
| 43 | DIG | **7.7/10** | 138 Magma CVEs, multi-category baselines, 18-vulnerability five-time repetition, and ablation are strong; no public raw runs, the 6 new vulnerabilities lack per-item evidence, and the cost figure conflicts |
| 44 | OpenVul | **7.7/10** | Pairs do not cross splits, code/model/HF data are public, and the main table is reproducible; the judge reads CVE/patch, 44/115 test projects appear in train, and the raw time-split construction is missing |
| 45 | PATCHEVAL | **7.6/10** | 1,000 CVEs and 230 dynamic dual-oracle tasks are strong; the original PoC oracle has been revised by the authors, and the current repository cannot reproduce the paper's old leaderboard |
| 46 | KNOWDIT | **7.6/10** | 331 contests, AuditEval, and five-time repetition give scale; 21/57/0 is union@5, confirmed disclosures lack per-item evidence, and the Figshare link is still redacted |
| 47 | CWEEP | **7.6/10** | PDF, fixed commits, and the public workbook can close the 51 alerts and the 0.608/0.515 overall precision; complete recall ground truth is missing and BugWhisperer is not fully manually reviewed |
| 48 | QLCoder | **7.5/10** | 176 CVEs, strong ablation, and public implementation; uses patch-target feedback and has no paper raw results |
| 49 | Human-Written vs. AI-Generated Code | **7.5/10** | 507,044 paired tasks, 16,928 repositories, and public data are strong; security results are still un-manually-confirmed Semgrep rule hits, and code length plus empty output are clearly confounded |
| 50 | Sailor | **7.5/10** | 6.8M LOC, 87,385 specs, 1,345 candidates, 421 ASan crashes, and substantial code are strong; the 379 finding manifest/reproducer is withheld, and location dedup plus 2.29B tokens reduce credibility |
| 51 | VulAgentRL | **7.5/10** | repository-level split, PrimeVul/TitanVul OOD, P-C, and tool-call ledger are valuable; the teacher node is not human root cause, baseline permissions are unequal, and core artifacts have no entry |
| 52 | DREA | **7.4/10** | Dataset and implementation are public, with human-judge validation; scale is small and the paper's original run logs are missing |
| 53 | From Lab to Reality | **7.4/10** | Cross-dataset and out-of-time pair evaluation is solid; VentiVul has only 20 CVEs, and LLM raw results are not public |
| 54 | Veritas | **7.4/10** | 18 of 20 stripped OOB detected, 623 exhaustive candidates with 0 FP; another 3,344 candidates only spot-check 334, and the core implementation is closed-source |
| 55 | VulnResolver | **7.4/10** | 200 real issues, dynamic properties, and a functional gate are relatively strong; baselines not fully unified, no repeated variance, no implementation, and verifier is not full semantic ground truth |
| 56 | VulnLLM-R | **7.4/10** | Cross-lingual OOD, public model, and partial data are verifiable; ARVO localization oracle, missing Agent, data-snapshot drift, and zero-day without evidence are penalized |
| 57 | REVELIO | **7.4/10** | 19 real findings, 10 maintainer fixes, and 4 official CVEs provide external evidence, and cost/main table are reproducible; CyberGym gives the vulnerable file, ablation is only 10 cases, and no code/raw PoV |
| 58 | TRACE | **7.4/10** | 15 CVEs, 5,000 on-chain contracts, and 83 repositories have multi-layer experiments, and the public CSV is reproducible; real set validates only positives, and the intro, main table, and LLM-change gate have convention conflicts |
| 59 | HardSecBench | **7.4/10** | 924 task assets are per-item reproducible; but the paper writes the 70.2% mutation of 1,170 initial-pass tasks as the retained result, and Verilog coverage is only a static-signal mention |
| 60 | SeedSmith | **7.4/10** | 23 Magma × 4 fuzzers × 10 trials, 115 ARVO, and cost/ablation are relatively complete; timeout/one-shot dominates the headline, with no multiple-testing correction and no raw runs |
| 61 | PatchIsland (`2601.17471`) | **7.4/10** | 92 internal-benchmark cases, repeated runs, and official AIxCC human confirmation reinforce each other; plausible/correct, vulnerability coverage, and PoV dedup confuse multiple denominators, and anonymous artifacts remain incomplete |
| 62 | EVMbench (`2603.04915`) | **7.4/10** | Three-mode tasks and executable grader are strong; Detect does not penalize out-of-report false positives, bootstrap is not repository-clustered, and 117/120 instances drift from the bounty artifact |
| 63 | AgenticRepair | **7.3/10** | SEC-Bench official page supports 150/200; single-run, unfair budget, functional oracle, and complete implementation are still insufficient |
| 64 | SecCoderX | **7.3/10** | Code, data, and model artifacts are complete; the security reward is still a learned classifier averaging F1 67.9, and raw results are not attached |
| 65 | SmartPoC | **7.3/10** | Two benchmarks and post-execution differential validation are valuable; latest-114 only manually spot-checks the confirmed/rejected subset, and no code, data, or raw results |
| 66 | Security Calibration | **7.2/10** | Large-scale repeated generation and execution tests are strong; safety/functional construct mixing, table conventions, and the currently-404 artifact are penalized |
| 67 | PVBench | **7.2/10** | 209 vulnerabilities, three AVR sets, and public raw data confirm a 40%+ FDR; the paper tables, public snapshot, and human taxonomy are still not fully aligned |
| 68 | CVE-Factory | **7.2/10** | PatchEval cross-validation is relatively strong, but the 66.2% denominator and body numbers are inconsistent |
| 69 | PyVul | **7.2/10** | Real Python packages, manual sampling, and multi-tool failure analysis are solid; public-data denominator drift, abnormal function/commit mapping, and identical-code cross-set duplicates in split |
| 70 | A.S.E | **7.2/10** | 120 repository tasks and 26 models give scale, and public v1 data is inspectable; the Security oracle only looks at alert decline, with `latest` image, internal number errors, and no raw leaderboard penalized |
| 71 | LASiR (`2511.09134`) | **7.2/10** | The 500-contract main confusion matrix and 31 real cases are relatively strong; abstract F1, baseline rows, DB1 cross-chain, and PoC count have version drift |
| 72 | Heimdallr | **7.1/10** | Real projects, Verifier ablation, and cost data are relatively strong; FPR convention, ground-truth handling, single-project stability pilot, and closed-source core are penalized |
| 73 | DrillAgent | **7.1/10** | 55/190, same-budget 15/60 vs. 6/60, and artifact list are checkable; ablation selects only the Full-success subset, and the nominal budget is also not a hard cutoff |
| 74 | LeanGuard | **7.1/10** | 801 samples, 15 settings all directionally positive, three repeated runs, and 6/9 significant; both classes single-run, n=3 testing, unreviewed labels, and inaccessible anonymous artifacts |
| 75 | Code Representation / FPR | **7.1/10** | 2,740 Java samples, complete negatives, two models, Python replication, and FPR/recall decomposition are relatively strong; still function-level benchmark, single training trajectory, and no public reproduction package |
| 76 | PAGENT | **7.1/10** | 203 vulnerabilities, program-analysis ablation, and sanitizer execution evidence are strong; 130/203 vs. 64.6% does not close, exit gate and post-patch root-cause verification are insufficient, and artifacts currently 401 |
| 77 | FuzzingBrain V2 | **7.0/10** | 36/40 dynamic results and 26 maintainer confirmations are strong; abstract/body number drift, unfair AIxCC resources, and paper raw runs not public |
| 78 | LogicScan | **7.0/10** | Two vulnerability sets, a production low-prior set, and ablation are relatively complete; unclear knowledge-base isolation, Top200 benign assumption, and 410 artifact are clearly penalized |
| 79 | RealSec-bench | **7.0/10** | 105 real-repository tasks, 89 manual alert validations, and public raw outputs are valuable; security recall holds only within the CodeQL-alert candidate space |
| 80 | SWExploit | **7.0/10** | 300 SWE-bench Lite tasks, 12 settings, and public prediction data support the attack surface; dynamic PER is far below static ASR, and the source repository is essentially empty |
| 81 | PRISM | **7.0/10** | 8,119 pairs, a 200 manual subset, 353 taint pairs, and multi-model comparison have a foundation; Good-Turing self-contradiction, pair clustering, and small 12-CVE-pair samples limit conclusions |
| 82 | TraceLLM | **7.0/10** | 148 real events, 27 expert reports, and module experiments are realistic; the 121-case review protocol, baseline task symmetry, repeated runs, and dedicated artifacts are insufficient |
| 83 | PBFuzz | **7.0/10** | 57 Magma vulnerabilities, strong baselines, and some repeated experiments support the main direction; the full 129-CVE main experiment is single-run, no per-target raw run, and TTE and cost ledgers are incomplete |
| 84 | ShadowProbe | **6.8/10** | PyEmailBench confusion matrix and ablation are reproducible, and the dynamic-validation contribution is clear; manual ground truth, 162 findings, raw timing, and cost cannot be independently verified |
| 85 | Diffploit | **6.8/10** | Four ablations are precisely reproducible from public JSON, and 3 CNA updates are externally verifiable; the main-table row and 590, the headline 580, and the public 579+1 flaky conflict each other |
| 86 | MAPTA | **6.8/10** | 104 metrics/trace reproduce 80/104, cost, and tokens; single-run, CTF without architecture ablation, and 19 real findings lack per-item evidence |
| 87 | TEERepair | **6.8/10** | 89 cases, 172 tests, manual root-cause review, and 2 merged PRs are supported; baseline not same-task, three percentage rows off by two orders of magnitude, and public repository lacks per-example results |
| 88 | DeepTraVul (`2603.27067`) | **6.8/10** | Public CSV reproduces the 235-case confusion matrix, and the union of five detection lists basically closes; table pasting, time split, 2,402/2,204, and applicable denominator are inconsistent |
| 89 | AVIATOR | **6.7/10** | Repeated runs and multi-dataset design are relatively strong; high-intensity samples are small, oracle mixed, external baseline borrowed, and no artifacts |
| 90 | T2L | **6.7/10** | Real ARVO cases, layered metrics, and error analysis are valuable; 50 manual curated cases, baseline migration, ATA not pure ablation, and code is 404 |
| 91 | EvoRepair | **6.6/10** | 389/430 is the cumulative verifier success over up to 15 rounds; same-scaffold gain about 9.3 points, cross-task memory during test creates order dependence, and no code |
| 92 | SemTaint | **6.6/10** | 172 known-missed vulnerabilities and 106 recovered results give scale; test does not measure FP, three-run union, OSS only deeply checks 10/102, and no artifacts |
| 93 | CPRVUL | **6.5/10** | Three datasets and same-model ablation give context gain; missing clean function-only baseline, project-level ground truth, multi-seed/CI, and the reproduction repository is nearly empty |
| 94 | AgentFlow | **6.5/10** | TerminalBench numbers and public optimizer implementation are relatively complete, and Chrome has an external CVE; the same 89 problems used for search and final score constitute the main validity threat |
| 95 | 2512.16538 Obfuscation Study | **6.4/10** | Scale and coverage are strong, but transformation correctness, sample-preserving vulnerability, and statistical-uncertainty reporting are insufficient |
| 96 | SecureReviewer | **6.4/10** | Public data and code are inspectable, and the main improvement arithmetic holds; Non-Issue, random split, duplicate patch, 262/261/260 denominators, and same-set SecureBLEU tuning are penalized |
| 97 | SecureForge | **6.4/10** | 500 prompts, 2,500 held-out samples, and 47,760 rollouts give scale; the same SAST oracle participates in both optimization and evaluation, and the symmetric-proposal core is an unverified assumption |
| 98 | SCRUTINEER | **6.4/10** | 42 TP, 10 FP, 9 FN are reproducible, and the deterministic gate filters 72.91% of LLM candidates; independent positives are only 51 events, and 13 zero-day/9 CVE with artifacts are unverifiable |
| 99 | AgentSZZ (`2604.02665`) | **6.4/10** | Relative F1 gains on four datasets are reproducible, and the investigation-trajectory design is reasonable; total samples, mini-SWE F1, token/time, and error taxonomy conflict in several places mathematically |
| 100 | TPL-Benchmark | **6.4/10** | Large-scale ground truth and ten-tool raw outputs are strong; 946-app bias, answer-containing candidate set, secret-total conflict, and complete APK/library/evaluator not yet public |
| 101 | SEC-bench Pro | **6.4/10** | The 344-set three-mirror and execution-adjudication framework is strong; same-page manual adjudication should be 480 verified/3477 illegal/3985 total candidates, but the paper writes 469/3486/3983 |
| 102 | HarnessAgent | **6.4/10** | Table 4 reproduces 202/243; the body's old rate, Venn unique counts, coverage denominator, ablation integrality, and cost denominator all have version drift |
| 103 | R+R (`2605.31135`) | **6.4/10** | Main ledger closes and design is systematic; valid-conditioning, in-taxonomy teaching, manual patching, clustered samples, and contamination limit strong conclusions |
| 104 | SPECA | **6.3/10** | Real competition ground truth is valuable; V2 is an after-the-fact retrofit and re-evaluation on the same data, and the model/artifact is opaque |
| 105 | SolAgent | **6.3/10** | 81 files, 1,188 tests, three models, and complete ablation are reproducible from the public database; Slither simultaneously optimizes and evaluates, and security findings are not real vulnerabilities |
| 106 | MOSAIC-Bench (`2605.03952`) | **6.3/10** | Broad coverage and strong execution oracle; but retained-set, single trial, BROKEN exclusion, non-IID, and several table-convention errors limit extrapolation |
| 107 | Co-RedTeam | **6.2/10** | Multiple benchmarks and a large execution ablation are valuable; no raw runs, and evolving memory across test tasks has order dependence |
| 108 | KASS | **6.2/10** | 104 SmartBugs, same-protocol Claude Code, dual-loop ablation, and partial Foundry archives are supported; the public package lacks the core Agent, unified adjudication, and many required tests/logs for 98/104 |
| 109 | VulAgent | **6.2/10** | The counterexample process on PrimeVul/SVEN pairs is supported, but P-C is about 26% and FPR 36.78%-55.42%, the context comparison is not same-sample, and artifacts are 410 |
| 110 | MPC-Patch-Bench | **6.2/10** | 205 cases, eight models, and functional/security dual leaderboards are valuable; gold-file scope, 163 synthetic tasks, super-context exclusion, and uncalibrated verifier limit absolute credibility |
| 111 | Library Hallucinations / LibHalluBench | **6.2/10** | Complete output reproduces the main table; 963 malformed prompts, repeated tasks, dynamic PyPI drift, no-code/error denominators, and member-detector defects need recomputation |
| 112 | Antares | **6.2/10** | 500-task repository-localization evaluation and multi-scale model comparison are valuable; GHSA/task duplication, patch-file ground truth, unknown training contamination, and unpublished 3B/CLI weaken conclusions |
| 113 | ASSEMBLAGE-DEEPHISTORY (`2605.21615`) | **6.2/10** | Scale ledger and appendix CVE are relatively complete; Eval configuration, reference-only convention, DB fix version, and production raw outputs are not yet locked |
| 114 | RuleForge | **6.1/10** | Has production traffic, 100 judges, and 50-CVE comparison; labels mainly depend on IP reputation, absolute denominators are confidential, and the system is closed-source |
| 115 | AI-Generated Python Refactoring PRs | **6.1/10** | 438 real PRs, 747 analyzable commits, and 2,528 file pairs provide real samples; no manual vulnerability ground truth, corrupted Pylint hash, and the paper/workbook do not close |
| 116 | ReasonVul | **6.0/10** | Paired confusion matrix can be back-computed; 389 correct of 542 conflicts lack per-example definition, budget comparison, and public artifacts |
| 117 | EntailLLM | **6.0/10** | Has repeated runs, random baseline, and formal rules; only tests self-consistent entailment, with no vulnerability ground truth |

| 257 | VulStyle (`2604.26313`) | **3.1/10** | Pretraining loader reads BigVul train/validation/test simultaneously, confusion matrix misaligned with metric columns, and the 34.04pp ablation jump unexplained |
| 258 | Hierarchy-Aware CWE Training (`2607.21069`) | **3.1/10** | Percentage and Welch arithmetic recompute, but best result does test-set model selection, significance has pseudo-duplicates, all RL single training seed, and no independent validation |
| 259 | 2308.10345 | **3.0/10** | Uses discovery count instead of accuracy, treats "can give repair suggestions" as low-false-positive evidence, and lacks ground truth with functional validation |
| 260 | Balkanization SoK (`2607.05743`) | **3.0/10** | 39 papers, 17 classes, and annual arithmetic close; citation-following has no query, screening flow, exclusion table, or public corpus, and single-author recheck cannot replace independent reviewer |
| 261 | Tool-Guided Repair | **3.0/10** | 5,000-task main table has two models and clear aggregate numbers; clean-rate miscalculated, Qwen missing, KLEE no semantic results, and no ablation/raw outputs/code |
| 262 | NeuroSCA | **3.0/10** | 106/122 and 76.4/82.1 exist in original pages; only single-seed two-row aggregates, bug/coverage undefined, budget not aligned, no statistical tests, and no LLM-vs-heuristic ablation |
| 263 | Okara (`2601.22770`) | **3.0/10** | Dynamic TLS engineering verifiable; 37,349-app run is not FM GUI, and multiple denominators, Novel Ratio, ground truth, and longitudinal statistics cannot hold simultaneously |
| 264 | CoTDeceptor | **2.9/10** | Formulas and the 15-class table verifiable from original pages; 500 samples no manifest, rollout cap conflicts with table, semantic preservation mainly static/LLM, and repository not anonymously accessible |
| 265 | Agent Skills in the Wild | **2.9/10** | 200 validation set reproduces TP52/FN11/FP8/TN129; Wilson CI, Rogan-Gladen, IPW recall, OR, and dynamic-experiment denominators all have errors |
| 266 | Security in a Workflow | **2.8/10** | Table 4 recomputes from the public workbook's 16 cases, but the body repeatedly says 25 cases, the 150/300 protocol conflicts, and verifier results plus batch-run artifacts are missing |
| 267 | ELVul | **2.8/10** | Random function split and small gains are already weak; data/split/raw outputs missing, DGS five experts all read the same result file, and the public implementation cannot support the main conclusion |
| 268 | SkillFortify | **2.8/10** | No benchmark at paper time, and supplemented 247/270 plus current 250/270 both do not equal 254/270; same-source template and theoretical contradiction undermine the main experiment |
| 269 | FPEval | **2.8/10** | 721 tasks and multilingual direction are clear; Table 1/4 do not close, 9/10 can be judged pass, conditional clean rate, regex labels, and post-paper artifact drift |
| 270 | LLMs in Code Vulnerability Analysis | **2.8/10** | Big-Vul/VulRepair scale is readable; split, seed, repetition, and denominator missing, rich-metadata input leakage, and the abstract conclusion contradicted by its own tables |
| 271 | Verify Before You Fix | **2.8/10** | Hybrid mean recomputes; 23.42%, Figure 4/Table 7, and terminal states do not close, random file split, and repair success only looks at detector flags |
| 272 | PCodeTrans (`2603.14855`) | **2.8/10** | BE greater than CS is definitionally impossible, Table 1/2 do not close, uncovered functions counted into near-100% equivalence, and no raw ledger |
| 273 | SmartCoder-R1 (`2509.09942`) | **2.8/10** | FuncRate is not a publicly reviewable executable single-test result, security training and evaluation are same-source, and baseline plus manual calibration ledger is incomplete |
| 274 | PatchSeeker (`2509.07540`) | **2.7/10** | Recall/ME math does not close; no-output query has different denominator handling in Recall/MRR vs. ME, and implicit-set labels plus training config are unreviewable |
| 275 | FusionVul (`2606.08553`) | **2.7/10** | F1 arithmetic and most tables close; random function split, single-run, missing statistics, incomplete weight-selection protocol, and central-formula failure are significantly down-weighted |
| 276 | SecureRAG-RTL | **2.6/10** | 18 models and 14-design main table exist; all positives, same-source GPT-5 data/knowledge construction, no repeated runs, no precision/FPR, and no artifacts limit conclusions |
| 277 | VCAO | **2.5/10** | Missing per-CVE manifest, run configuration, manual review protocol, live findings, and usable artifact links |
| 278 | CryptoScope | **2.5/10** | 92-case domain benchmark is valuable, but mainly measures text similarity, Credibility, and same-source LLM judge; no security negatives, confusion matrix, leakage isolation, or public artifacts |
| 279 | MULTIVUL | **2.5/10** | Percentage-point improvement over same-text fine-tuning; OOD main result below the all-positive baseline, 12,482 subset sampling/split unclear, no repeated runs, CI, code, or raw prediction |
| 280 | Llama Prompt vs Fine-tuning (`2512.09006`) | **2.5/10** | Single split, no CI, BigVul isolation not auditable, PrimeVul still has CVE/project overlap, and Double tuning budget is very unequal |
| 281 | IaC Security Smells | **2.4/10** | Data scale, pair split, ablation retraining, LLM baseline fairness, and P/R/F1 all have hard problems, and anonymous artifacts inaccessible this time |
| 282 | MulVul | **2.4/10** | 130 CWE and multi-component ablation narrative complete; no split, raw outputs, code, cost, or same-model evolution baseline, and the headline cannot be recovered |
| 283 | Code Provenance / OSINT | **2.4/10** | 528, 406, and 101/131 close; sample-level split may leak tasks, Human/LLM corpus asymmetric, and cross-lingual plus body conflicts in several places |
| 284 | RampoNN (`2511.16765`) | **2.4/10** | Surrogate table body recomputes; 1900.82% shrunk 100x, the H=10 claim contradicts Figure 4, and random experiments plus error bounds missing |
| 285 | Cross-Domain Vulnerability Detection (`2509.09313`) | **2.4/10** | Labels are not vulnerability ground truth, data ledger does not close, four F1 recomputations fail, and function-level split plus dedup implementation are high-risk |
| 286 | Solidity Semantic Clones (`2604.26526`) | **2.4/10** | 97% recall stitched from different strata raw counts, Table 3 TP self-contradicts, and pair dependence plus oracle loop not handled |
| 287 | Lightweight Vulnerability Detection (`2605.04260`) | **2.2/10** | identifier rename should not change five metrics, yet reports .638→.550/.434→.461; single seed, two projects, and no code or raw CSV |
| 288 | Multilingual Vulnerability Repair (`2508.03470`) | **2.2/10** | Percentage, McNemar, and table arithmetic recompute, but 82% same CVE/URL leakage, no dynamic ground truth, closed-source instruction-tuning unreviewable, and main-conclusion credibility very low |
| 289 | Rust Multi-LLM + KLEE | **2.1/10** | Prompt directly asks for a vulnerable wrapper, no benign/FPR; 1,082/1,206 are external/panic, and top-10 counts, Listing, CVE/CWE mapping, and artifacts all conflict |
| 290 | Solidity Zero-Shot Benchmark (`2603.13239`) | **2.1/10** | Only 3 of the 48 result rows over the 400 balanced set have an integer confusion-matrix solution; F1, denominator, multi-label, and failure outputs all do not close |
| 291 | Constitutional SDD | **2.0/10** | n=1, same developer same model, no baseline artifacts; public code directly violates several constitution clauses and lacks actual tests |
| 292 | NFT Vulnerability Detection | **2.0/10** | 450-case count closes and the authors admit no ground truth; no data ID, independent label, model config, precision/recall, or public artifacts |
| 293 | Solidity Execution Validation (`2509.13023`) | **2.0/10** | Paper explicitly an incomplete feasibility study; 7 classes only manual experiments, 3 classes shown, no complete count, baseline, repetition, cost, or artifacts |
| 294 | TaintSentinel (`2510.18192`) | **2.0/10** | Data totals, confusion matrix, ROC/AUC, PRA cyclic labels, and different-denominator baselines all distorted simultaneously, with no ablation/repeated runs |
| 295 | MulVuln (`2510.04397`) | **2.0/10** | Near-97% CVE/commit cross-split overlap plus many function clones; only single results, no statistical uncertainty |
| 296 | PYPILINE (`2606.19063`) | **2.0/10** | Knowledge base/test boundary unclear, public subset cannot recompute headline, failures and non-boolean outputs not accounted, and no statistical uncertainty |
| 297 | VulnGraph | **1.8/10** | Public artifacts recover the main confusion matrix; 87% embedding missing and label-selected, explicit label-word leakage, and insufficient split/provenance invalidate the headline |
| 298 | VISION (`2508.18933`) | **1.8/10** | Public repository provides some CSV; 60% train/10% test vs. the paper's 80/10/10 cannot form one unique protocol, and multiple Accuracy and pair metrics rows do not close |
| 299 | ParaVul (`2510.17919`) | **1.8/10** | Headline F1 self-contradicts, no sample ledger, split, raw predictions, confidence interval, or executable artifact, and exact-clone leakage risk is very high |
| 300 | Android Obfuscation Detection (`2606.14233`) | **1.8/10** | Dataset scale conflicts, public CSV cannot recompute the main table, and RQ4 metrics are incompatible with the 44/44 sampling constraint |
| 301 | Argus | **1.7/10** | Only reports sink/vulnerability output counts, no ground truth or validation; DataGear/Yudao self-contradict in Table 2/4, two Sonnet versions have identical per-item results, and no artifacts |
| 302 | VIC-RAGENT | **1.7/10** | 241 paired commits and ablation table are checkable, but code reads test labels to maintain RAG memory containing only true positives, directly polluting downstream test retrieval; no raw predictions or repeated runs |
| 303 | RAG/SFT/Dual-Agent | **1.7/10** | Inputs contain commit/CVE/CWE/post-modification code; SFT F1 wrong, five-category scores and balance claim do not close, and three paired t-tests all unreproducible |
| 304 | Adaptive Survey (`2604.27000`) | **1.6/10** | `213-179=34` not 39, plus 16 should be 50 not 55; same-author repeated extraction cannot be an independent reviewer κ, and no public corpus |
| 305 | Tailored Prompts (`2605.03697`) | **1.6/10** | Table II can be revisited as 0.92/0.87; directly tunes the prompt on evaluation misclassifications, no independent holdout, project split, key baseline, or obtainable artifacts |
| 306 | SAST-Genius | **1.5/10** | 100% recall is logically incompatible with Semgrep-only triage architecture; the repository has only a 25-project list, prompts, and 10 synthetic samples, missing all main-experiment artifacts |
| 307 | Dissecting the Black Box (`2605.29901`) | **1.5/10** | Artifact uses fine-tuned Gemma rather than the body's base Gemma; the 100% baseline is the post-hoc correct subset, 6% is only TP retention rate, and L0 with length r=.9851 |
| 308 | Benign/Malicious Packages | **1.5/10** | Notebook produces the paper's tables, but actually 4,043 rows not about 2,169, only 251 vectors, strong label/feature coupling, and incorrect model/preprocessing descriptions |
| 309 | Embedded Firmware | **1.5/10** | Main-table metrics readable from PDF original pages; no sample count, numerator/denominator, repeated statistics, or raw output, and public scripts cannot generate the headline |
| 310 | Cross-Language CodeBERT | **1.4/10** | 1,990 test denominator unexplained by 93,687 samples, confusion matrix only supports accuracy, precision/recall/F1/FPR all conflict with body, and no baseline artifacts |
| 311 | LiquiLM | **1.4/10** | 1,490/1,380 etc. visible in original pages; split unclear, AI completion, mixed ablation rounds, different baseline denominators, CVE without list, and zero artifacts |
| 312 | Attack-to-Vulnerability Mapping (`2509.02077`) | **1.4/10** | Metric formula does not close, TP implementation differs from paper, target-term leakage, split leakage, and example ground truth wrong |
| 313 | MARD (`2604.25264`) | **1.4/10** | The main-table 7,825 narrative only publishes a minimal integer ledger equivalent to 124 items, cross-domain equivalent to about 158, and the AndroZoo table arithmetic/denominator is inconsistent |
| 314 | CFCEval (`2512.06248`) | **1.3/10** | Sample count, ELRM weights, paired/Welch, score>0 filtering, and 10-point correlation all have protocol cracks, and raw human ratings plus security-execution oracle missing |
| 315 | AI Code in the Wild | **1.3/10** | Detector benchmark has clear denominator; human/AI completely confounded with era, task, repository, real data has no author ground truth, and code/predictions not public |
| 316 | AdaTaint | **1.2/10** | 2018 placeholder template, project count and ablation percentage conflict, missing split, confusion matrix, per-project results, user protocol, cost, and all artifacts |
| 317 | Securing the Dark Matter | **1.0/10** | `n=5` cannot yield the claimed Wilcoxon `p<0.01`; public data missing, and PoC runner can reproduce the headline with mock scores |
| 318 | AutoVulnPHP | **1.0/10** | PVts/PHPVD, 83.5/89.4/99.7 three sets not mapped; AST ablation back-computes different prevalence, and localization weights plus 351 CVE lack per-item evidence |
| 319 | Rust RL + simulated fuzzing | **1.0/10** | Table 1 exactly from the full 4,789 entries with positive class false_positive=1; no 732 test, no split, and the paper method differs fundamentally from the public implementation |
| 320 | Hybrid Fuzzing | **0.9/10** | Ten repetitions directly conflict with Table VII single integer, raw runs missing; the claimed CVE has 0 entries in NVD and 404 in MITRE, and no crash/patch artifacts |
| 321 | SecureFixAgent | **0.8/10** | Public pipeline does not implement the core loop, only 740 synthetic data of 2,168 samples public, tests have clear duplicates, and only validate syntax |
| 322 | SDLLMFuzz (`2604.17750`) | **0.8/10** | Method/baseline/target/budget inconsistent with plots, no raw data, run count, statistical tests, model config, or recomputable tables |
| 323 | HYDRA (`2511.06220`) | **0.8/10** | No vulnerability ground truth, no executable validation, no independent repetition, clustering metrics cannot prove security semantics, and key headline from post-hoc construction |
| 324 | Multi-LLM Energy Reasoning | **0.7/10** | Synthetic descriptors and same-source proxy form a cyclic validation; η² actually about .156 not .31, ω² about .144 not .28, no real vulnerability ground truth or artifacts |

| 326 | Agentic Supply Chain Defense (`2512.23480`) | **0.5/10** | No experimental unit, sample count, raw data, statistical tests, or traceable run records, and in-figure numbers cannot be independently verified |

| 328 | CogniCrypt | **0.2/10** | AI-Gen-Malware written as 2,500 positives, 0 negatives, yet reports AUC, FP/precision, and accuracy≠recall; public repository lacks the paper implementation, data, models, or results |

#### 2. Auditing Agent Method Insight

| Rank | Paper | Project insight | Module most worth adopting |
|---:|---|---:|---|
| 1 | KNOWDIT | **9.9/10** | DeFi semantic knowledge graph, project-specific specification, PoC execution, reflection, and long-term knowledge evolution closed loop |
| 2 | Code-Augur | **9.9/10** | specification-first, explicit security assumptions, in-source assertion, guided fuzzing, and the reason-falsify-refine closed loop |
| 3 | EvoPoC | **9.9/10** | three-layer HKG of Contract Semantics, Failure Mode, and Exploit Primitive, multi-hop retrieval, evolving memory, and reachability/profitability dual validation |
| 4 | Sailor | **9.9/10** | extracting targets from CodeQL specifications, statically locating candidates, LLM reachability estimation, and KLEE/ASan hard validation forming a static--symbolic-execution evidence funnel |
| 5 | Antiproof | **9.8/10** | high-recall detector, attack surface, eCPG, PoV/PoE oracle, and disclosure funnel |
| 6 | CyberGym-E2E | **9.8/10** | utilizing S1-S4 to layer localization, repair, functional preservation, and hidden target PoC, directly defining the end-to-end Agent's task contract and main bottlenecks |
| 7 | QuartetFuzz | **9.8/10** | P1-P4 harness contract, generate-check-fix, harness-blame exclusion, repeated fuzzing, and maintainer feedback |
| 8 | Chai | **9.8/10** | deterministic differencing -> guarantee explanation -> dependency graph -> downstream PoC, placing the LLM after hard signals rather than acting as a vulnerability oracle |
| 9 | Vul4Py | **9.8/10** | exploit and functional paired oracle, human semantic audit, and cost stratification, directly constraining the success definition of the repair Agent |
| 10 | ARVO | **9.8/10** | rebuildable vulnerable/fixed images, PoC replay, patch locating, multi-PoC regression, and environment-failure stratification |
| 11 | REVELIO | **9.8/10** | cheap-breadth hypothesis, strong PoV construction, programmatic phase isolation, sanitizer replay, and security-impact triage forming a complete hard-evidence funnel |
| 12 | SemTaint | **9.8/10** | package-specific source/sink, LLM call-edge repair, third-party flow summary, and CodeQL path computation responsibility split, directly filling project-level semantic gaps |
| 13 | RECEIPT | **9.7/10** | clean snapshot, attacker/victim roles, PoC capability, random sentinel tokens, and verifier binding as a deterministic acceptance gate |
| 14 | ALIBI | **9.8/10** | establishing a natural-language low-trust zone: comments, PRs, issues, tool output, and memory can only generate hypotheses, which must be independently adjudicated by code-only and dynamic evidence |
| 15 | KLEECopilot | **9.5/10** | LLM proposals, directed weights, stagnation feedback, constraint rewriting, and sanitizer/developer feedback turning symbolic execution into a learnable search control loop |
| 16 | FuzzingBrain V2 | **9.7/10** | Suspicious Point, Direction, global/SP fuzzer, sanitizer verifier, and submittable PoV report chain |
| 17 | SLYP | **9.7/10** | binary entry enumeration, decompilation/cross-references, dynamic debugging, PoC adapter, and vendor-confirmed vulnerability state machine |
| 18 | Veritas | **9.7/10** | first fixing witness-backed flow with deterministic slicing, then letting the LLM construct claims, and finally adjudicating with runtime evidence, avoiding direct conviction from free text |
| 19 | KASS | **9.7/10** | typed attack plan, PoC Generator, inner code repair, outer strategy replan, Foundry path/postcondition, and target integrity contract |
| 20 | RustMizan | **9.7/10** | compile-first benchmark, file/function context ladder, vulnerable/safe mutation, and contamination diagnosis |
| 21 | Mastermind | **9.7/10** | strategy Planner, task-local Curator, failure attribution, in-budget strategy selection, and dual-build M7 verifier |
| 22 | PATCHEVAL | **9.7/10** | real CVE dynamic dual oracle, location/end-to-end/feedback separate leaderboards, oracle versioning, and reference-patch hiding |
| 23 | AgenticRepair | **9.7/10** | structure/runtime/history three-path context producer, persistent memory, dedicated repairer, and patch failure taxonomy |
| 24 | AgentFlow | **9.7/10** | typed harness IR, local rewrite, structural verification, diagnoser, and execution feedback providing a complete blueprint for automatically optimizing multi-Agent systems |
| 25 | PHOENIX / SBIR | **9.7/10** | converting historical PR/CWE into cross Python/C++/backend semantic contracts with repository identifiers, then checking whether properties, state, and guards are preserved across layers |
| 26 | KeaRepair (`2607.00820`) | **9.7/10** | dual-view vulnerability knowledge, `trace_data_flow`, reachability/API tools, ReAct diagnosis, and PoC/test repair closed loop directly convertible into an Agent |
| 27 | DIG | **9.7/10** | oracle synthesis, constraint inference, generator evolution, directed mutation, and execution feedback forming the most complete hard-to-trigger-input search module |
| 28 | PAGENT | **9.7/10** | combination of vulnerability rules, program-analysis evidence, coverage/profiler typed feedback, repeated PoC refinement, and a root-cause verifier |
| 29 | VulAgentRL | **9.7/10** | using CPG to simultaneously provide cross-procedure investigation tools and a node-ID evidence verifier; a deployed version should add multi-path ground truth, negative proof, and same-permission baseline |
| 30 | ContraFix | **9.6/10** | same-crash/oracle-passing control execution, RepairSpec, Mutation/Correction dual-track experience, and semantic repair audit |
| 31 | Kumushi | **9.6/10** | diversified crash variants, dynamic/static evidence families, FoI weighting, and root-cause review, upgrading "passing the oracle" to "truly fixing the root cause" |
| 32 | LeanGuard | **9.6/10** | the LLM only proposes/filters facts; a deterministic translator, scope-aware pairing, and the Lean kernel can discharge obligations; explicitly preserving context-missing |
| 33 | EvoRepair | **9.6/10** | extracting success/failure trajectories into versioned experience, quality scores, and retrieval assets; should add cold start, frozen memory, and task-order protocol |
| 34 | CVE-GENIE | **9.6/10** | Knowledge Builder, environment construction, exploit, CTF-style verifier, critics, feedback iteration, and per-task evidence preservation |
| 35 | QLCoder | **9.6/10** | CVE-to-detector, LSP, document retrieval, vulnerable/fixed execution feedback, and rule regression |
| 36 | VulnAgent-R2 | **9.6/10** | graph retrieval, role division, counterexample Agent, CER, dynamic verification plan, confidence calibration, and budget scheduling |
| 37 | FIRMHIVE | **9.6/10** | turning delegation into local Agent capabilities, directory/file/function scope, runtime-grown ToA, PKH provenance, and independent verification |
| 38 | NeuroLog | **9.6/10** | restricting the LLM to typed facts/input synthesis, and handing cross-procedure composition, satisfiability, and final truth to Datalog, SMT, and ASan |
| 39 | VulAgent | **9.6/10** | TriggerPlanner, AssumptionPruner, and FinalValidator splitting trigger conditions, counterexamples, and guard verification into an inspectable evidence state machine |
| 40 | SmartPoC | **9.6/10** | BCE, generate--repair--execute loop, and Action-State Differential Verification, upgrading "runs" to state-difference evidence |
| 41 | DrillAgent | **9.6/10** | RCA, instrumentation self-check, coverage distance, path exploration, and crash-triggering in stages, very suitable as the PoV layer after candidates |
| 42 | VulnLLM-R | **9.6/10** | using a dedicated security reasoning model as the repo Agent base, combined with function/project training, cross-language OOD, and lightweight LoRA updates |
| 43 | ShadowProbe | **9.6/10** | static shadow-complexity, project dependency recovery, controllable worst-case input, multi-scale dynamic timing, and robust growth inference |
| 44 | LLMVD.js | **9.6/10** | Finder high recall, constrained Judge, PoC execution, and human security boundaries forming a strong funnel for dynamic-language package audit |
| 45 | CHAINTRIX | **9.6/10** | typed protocol IR, multi-pipeline discovery, claim-first counterexample, coverage gap, attention residual, and PoC/formal layering |
| 46 | PBFuzz | **9.6/10** | PIER constraint loop, persistent memory, program analysis, typed generation, and property-based search, clearly dividing reasoning and high-throughput testing |
| 47 | Security Calibration | **9.5/10** | False Trust, selective refusal, coverage-risk, execution gating, and decomposed security confidence |
| 48 | CyberChainBench | **9.5/10** | historical mainnet state, profit oracle, attack replay, normal-transaction regression, and reward-hacking protection |
| 49 | VulnResolver | **9.5/10** | CPCAgent directed repository exploration, Safety Property generation/instrumentation/execution, multi-candidate dynamic selection, and functional gate |
| 50 | FLAMES | **9.5/10** | generating executable invariants then using compile, semantic, functional, and exploit oracles to eliminate candidates in tiers, suitable as a guard synthesis/validation layer |
| 51 | Diffploit | **9.5/10** | failure symptoms, causing/supporting diff, iterative modification, and annealing search forming a complete method transferable to multi-version validation |
| 52 | Co-RedTeam | **9.5/10** | discovery/exploitation staged, code browser, execution, validation, and layered long-term memory |
| 53 | FlowArk | **9.5/10** | evidence-backed, bounded, actionable, code-anchored knowledge entries, runtime matching/injection, and version-invalidation governance |
| 54 | Dual-Mode Benchmarks | **9.5/10** | P3 vulnerability-family methodology, P4 multi-path ARG, LLM reconnaissance/payload, and programmatic evidence gate separation |
| 55 | SmartFuzz (`2511.12164`) | **9.5/10** | local parameter reflection, global transaction sequence rearrangement, role collaboration, and real EVM feedback very suitable for stateful vulnerability fuzzing Agents |
| 56 | PCodeTrans (`2603.14855`) | **9.5/10** | minimal compile context, bidirectional relocation, in-place replacement, ASan, and BP-Diff composable into an executable pseudo-source layer for binary audit Agents |
| 57 | MOSAIC-Bench (`2605.03952`) | **9.5/10** | "stepwise induction + final-state verification + multi-view reviewer" is a high-value experimental paradigm for repository-level security Agents |
| 58 | 2601.19239 | **9.4/10** | project-specific source/sink/guard, cross-procedure evidence, false-positive governance, and cost/human-burden metrics |
| 59 | Staczzer | **9.4/10** | LLM-predicted call stack as DGF guidance, routing single-round/code Agents by slice size, and regression patch testing to find incomplete fixes |
| 60 | PVBench | **9.4/10** | PoC+ incorporating root cause, specification, and developer intent into the repair oracle, directly correcting the common error that passing the test suite means success |
| 61 | GONDAR | **9.4/10** | sink candidates, path exploration, exploit generation, fuzzer feedback, and dynamic sanitizer closed loop |
| 62 | SeedSmith | **9.4/10** | harness/sink-driven code exploration, reusable analysis reports, batch seed synthesis, sanitizer feedback, and fuzzing execution closed loop |
| 63 | Web Vulnerability Reproduction | **9.4/10** | staged evaluation of environment, authentication, localization, PoC execution, real trigger, and Success@k |
| 64 | V2E | **9.4/10** | PoC generation, execution feedback, trigger/impact oracle, and inconclusive three-state output |
| 65 | VulWeaver | **9.4/10** | UDG semantic repair, overall context, explicit/implicit dependencies, and CWE expert reasoning |
| 66 | DREA | **9.4/10** | strong Planner, local Explorer, falsifiable evidence gaps, Lucky Hit, and reasoning-correctness evaluation |
| 67 | Words Speak Louder Than Code | **9.4/10** | turning framing, anchoring, halo, direction reversal, and compositional attacks into an auditor-metadata red-team test set |
| 68 | SWExploit | **9.4/10** | modeling issue/traceback/reproduce code as untrusted supply-chain input, directly completing the repair Agent's threat model |
| 69 | AEGIS | **9.4/10** | CPG evidence trace, Red/Blue support and counterexample, independent Audit, and top-k/cost joint scheduling; absence-of-protection must redo negative proof |
| 70 | EvoHunt | **9.4/10** | turning the audit process into a versionable playbook, evolving from verified failures, and transferring strong-model procedures to weaker execution models |
| 71 | PMDetector | **9.4/10** | business-logic audit chain of cross-procedure taint candidates, defense evaluation, attack simulation, and deterministic semantic checker |
| 72 | SecureForge | **9.4/10** | continuously red-teaming the failure distribution of the current system prompt, then optimizing audit/generation strategy with an independent held-out set, security oracle, and versioned regression gate |
| 73 | PatchIsland (`2601.17471`) | **9.4/10** | heterogeneous repairers, shared build environment, best-of-N, failure-aware scheduling, and a layered state machine from plausible to official correctness |
| 74 | Antares | **9.4/10** | explore-first repository navigation, hierarchical file filtering, localization-specialized small models, and a hard-example benchmark directly filling the candidate-localization layer |
| 75 | SCAFFOLD-CEGIS | **9.4/10** | semantic anchor, scope/compile/functional/security four-layer gate, counterexample, and failure assimilation defining a secure-edit state machine |
