| 240 | VISION (`2508.18933`) | **8.0/10** | vulnerable-safe counterfactual paired generation testing whether the model relies on spurious correlation; must do compilability, testing, semantic equivalence, and project-level isolation |
| 241 | Solidity Execution Validation (`2509.13023`) | **8.0/10** | vulnerability-family preconditions, context extraction, template slots, and Forge/Kontrol result interpretation forming a finding-validator sketch |
| 242 | Adversarial Comments | **7.9/10** | comment trust tagging, static/adaptive attack comparison, and temporal/compositional hard sets |
| 243 | AI-Generated Python Refactoring PRs | **7.9/10** | incorporating AI refactoring into commit/diff security governance: forcing build, test, lint/SAST diffs, finding attribution, and human re-review before merge |
| 244 | SSR (`2601.05827`) | **7.9/10** | mapping staking users, shares, time, checkpoints, claims, and asset bookkeeping into six types of executable business properties |
| 245 | VulnScout-C | **7.8/10** | lightweight dedicated candidate ranker, multi-stage training curriculum, and scarce-CWE data completion; must redo the blind verifier and real low-base-rate evaluation |
| 246 | VCAO | **7.8/10** | belief-based dynamic scheduling of token, static-analysis, fuzzing, GPU, and human budgets |
| 247 | SLEAN | **7.8/10** | isolated independent judgment, symmetric cross-review, final arbitration, and documented provenance; must replace self-reported attribution with an execution oracle |
| 248 | VulGNN | **7.8/10** | lightweight CPG/GNN as candidate prior, outputting interpretable subgraphs and aiming at candidate reduction under high recall; should not serve as the final verdict |
| 249 | CLAWAUDIT | **7.8/10** | Agent-runtime-specific source/sink/guard taxonomy, Semgrep/CodeQL dual backend, and post-broad-rule semantic filtering |
| 250 | FinDet (`2509.18934`) | **7.8/10** | bytecode lifting, prepare/exploit/exit fund flows, and multi-prompt uncertainty as an attack-contract candidate explanation and ranking module |
| 251 | The Language of Security | **7.8/10** | prompt-transformation experiment factory, cross-language/model generation, feature combination, and risk ratios; must add a semantic-equivalence gate |
| 252 | TPL-Benchmark | **7.8/10** | dependency/SBOM lane, version evidence, R8 robustness, timeout full denominator, and reachability/exploitability gate after CVE mapping |
| 253 | Bastet | **7.8/10** | DeFi root-cause Tag + implementation Subtag, multi-label findings, expert summaries, and project/protocol/time-isolated data knowledge layer |
| 254 | CWEEP | **7.8/10** | layering asset correctness, pattern correctness, overall verdict, exact location, and auto-fix; repair must not inherit the detector verdict |
| 255 | Okara (`2601.22770`) | **7.8/10** | dynamic MitM, TLS flow, ART TI call localization, and fine-grained attribution transferable to a mobile audit Agent's evidence-collection layer |
| 256 | Solidity Zero-Shot Benchmark (`2603.13239`) | **7.8/10** | reminding the system to layer high-recall screening and high-precision triage, and to treat prompt as a model-specific hyperparameter rather than a universal trick |
| 257 | CAPEC/CWE Code Generation (`2604.02548`) | **7.8/10** | the structured knowledge chain of CAPEC-CWE-code and multi-model consistency audit worth doing; should add counterexample, compiler, static analyzer, and expert adjudication |
| 258 | PYPILINE (`2606.19063`) | **7.8/10** | suitable for borrowing candidate-API filtering, evidence retrieval, and automatic-report closed loop; should not copy the experimental protocol of directly inducing from known malicious packages then back-testing |
| 259 | Solidity Semantic Clones (`2604.26526`) | **7.8/10** | "language-intent recall + code-difference filtering + human/dynamic validation" is a good retrieval framework; natural-language similarity cannot directly serve as execution equivalence |
| 260 | HardSecBench | **7.7/10** | benchmark factory of specifications, functional/security oracles, mutation, and per-task manifest; needs dynamic coverage and security-specific mutants |
| 261 | AgenticVM (`2605.01739`) | **7.7/10** | vulnerability-management module boundaries of deterministic parsing, authoritative-data completion, risk prioritization, recommendation generation, and human approval |
| 262 | LiquiLM | **7.7/10** | liquidity intent, DCN candidates, risk thresholds, and multi-round verifier for uncertain items; implementation must do same-budget ablation and an independent execution oracle |
| 263 | Tool-Guided Repair | **7.7/10** | layered repair loop of compiler, CodeQL, KLEE, functional tests, and verified-repair memory; must add same-budget component ablation and counterexample semantic verification |
| 264 | DeFiScreener | **7.7/10** | the layered "historical pattern library first recalls, call graph then verifies" transferable; must do time/family isolation and open output budget |
| 265 | RampoNN (`2511.16765`) | **7.7/10** | statically pruning with path intervals and neural reachability, then directing the falsifier budget to remaining candidates; a valuable but unverified hybrid route |
| 266 | Android Obfuscation Detection (`2606.14233`) | **7.7/10** | "first narrow the code surface, then let the LLM do local judgment, finally aggregate" very suitable for large-artifact audit; statistical sampling and failure semantics must be redone |
| 267 | MultiVer | **7.6/10** | suitable for borrowing multi-view analysis and self-consistency, more suitable as a negative example for any-agent-positive union and low-base-rate FPR |
| 268 | Retrieval-Augmented Few-Shot | **7.6/10** | retrieving verified vulnerability mechanisms, source/sink/guard, counterexamples, and PoCs; cannot just transfer labels by function similarity |
| 269 | Esim | **7.6/10** | stable instructions, CFG/DFG heterogeneous graph, and similarity retrieval as a bytecode candidate layer, followed by invariant/trace/PoC validation |
| 270 | GiAnt Corpus | **7.6/10** | a corpus factory recovering findings, code, severity, fixes, and provenance from real audit reports, enterable into the knowledge layer |
| 271 | Agent Skills in the Wild | **7.6/10** | skill parsing, static rules, LLM second review, and dynamic sandbox convertible into an Agent skill/plugin supply-chain scanner |
| 272 | SDLLMFuzz (`2604.17750`) | **7.6/10** | the closed loop of dynamic fuzzing, static crash triage, and LLM seed refinement worth independently redoing |
| 273 | SkillFortify | **7.5/10** | declared/inferred capability diff, skill lockfile, ASBOM, dependency graph, and registry/content risk stratification |
| 274 | Verify Before You Fix | **7.5/10** | alerts producing validation evidence before fixing is a correct gate; needs extension to post-fix execution-based dual validation |
| 275 | SecureRAG-RTL | **7.4/10** | summary/keyword hybrid retrieval, top-k CWE candidates, and open-world fallback; final findings must bind signal/property/witness |
| 276 | Multi-LLMSecCodeEval | **7.3/10** | multi-model candidate expansion, static-tool selection, and equal-budget cost-security frontier; do not rely on pure LLM discussion |
| 277 | Dissecting the Black Box (`2605.29901`) | **7.3/10** | circuit, causal patching, matched control, and length normalization used to find vulnerability-model shortcuts, provided the stability protocol is redone |
| 278 | LLMs Versus SAST | **7.3/10** | hybrid orchestration of LLM high-recall triage, SAST deterministic localization, and a unified finding schema |
| 279 | LLM Contract Static Analysis Benchmark (`2605.11163`) | **7.3/10** | lexical counterfactual, paired experiments, and hybrid-input judge isolation usable for model and evaluator governance |
| 280 | ParaVul (`2510.17919`) | **7.3/10** | "lexical retrieval + semantic retrieval + LLM + verifier" worth borrowing, but SLoRA and meta-learning should not be implemented per the paper's original form |
| 281 | ReVul-CoT | **7.2/10** | historical case retrieval and severity triage after a vulnerability is confirmed; must remove label leakage and same-CVE neighbors |
| 282 | LISA | **7.2/10** | historical audit knowledge base, specialized/general agent scheduling, staged scanning, and evidence merging; must add freezing and a verifier |
| 283 | Broken by Default | **7.2/10** | adding a dedicated SMT counterexample lane for allocation size, integer boundary, and length invariants, then connecting reachability and runtime verification |
| 284 | CodeScan | **7.2/10** | building a code-model vulnerability probe suite, clean baseline, cross-judge and adaptive attacker, as a model supply-chain health check layer |
| 285 | Reentrancy Detection | **7.2/10** | realistic contract + minimal semantic stress-test dual leaderboards, version compatibility, and explicit missing/error governance |
| 286 | DVDR-LLM | **7.2/10** | multi-model disagreement, detection/repair threshold separation, and consensus calibration worth borrowing; final adjudication should upgrade to evidence validation rather than simple voting |
| 287 | MULTIVUL | **7.1/10** | code, structure, and generation descriptions as multi-view candidate features; only after adding zero-cost baseline, project isolation, and independent program evidence is routing worthwhile |
| 288 | CogniCrypt | **7.0/10** | letting the LLM only rank concolic paths, leaving SAT, traces, and final rule validation to deterministic systems; the current paper does not implement this |
| 289 | Human-Written vs. AI-Generated Code | **7.0/10** | using large-scale paired telemetry to build an AI-code-specific quality profile, calibrating findings by LOC, functional pass rate, unparseable rate, and source |
| 290 | VulStyle (`2604.26313`) | **7.0/10** | AST/style multi-channel representation suitable as a low-confidence prior, but must distinguish project/author shortcuts from real source-sink/guard semantics |
| 291 | Attack-to-Vulnerability Mapping (`2509.02077`) | **7.0/10** | "MITRE relation graph + dense retrieval + human link completion" suitable as the Agent's external knowledge layer |
| 292 | Vul-R2 | **6.8/10** | reasoning-data and RL repair training framework; reward must switch to build/test/exploit oracle |
| 293 | Security in a Workflow | **6.8/10** | basic responsibility boundaries of Planner/Analyzer/Fixer/Verifier, and the lesson that explicit arbitration is needed when CodeQL and LLM conflict |
| 294 | SAST-Genius | **6.8/10** | keep only the product funnel of SAST candidates -> LLM triage -> PoC -> human confirmation, and count independent discovery separately |
| 295 | QuiLL | **6.8/10** | separating verdict, localization, evidence, abstain, and malformed, with RAG recording retrieval sources and isolation per item |
| 296 | SPLVD | **6.8/10** | scheduling training by label confidence and sample difficulty, suitable for weak-label governance; must use project/CVE/clone isolation and an independent human set to validate the selector |
| 297 | BugSweeper | **6.8/10** | FLAG and two-stage pooling as function-level candidate rankers, but must retain cross-function context and use an independent human/execution oracle |
| 298 | Rust Multi-LLM + KLEE | **6.8/10** | auto-synthesizing harnesses for uncompilable snippets and connecting symbolic execution, compile feedback, and structured graph index has insight, but needs independent semantic equivalence and original CVE witness gate |
| 299 | Code Fingerprints / DCAN (`2603.04212`) | **6.8/10** | task-group split, plain/comment comparison, and cross-language analysis worth borrowing; anti-collapse and open-set design must be redone |
| 300 | ELVul | **6.7/10** | routing models/tools by language/CWE/evidence type and verifier historical capability; the current DGS serves only as a negative-implementation reference |
| 301 | IPv6 Periphery / HLEV | **6.6/10** | layered confirmation protocol of reachable→application signature→model endpoint, transferable to exposure surface and runtime validation |
| 302 | Refusal Evaluation Review (`2605.20351`) | **6.6/10** | unified extraction schema, refusal-error meta-analysis, taxonomy, and gap checklist worth adopting |
| 303 | Beyond Single Bugs | **6.5/10** | adding 1/3/5/9 vulnerability density, count MAE, exact-set, instance, and location joint evaluation on executable paired variants |
| 304 | MARGIN (`2605.10240`) | **6.5/10** | margin-aware geometry as a long-tail candidate ranker; still needs project isolation, path evidence, and execution validation |
| 305 | VulnGraph | **6.5/10** | CFG/GAT and local-small-model semantic fusion as a candidate ranker, but must first eliminate label-word, pair, and missingness shortcuts |
| 306 | HYDRA (`2511.06220`) | **6.5/10** | "rule evidence + code representation + anomaly candidates + human review" is a reasonable framework, but needs redesign rather than patching the current pseudocode |
| 307 | FusionVul (`2606.08553`) | **6.1/10** | multimodal contrast and dataset feature stratification worth keeping; a truly usable method should interact over token/node sets and let weights vary with evidence quality |
| 308 | Taught by the Flawed | **6.0/10** | analyzer-aware training-data governance, clean/dirty evidence preservation, and filter/reweight/repair/contrastive-pair comparison; the current experiment provides only pilot motivation |
| 309 | SecureFixAgent | **5.8/10** | keep only finding structuring, post-patch rescan, and limited-retry concepts; must add build/test/PoC/differential oracle and a real state machine |
| 310 | Benign/Malicious Packages | **5.8/10** | dynamic package behavior as a supply-chain candidate-ranking signal; should change to temporal/graph witness, time/package-family split, and independent malicious ground truth |
| 311 | FPEval | **5.8/10** | layered generation validation of compile, all tests, formatter/lint, and language-specific quality checks transferable to a security-code Agent |
| 312 | IaC Security Smells | **5.7/10** | IaC code+text/long-context dual representation and context-ablation topic; the existing protocol serves only as a negative example, needs repo/pair isolation and retraining |
| 313 | Multi-LLM Energy Reasoning | **5.5/10** | model disagreement, uncertainty, latency, and cost as human-triage signals, but not as vulnerability evidence or zero-day ground truth |
| 314 | Lightweight Vulnerability Detection (`2605.04260`) | **5.5/10** | metrics/token cheap prior and Recall@human-budget suitable as an Agent front-end baseline, not for independently adjudicating vulnerabilities |
| 315 | NFT Vulnerability Detection | **5.5/10** | vulnerability-focused slicing, candidate window widening, and domain-knowledge prompting; regex can only be a prior, followed by global context and ground-truth validation |
| 316 | Embedded Firmware | **5.5/10** | the direction of layered validation with security oracle and real-time oracle dual tracks, QEMU to hardware-in-the-loop, is sound; the existing implementation is insufficient |
| 317 | AutoVulnPHP | **5.3/10** | the responsibility split of high-recall candidates, risk-prior review, and template/constraint/compile-test validation as an architecture sketch |
| 318 | Code Provenance / OSINT | **5.1/10** | source and language signals for risk routing, validation strength, and forensic corroboration, not for directly deciding security or author responsibility |
| 319 | Context-Based Adversarial Attacks | **5.0/10** | direct/semantic/example-based adversarial contexts, position, and cross-model transfer evaluation framework; all statistics and data must be redone from scratch |
| 320 | Rust RL + simulated fuzzing | **5.0/10** | the abstraction that static confidence decides whether to invoke dynamic validation has insight, but must rewrite labels, real fuzzing, and project-isolated evaluation |
| 321 | CFCEval (`2512.06248`) | **4.5/10** | keep only benchmark family/transform and human-calibration awareness; ELRM, GPT tagger, and Exact Match should not enter final security adjudication |
| 322 | 2308.10345 | **4.4/10** | keep only the "detect then repair then rescan" closed-loop idea; cannot follow its experimental argumentation |
| 323 | Who Said CVE? | **4.3/10** | actor provenance, PR position, and security identifier can enter the alert responsibility chain, but should not replace vulnerability ground truth and false-positive evaluation |
| 324 | LLMs in Code Vulnerability Analysis | **4.3/10** | multi-task pipeline and metric divergence as early exploration; unknown-code audit must remove CWE/CVE/commit/bug-span answer clues |
| 325 | AI Code in the Wild | **4.0/10** | keep only the research questions of provenance risk, near-duplicate propagation, and human/AI edit-chain, not the existing binary author attributor |
| 326 | RAG/SFT/Dual-Agent | **3.8/10** | Detector + sceptic/validator as a local pattern; must remove answer metadata and count validator corrects/breaks |
| 327 | Traditional CNN Vulnerability Detection | **3.0/10** | only suitable as a cheap historical baseline; modern systems should use project/family split, pretrained encoders, and independent execution evidence |
| 328 | Cross-Language CodeBERT | **3.0/10** | keep only the concept of a cross-language classifier plus an independent verifier; the paper has no experiments proving its multi-model or GPT-4 validation loop |
| 329 | Tailored Prompts (`2605.03697`) | **2.5/10** | vulnerability-type-specific context slicing usable only as a candidate-review prompt; tuning the prompt on the evaluation set cannot enter the method template |

#### 3. Engineering Implementation Reference

| Rank | Paper | Engineering value | Judgment |
|---:|---|---:|---|
| 1 | EthiBench | **9.7/10** | 1,089 tracked files, 108 GT entries, fixed targets, raw findings, per-run matching/result, cumulative analysis, cost, charts, and comparison fully open, most suitable for directly connecting to an evaluation platform |
| 2 | SecCodeBench-V2 | **9.7/10** | 98 cases, five-language project scaffolds, Docker verifier, tests, unified runner, per-round generated code, and raw test logs forming a complete platform |
| 3 | ARVO | **9.6/10** | real vulnerability metadata, historical reconstruction, PoCs, vulnerable/fixed images, patch diffs, and manual logs forming one of the largest dynamic vulnerability assets |
| 4 | CyberChainBench | **9.5/10** | 541 cases, on-chain tools, Harbor isolation, Foundry exploit/patch oracle, and reward-hacking protection forming a complete benchmark platform |
| 5 | CVE-GENIE | **9.5/10** | 6,200 tracked files, 428/429 per-example runs, visualizer, VM library, and five OSF experiment packages forming a complete CVE reproduction platform |
| 6 | codebadger | **9.4/10** | already formed a CPG lifecycle, caching, async tasks, container isolation, query locking, and high-level security tool system |
| 7 | RustMizan | **9.4/10** | Apache-2.0, 173 compilable variants, complete path manifest, HF parquet, and 16 `.eval` logs forming one of the strongest cross-language benchmark artifacts |
| 8 | QuartetFuzz | **9.4/10** | main code, 100-case data, 25-case reproduction package, and precomputed trajectories inspectable; RQ1/RQ5, full RQ3, tests, and license still missing |
| 9 | AXE | **9.4/10** | 40 container targets, complete logs, PoCs, oracles, and fresh replay suitable for directly connecting to the validation layer |
| 10 | KLEECopilot | **9.4/10** | 12 real programs, complete configs/results, and ~804.78GB extracted assets proving engineering is real; reproduction cost is extremely high, so a lightweight replay subset is needed first |
| 11 | VULBENCH-CPP | **9.3/10** | complete data/scripts/results/notebooks for 71,661 files with reproduction instructions, ~50GB needed, suitable as a multi-layer validation benchmark base |
| 12 | CodeCureAgent | **9.3/10** | 1,000 inputs, complete logs, patches, diffs, CSV, and recomputation process public, the most mature alert-handling pipeline |
| 13 | Antiproof | **9.3/10** | detector, eCPG, isolated validation, and disclosure state machine forming a complete platform; core implementation not yet public |
| 14 | PATCHEVAL | **9.3/10** | 230 verified tasks, ~500GB images, Agent adapters, patch runner, and dynamic evaluator forming a runnable repair benchmark platform |
| 15 | Sailor | **9.3/10** | 89 substantive files, ~406KB main scripts, and CodeQL/KLEE/ASan flow separable; core finding/reproducer/raw runs not yet public |
| 16 | CyberGym-E2E | **9.2/10** | 920 tasks all have config, build/test/PoC scripts, and patches; 111/139 images digest-pinned, missing raw trajectories, per-task results, and complete cost logs |
| 17 | QLCoder | **9.2/10** | main flow, three Agent backends, Docker, and LSP MCP usable; missing paper raw results and automated tests |
| 18 | GONDAR | **9.2/10** | clear boundaries between CodeQL/Joern, Agent, Jazzer, and exploit oracle, complete artifacts |
| 19 | FLAMES | **9.2/10** | 10,531 repository files, ~412.6MB, training/compile/semantic/functional/exploit artifacts inspectable; missing root license and raw results have caliber cracks |
| 20 | OpenAnt | **9.2/10** | six-stage end-to-end funnel and multilingual engineering scale strong, but paper version not frozen and evidence not item-by-item public |
| 21 | Library Hallucinations / LibHalluBench | **9.2/10** | 205 files, 127 output artifacts, PyPI packages, and HF data snapshot, most suitable for engineering extraction; the official release also carries malformed-prompt and dynamic-oracle defects |
| 22 | EVMbench (`2603.04915`) | **9.2/10** | real repository tasks, containers, grader, oracle patch/exploit, RPC gatekeeper, and transaction replay are the most complete engineering assets in this batch |
| 23 | VulWeaver | **9.1/10** | source, benchmark, RQ data, and real-project results public; graph-augmentation cost high but interfaces reusable |
| 24 | CVE-Factory | **9.1/10** | task generation, environment construction, PoC, validation, training data, and continuous leaderboard forming complete infrastructure |
| 25 | SecDrift | **9.1/10** | prompts, 5,355 generation records, SAST findings, two-person verdicts, statistical scripts, and functional checks public, directly modifiable into a prompt robustness regression platform |
| 26 | JavaVulBench | **9.1/10** | large public data, unified multi-backend harness, prediction files, checkpoints, and table pipeline directly researchable; need to freeze the paper snapshot first |
| 27 | AutoTrace | **9.0/10** | rich intermediate artifacts, trigger/witness tool layer reusable; metric scripts and paper version must be unified first |
| 28 | Agent Audit | **9.0/10** | installable CLI, 72 rules, SARIF/CI, MCP checks, and large-scale tests forming a usable product; paper benchmark drift is separate |
| 29 | SecureVibeBench | **9.0/10** | 105-task data entry, Docker, functional/PoV/SAST evaluation, and multi-Agent adapters public; paper snapshot and current leaderboard need freezing |

| 39 | FuzzingBrain CRS | **9.0/10** | fixed commit includes multi-services, scheduling, static analysis, and 23 strategies; runtime failures also provide fail-closed, logging, and config governance counterexamples |

| 43 | Sifting the Noise | **8.8/10** | directly connectable after CodeQL/Semgrep to add alert evidence, tier, and human review |
| 44 | VulnAgent-R2 | **8.8/10 (design draft)** | architecture blueprint extremely strong, but public code cannot yet prove R2 modules are implemented per the paper |
| 45 | SecCoderX | **8.8/10** | code, training data, reward model, aligned checkpoints, and evaluation pipeline public; complete retraining cost high |

| 49 | CPRVUL | **8.8/10 (design) / 0.2/10 (artifacts)** | CPG context collection, summarization, and ranking flow easy to replicate; repository only 44-byte README, no license, code, or data |

#### 4. Core Conclusion Dependability

| Paper | Conclusion that can be relied on | Conclusion that cannot be directly relied on |
|---|---|---|
| KLEECopilot (`2607.21676`) | The combination of directed weights, LLM feedback, and KLEE found 6 independent sanitizer locations not covered by random-path in heavy real experiments, and 12 of the 18 filtered reports received developer confirmation | Cannot write the 87 independent locations as 87 real vulnerabilities; random-path already covered 81, and the 12 projects, huge compute, and manual filtering limit extrapolation |
| Sailor (`2604.06506`) | CodeQL specs, static localization, LLM prioritization, and KLEE/ASan can form a high-throughput candidate-to-crash funnel; 251 of 421 independent `.a` crashes are reproducible via libFuzzer | Cannot call the 379 `(file,function,line)` locations 379 new vulnerabilities; manifest, reproducer, and raw runs are withheld, and a crash is not a maintainer confirmation |
| Antares (`2608.02407`) | Repository exploration and localization-specialized training let a 3B-scale model approach strong closed-source models' file-localization F1, and explore-first prompt gives practical gains on small models | Cannot treat 500 problems as 500 independent vulnerabilities, nor security-patch changed files as human root-cause ground truth; duplicates, contamination, and the unpublished 3B limit the headline |
| GRASP (`2510.09682`) | The security-practice DAG and relevance traversal are a reusable security-specification representation, usable for condition/action/outcome constraints in the generation stage | Cannot rely on "maintaining functional correctness" or the "zero-day" conclusion; Security Rate excludes invalid outputs, 3/4 models' secure-pass@1 declined, test-set threshold selection, and no public artifacts |
| SCAFFOLD-CEGIS (`2603.08520`) | Semantic anchor, four-layer gate, counterexample backfill, and failure assimilation are a high-value secure-edit architecture | Cannot treat DR=0, SMR=100%, or latent-security gains as general evidence; the complete comparison is ~96 chains, same-source Semgrep gate, single Opus judge, and no artifacts |
| MULTIVUL (`2604.25711`) | Multimodal code representation can be a candidate-enhancement direction awaiting independent validation; the percentage-point improvement over same-text fine-tuning can be seen as limited internal signal | Cannot rely on its OOD generalization conclusion; all four DiverseVul F1 values are below the all-positive 66.67% baseline, and subset split, repeated runs, code, and raw predictions are all missing |
| HoF-Bench | Given a target file scope, non-frontier models can indeed rediscover many real AI-discovered CVEs; vulnerability identity should simultaneously match path, root cause, condition, and impact | 65/95 is not open-ended project-audit recall; all-positive provides no precision/FPR, and the LLM judge, dedup, provider raw calls, and cost are still not fully calibrated |
| Library Hallucinations / LibHalluBench | Models comply with user-provided wrong package/member names, forming real build and slopsquatting risk; public outputs prove the experiment really ran at scale | Specific percentages must be recomputed after fixing the 963 malformed prompts, freezing the PyPI snapshot, isolating no-code/error, and calibrating the member detector |
| SecPI | Training structured security reasoning as default behavior without an explicit security prompt is a security-generation route worth validating | Cannot rely on cross-CWE/cross-language exact gains; the repository 404, no raw predictions, and the FUNCSEC/SECRATIO tables violate mathematical invariants |
| PromptAudit | Models' vulnerability judgments vary significantly with prompt, abstention policy, and model combination; prompt sensitivity should enter deployment regression | Cannot directly rely on the absolute rankings of CoT, adaptive CoT, or self-consistency; data cleaning, fixed seed, and custom accuracy/effective F1 all distort results |
| MulVul | CWE-family routing, dedicated detectors, retrieval, and an independent prompt evaluator form a high-value multi-Agent architecture | Cannot rely on 34.79%, 41.5%, or 51.6%; missing same-model/equal-budget comparison, data manifest, code, cost, and prompt-evolution trajectory |
| Benign/Malicious Packages | Dynamic command, network, and file behaviors can be candidate-ranking signals for malicious packages, and some separability remains after group split | Cannot treat the current AUC/FPR as the paper's stated ~2,169-package, 17-model experiment; the notebook is actually 4,043 rows, 16 models, and labels strongly couple with features |
| MPC-Patch-Bench | MPC security/numerical risks do exist in functionally-passing patches; a patch benchmark should report both functional and security-verified resolution | Cannot treat 17.1% as an open-ended repository repair rate; tasks give gold-file scope, 163/205 synthesized from public patches, and verifier precision and gold-patch pass rate not reported |
| CHAINTRIX | Structured protocol evidence, multi-pipeline discovery, claim-first counterexample, and coverage-gap backfill form a high-value audit Agent architecture; 86/120 overall recall is recomputable from the table | Cannot rely on 81.0% mean, 61.0% perfect, 26pp gain, 14/28 minutes, or the low-false-positive conclusion; 198/222 claims in the public traces' reports remain unknown |
| The Language of Security | Deleting security constraints, boundary conditions, and entity bindings from prompts substantially changes the CodeQL finding rate; systematic prompt transformation is worth robustness testing | Cannot call it pure syntax causality; Java success/failure, language prevalence, and Python baseline/permutation direction have hard conflicts, and the outcome is not a real vulnerability |
| IaC Security Smells | IaC task descriptions, comments, and long context may provide security signals; CodeBERT/LongFormer can be a baseline to strictly redo | Cannot rely on 0.90 F1 as project-isolated generalization, the existing RQ2 as semantic ablation, or the LLM comparison as a fair experiment; data scale, pair split, tables, and F1 do not close |
| NFT Vulnerability Detection | Code slicing and external knowledge raise models' positive-label rate on prescreened NFT candidates | 97.11%/87.11%/73.78% are neither accuracy, recall, nor true detection rates; no addresses, data IDs, independent labels, model config, or confusion matrix |
| Context-Based Adversarial Attacks | Unsafe instructions, example copying, and context position are attack surfaces worth including in code-generation security evaluation | Cannot rely on 10.7×, χ²=847.3, 83% transfer, r=.47, 2.3× attention, 89.1% DR/0.3% FPR, or 520ms; the master data ledger is unrecoverable |
| PatchIsland (`2601.17471`) | Heterogeneous repair Agents, shared environment, and failure-aware scheduling improve plausible-candidate coverage; official AIxCC review also confirms PoV pass does not equal correct fix | Cannot directly write the internal 84/92, best-of-agents 87/92, or 27/33 as correct-patch rates; plausible/correct/coverage denominators conflict and unintended precision is only 28.57% |
| AgentSZZ (`2604.02665`) | blame-first, cross-refactor history tracking, and constrained git tool investigation clearly outperform simple SZZ/LLM baselines, with relative gains directionally valid across four datasets | Cannot rely on the exact sample totals of 2,095/2,096/2,098/2,104, mini-SWE F1, cost table, or error taxonomy; 40/50 high-confidence errors show calibration unresolved |
| DeepTraVul (`2603.27067`) | issue, PR, commit, diff, and CWE anchor have complementary detection value; the public 235-case CSV supports ~81.21% precision, 90.30% applicable recall, 85.51% F1 | 90.30% applies only to 134 processable samples, cannot extrapolate to all 826/2,402; paper tables, time split, formulas, and DeepDFA incremental caliber conflict |
| VulKey (`2605.01769`) | Action + Key Element is a valuable fix-knowledge representation, usable as a structured post-generation constraint for retrieval, planning, and generation | 31.5%, Vul4J's 18, and manual 159/188 all lack compile/test/PoC or public annotation; budget unfair and repository cannot reproduce Recall@10/Precision@10 |
| SCPatcher (`2604.00687`) | Contract knowledge graph, retrieval re-ranking, and iterative static feedback can be a fix-candidate generation layer, with 182/200 compile rate as a syntax metric | Cannot treat 163/182 Slither alert disappearance as 89.6% semantic accuracy, nor 163/200 as a real fix rate; no dynamic, functional, or human oracle or public artifacts |
| AgenticVM (`2605.01739`) | Deterministic parsing, authoritative vulnerability-source completion, prioritization, and recommendation Agents can form a runnable vulnerability-management pipeline, and public artifacts let main counts be recomputed | 97.94% is CVE-ID dedup and threshold filtering, not false-positive elimination; 0.4613 is not per-item triage F1, and 89.3% is not complete CVSS-vector exact accuracy |
| KeaRepair (`2607.00820`) | Structured control/data-flow knowledge, program-analysis tools, and the diagnose—repair—PoC/test loop form a high-value repair-Agent design | 14/55 target CVEs are already in the knowledge-base sources, 12 enter the success numerator; cannot treat 46/55 as unseen-vulnerability generalization or a fair-baseline advantage |
| MARGIN (`2605.10240`) | Under the current random function-classification protocol, margin-aware geometry is a competitive long-tail training objective, usable as a cheap candidate ranker | Cannot rely on BigVul 90.30 as project-level capability, 1e-10 significance, uniqueness over standard imbalance loss, or industrial CI readiness |
| VulStyle (`2604.26313`) | AST and stylometry may provide complementary candidate signals, worth re-studying under strict source isolation | BigVul test enters pretraining, metrics misaligned, and artifacts incomplete; cannot rely on 95.06% F1, test-unseen, or 34.04pp multimodal gain |
| Adaptive Survey (`2604.27000`) | Structural analysis, runtime feedback, AI adaptivity, human triage, and CI/CD gate should form a typed-evidence closed loop | PRISMA arithmetic does not close, single-author κ is not independent and no corpus; cannot rely on 55 primary studies or topic frequencies as systematic-review facts |
| Dissecting the Black Box (`2605.29901`) | circuit/causal analysis can find length and surface shortcuts in vulnerability models, and public artifacts prove auditability itself matters | Model identity, correct-sample filtering, cached baseline, re-verification, and length confounding make the 100%→6%, L0, and 16.1% capacity conclusions unreliable |
| Lightweight Vulnerability Detection (`2605.04260`) | Simple metrics/token models are cheap and transparent but limited cross-project, usable as a low-cost floor for complex systems | Under identifier renaming, the metrics-only model should be unchanged by definition yet changes markedly; cannot rely on the robustness table and specific performance deltas as established results |
| GAPS (`2511.23213`) | target-directed static paths and constrained semantic fallback indeed let some Android target methods be observed by Frida hooks; the strict all-target end-to-end rate is still ~49.2%/34.0% | Cannot rely on 56.93%/54.80% as all-target dynamic reachability, and cannot ignore the 2,713/2,800 and 47/50 JSON/CSV snapshot inconsistency |
| Solidity Verification Oracle (`2509.19153`) | GPT-5 reaches ~92.20% accuracy and 92.09% F1 on 667 controlled properties, usable for triage and explaining verification obligations | Cannot rely on RQ2 manual summaries, several real-project exact counts, or treat the LLM oracle as a sound formal verifier |
| SmartFuzz (`2511.12164`) | Stateful fuzzing should do local/global continuous reflection around real execution feedback, and multi-role responsibility separation has methodological value | Cannot rely on 150/154, 105/108, 74.7%, or 80% given example isolation, unified supported baseline, repeated runs, and raw trajectories missing |
| FinDet (`2509.18934`) | bytecode semantic lifting, three-stage fund flow, and multi-prompt entropy fusion are a reasonable training-free candidate-detection route | Cannot treat 0.9374 as strict held-out generalization, 29 items as real confirmed findings, or the existing experiments as proving real obfuscation robustness on FinDet inputs |
| Balkanization SoK (`2607.05743`) | denylist fragility, benign scope creep, TOCTOU, MCP stale-state, and policy-authoring error are stable Agent execution risks | Cannot treat the 39-paper corpus as the complete domain population, nor the single-author review as an independent two-person systematic-review check |
| CFCEval (`2512.06248`) | exact match/CodeBLEU are insufficient for evaluating secure code generation; a benchmark should include transformations and human-relevance calibration | Cannot rely on ELRM as a reliable automatic security metric, GPT-Tagger/Score percentages as real fix rates, or FixCap/PTFixCap as already execution-oracle validated |
| QuartetFuzz | P1-P4 quality contracts, pre-generation checks, and repeated fuzzing significantly reduce erroneous harnesses and false crashes; production-harness fixes and real vulnerabilities show real external value | The current artifacts only fully cover the 25/100 RQ3 subset, excluding RQ1/RQ5; cannot treat 96/100, 40/42, and all maintainer statuses as already independently recomputed |
| CyberGym-E2E | 920 real vulnerabilities, 139 projects, and S1-S4 layering show end-to-end target-vulnerability handling is still far below patch-only, with vulnerability localization rather than patch generation the main bottleneck | S3 can fix a different vulnerability, only the hidden-target-PoC S4 represents target capability; 28 project images not digest-pinned, and raw trajectories, per-task results, and complete cost logs missing |
| Antiproof | A high-recall detector, scope pruning, and executable capability oracle form an effective discover—validate funnel; public disclosures show the system has real output | Before core code and raw results are public, cannot treat 64/66, 510 PoEs, 25x performance, or all CVE assignments as independently reproduced facts |
| ContraFix | Control-runtime evidence and dual-track experience clearly outperform the same-backbone Patcher; oracle pass greatly overestimates the true semantic fix rate | Before the Figshare artifacts are verified, cannot fully treat 91.8% as independent reproduction; stability beyond the fixed task order also not proven |
| Vul4Py | Exploit oracle, functional regression, and human semantic audit must be reported in layers; 15 of 119 security-only passes were filtered by the functional gate, proving a lone security test overestimates fix success | Tasks give the CVE and human patch file/line range, and the public repo lacks paper patches, raw verdicts, and human labels; cannot treat 51/43/41 as open-ended detect—localize—fix success rates |
| PATCHEVAL | Real CVE repair must be separated into location, end-to-end, and feedback settings using a security + functional dynamic oracle; complex cross-file patches remain a hard bottleneck | The original PoC oracle is acknowledged by the authors to reject some equivalent fixes; the paper's 23% and the current verified leaderboard's 80%+ are not the same snapshot and cannot be directly compared |
| VulnRepairEval | A repair evaluation should at least require the vulnerable version PoC to trigger and the model's patched PoC to fail; the best of the current 12 models is only 5/23, showing localization and correct repair remain weak | The original PoC failing is necessary but not sufficient; the 23-case selection is opaque, no functional regression, PoCs and container artifacts not public, cannot treat 5/23 as a root-cause fix rate |
| SLYP | Strong binary tools, dynamic debugging, and debugger-verified PoCs let the Agent discover and validate real COTS vulnerabilities; real CVE/bounty confirm the directional value | With post-run constructed ground truth, best-of-object triple aggregation, and asymmetric tool permissions, cannot treat 64/64 as stable single-shot recall or attribute all gains to SLYP orchestration |
| CyberChainBench | Historical mainnet fork, profit scoring, attack replay, and normal-transaction regression provide harder security ground truth than a text judge for detect/exploit/patch | Cannot treat the full leaderboard as a recomputed fact given the paper's 94-patch subset, the current public filtered 110, the unpublished 200-case exploit manifest, and no raw eval tree |
| 2601.19239 | Current project-level detection remains low-recall, high-false-positive, high-cost; source/sink and cross-procedure reasoning are the main bottlenecks | Should not generalize specific tool rankings to all languages, CWEs, and future models |
| SecDrift | In the current 9-CWE Python generation setting, there is no evidence that critical-infrastructure industry prompts cause systematic security drift; the matched baseline, placebo, and CWE exclusion support this negative conclusion | 14.0%/13.2%/11.4% are Bandit ∪ Semgrep alert rates, not real vulnerability rates; single template/CWE, interface drift, refusal, and XSS/weak-crypto missed-detection limit extrapolation |
| GONDAR | Given a harness and sink-type Java vulnerabilities, LLM semantic guidance significantly enhances Jazzer, dynamically confirmable via sanitizers | Cannot generalize 41/54 to universal recall for arbitrary repos, nor ignore the 35/54 injected/competition vulnerabilities |
| Chai | Multi-implementation deterministic differencing provides hard candidate signals for crypto misuse, then the Agent explains guarantees and traces downstream dependencies; two wolfSSL high-severity CVEs and fixes externally confirmed | Cannot write the 159 preliminary downstream PoCs or the abstract "100+" as confirmed vulnerabilities; no public harness, inputs, differencing, and finding artifacts, and the exact efficiency multiple is not independently recomputed |
| Web Vulnerability Reproduction | Current Agents remain weak on real web environments, authentication, precise localization, and real triggers; a runnable PoC is far from a triggered vulnerability | Figshare artifacts unverified, project under 30MB, and containers simplify the production environment; cannot generalize exact Agent rankings to all web systems |
| CVE-GENIE | Multi-stage knowledge preparation, build, exploit, execution verifier, critics, and feedback can reproduce many CVEs at scale, and public code plus per-example logs show the system has runnable output | Cannot interpret all 428 LLM-verifier successes as zero-misjudgment, zero-contamination independent confirmed reproductions; the current repo's 429 directory and paper's 428 not aligned |
| Diffploit | Failure-symptom-driven causing/supporting diff retrieval, iterative transfer, and annealing search significantly improve cross-version exploit adaptation; three CNA updates have external evidence | Cannot rely verbatim on 580/689 and 82 accepted: the PDF row sums to 590, public results are 579+1 flaky, and the body writes 57 affected versions |
| ARVO | Thousands of OSS-Fuzz vulnerabilities can be rebuilt into triggerable, modifiable, multi-PoC-regressable dynamic assets; the public database, images, metadata, and manual logs confirm infrastructure value | The full 6,138/8,921 68.8% and the random-100 81% must be separated; body 96/97, 92/93, 36/37, and weighted accuracy slightly disagree with logs |
| FLAMES | Executable guard synthesis should report compile, semantic equivalence, normal function, and exploit blocking in layers; public results confirm 4,835/5,000 compile success and best 22/108 heist blocking | Given insertion positions is not open-ended audit; the current split is not isolated by contract/family, and raw CSV cannot fully rebuild all multi-round triples, so cannot count the stronger/weaker 446 as semantic equivalence |
| VULBENCH-CPP | On this C++ competition sample, after controlling task relevance, length, and tests, AI code still has significantly higher odds of triggering sanitizer violations; static tools cannot replace dynamic validation | Cannot write ESBMC 29.5% vs. 18.8% as significant, nor ignore the all-tests-pass subset AI 1.8% / human 3.6%, nor extrapolate to production repos |
| RustMizan | compile-first, vulnerable/safe pair, and file/function context ladder reveal the fault line where models "know there is a vulnerability but cannot localize the line"; public data and 16 logs confirm the benchmark artifact | 42 CVEs, 173 mutation variants, and single-run per setting cannot determine a universal Rust ecosystem ranking; mutation security/vulnerable semantics remain bounded by author construction |
| QLCoder | LSP, precise retrieval, and vulnerable/fixed execution feedback significantly improve dedicated CodeQL query synthesis for known CVEs | 53.4% is the rule-synthesis success rate given patch ground truth, not an unknown-vulnerability discovery rate; current artifacts also cannot recompute all tables |
| VulWeaver | Graph semantic repair and overall context significantly improve vulnerability judgment on given target functions, and real-project candidates obtained strong human and maintainer evidence | Cannot interpret results as proven open-ended whole-repo search; evaluation gives target functions and CWE, and graph-augmented edges are only sampled-verified |
| SecureVibeBench | Real vulnerability-introducing scenarios distinguish functional from security capability; functional tests and PoV together show current code Agents struggle to be both correct and secure | C-SEC mixes functional failure, PoV failure, and high-false-positive Semgrep suspicious items; single-run, indirect contamination analysis, and paper/current leaderboard drift limit exact ranking |
| RealSec-bench | In real-repository context, joint functional and secure generation is far harder than function-level tasks; RAG mainly improves function, and general security prompts may yield negative returns due to compile/function failure | SecurePass security ground truth is bounded by 19 CodeQL query classes and an LLM-judge candidate space; 59/105 is Log Injection, cannot serve as cross-CWE or complete exploit-free security rate |
| CPRVUL | CPG context of caller, callee, and global, after summarization and relevance ranking, gives a small but consistent gain on given target functions | The 6.7-11.1 point summary cannot be fully attributed to context; the same Qwen2.5-32B ablation is only 1.56-2.42 points, and there is no open-ended repo search, clean function-only baseline, or usable reproduction package |
| Cross-Procedure Context Evaluation | On known-vulnerability positives, naively adding direct caller/callee usually does not improve recall but significantly increases tokens; public raw outputs suffice to recompute the main table | The data has no negatives, precision is always 1, accuracy equals recall; cannot interpret 0.98 F1 as true detection precision, and overall cost mixes in 58 out-of-scope Java prompts |
| Sifting the Noise | Strong-model Agent cross-file navigation significantly improves SAST FP identification | Cannot auto-close alerts just because FPR dropped to 6.3%; on OWASP it wrongly deleted 22.25% true vulnerabilities |
| PVBench | Basic tests and original PoC passing greatly overestimate AVR success; the current public raw still gives 41.26% FDR, and PoC+ should become a necessary supplementary oracle | Cannot treat the paper's 42.34% and exact failure-taxonomy ratios as fully recomputed; public raw misses one vulnerability and drifts from the paper pass/fail status, per-item human labels not open |
| LASAA | Multi-run consistency, reasoning comparison, and dynamic trigger provide supplementary evidence for static alert adjudication; valid trigger is a strong true-positive signal | Generalization mainly depends on 76 unpublished FormAI labels, 7 of which were revised after observing model/trigger; 98%/94.8% cannot extrapolate to industrial SAST auto-closing capability |
| FuzzingBrain V2 | Given a harness/sanitizer, LLM Suspicious Point and Direction with global/directed trippers significantly improve dynamic vulnerability discovery, and real maintainer confirmations show real value | Cannot claim to fairly beat AIxCC teams with unequal resources; abstract 29/all confirmed fixes conflicts with body 41/26/23, and the current repo lacks paper raw runs and per-item finding evidence |
| Veritas | The evidence boundary of witness-backed static flow, LLM claims, and runtime confirmation is supported by 18/20 OOB and strong slicing results, a reasonable architecture for reliable binary reasoning | The 3,344 sampled candidates only verify 334, cannot be written as only 2 FP overall; 20 cases, `-O0`, and closed-source Slicer/Validator limit generalization and reproduction |
| AXE | Given CWE and localization info, multi-role Agents can generate a subset of reproducible exploits, 11 of the 12/40 replayable in a fresh environment | Cannot treat Success@5 30% as open-ended vulnerability-discovery recall, nor generalize to repos without harness, entry, or oracle |
| KASS | structured plan, inner code repair, outer strategy replan, and Foundry postconditions are a reasonable architecture for attack validation after a known finding; the public package proves at least substantial structured attack material and some runnable artifacts | Input gives vulnerability type/location; the existing package lacks core Agent, unified adjudication, and many per-example PoCs/logs, and some target copy/harness weakens integrity, so cannot treat 98/104 as independent reproduction |
| DrillAgent | Given a vulnerability, RCA, instrumentation-effectiveness checks, coverage feedback, and an independent crash stage clearly improve PoV generation under a fixed budget | Cannot write 28.9% as open-ended discovery recall; Full-success-30-problem-tuned ablation, non-hard budget, and missing complete raw traces limit effect-size extrapolation |
| V2E | For the 5 localized classes of smart-contract alerts, execution-feedback PoCs provide trigger and economic-impact evidence for a subset, honestly retaining MC | Cannot treat single-digit FPR on the definite-conclusion subset as a full automatic-filter rate, nor generalize "no direct profit" to "no security impact" |
| SmartPoC | BCE and Action-State Differential Verification upgrade "PoC executes" to key-state-change evidence, significantly improving PPV on both benchmarks | The 64 TPs of latest-114 come from only 85 confirmed manual samples, not the full 545-finding ground truth; without code, data, and raw results cannot independently recompute 98% PPV |
| Heimdallr | Graph-community context, knowledge reminders, and an independent threat-model Verifier significantly improve the raw Auditor's precision/F1, and real deployment shows practical findings | The paper's FPR is actually false discovery rate; under a closed-source core, possibly asymmetric D3 ground-truth handling, and weak stability pilot, cannot directly cite 86.7%, cost advantage, or cross-tool ranking |
| Contextual Bias | Semantic framing in PR descriptions, commit messages, and comments changes real ACR decisions, and adaptive iteration is more dangerous than fixed templates | 17/17 cannot directly generalize to all ACRs; patch rollback does not guarantee all 17 vulnerabilities remain exploitable, and the replication package is not actually public |
| SWExploit | issue, fake traceback, and reproduce code can induce a repair Agent to generate a test-passing but payload-carrying patch, showing requirement text is also a supply-chain input | Static Correct-ASR cannot serve as exploitability; dynamic PER is clearly lower, the attack needs to affect issue content, at most ten iterations, and the public GitHub does not contain core source |
| Words Speak Louder Than Code | framing, anchoring, and halo metadata systematically change vulnerability-reporting propensity; the delta-FPR-on-delta-Recall slope 0.994, R²=.93 shows most change is threshold shift rather than discriminative gain | ASR is computed only on already-neutral-correct TPs, not overall miss rate; single-run, no public prompts/raw outputs, cannot directly extrapolate combined ASR to production repos |
| Security Calibration | Models' self-reported high confidence cannot serve as a security oracle; repository build, function constraints, and execution gating significantly change False Trust | Cannot attribute ECE mixing build fragility entirely to security-knowledge failure, nor report only gated ECE while hiding the 46%-86% refusal rate |
| AgentFlow | Harness role, topology, schema, prompt, tools, and coordination protocol can be jointly searched, and typed validation with execution diagnosis is a reasonable automated-optimization mechanism; Chrome external records show real output | Cannot treat the 84.3% from repeatedly optimizing the same 89 problems as held-out generalization; four vendor-only findings, two CVE type/severity headlines, and complete Chrome raw runs are not yet reliable |
| CVE-Factory | Automatically generating large-scale real, executably replicable CVE tasks is feasible and valuable | Before the denominator is clarified, cannot unconditionally cite 66.2%; training gain is also not open-ended zero-day capability |
| AVIATOR | Decomposing the manual vulnerability-injection workflow and adding multi-round static, formal, and human validation improves clean/vulnerable pair quality with some downstream gain | 93% mixes different oracles, PrimeVul has only 34 analyzable samples; cannot interpret 133 CWE support or relative F1 gains as universally effective project-level |
| SecureReviewer | Security-specialized fine-tuning, structured review contract, and expert-revised references make diff-level comments focus on security issues; public data and implementation provide a rebuildable base | Non-Issue is only no-comment diffs, random split has duplicate patches, 262/261/260 denominator misaligned and SecureBLEU tuned on the same-batch human scores; cannot treat 71.98 F1 as a real-project vulnerability-review rate or claim RAG reduces hallucination |
| DREA | Decoupling strong Planner and local Explorer improves pair correctness and recall, and the Lucky Hit metric reveals "guessed right but reasoned wrong" | Cannot ignore the FPR rise, 0.35M-1.56M tokens per sample, local A800 cost, and missing raw logs |
| EvoRepair | Structured distillation of success/failure experiences, quality re-ranking, and cross-task retrieval improve a multi-round repair Agent's cumulative verifier success, suitable as a versioned experience library | 90.46% is Success@15, not one-shot; continuously writing experience within the test set is transductive learning, results depend on task order, and no public code |
| Mastermind | Taking reusable strategy rather than raw trajectories as the learning unit, calibrated by a task-local Curator and dual-build verifier, is a strong design for high-budget repo-reproduction Agents | 84.5% is the result after training 260 tasks, Level-3 info, and up to 8 attempts; project/CVE/family isolation not proven, training rollout cost not counted, and the public link is 404 |
| SecCoderX | CWE-conditioned learned reward with online RL slightly improves secure-code ESR, and public code, data, and checkpoints suffice for later reproduction | Reward model average F1 67.9, function proxy mainly length/AST, cannot interpret ESR gains as build/test/exploit-confirmed secure-code rate |
| LogicScan | Extracting structured BSL invariants from mature protocols and comparing missing constraints is an effective business-logic-audit direction, with context/aggregation ablation supporting denoising value | Knowledge-base isolation from target/post-fix clones not strictly proven, Top200 is not complete benign ground truth; with artifacts 410 cannot directly cite 85.2% as a no-leakage generalization rate |
| DAGVul | Vulnerability reasoning should be represented as structured causal dependencies, checked via semantic perturbation to see if conclusions rely on surface patterns | With 114 real test samples, LLM-synthesized ground truth/same-source judge, and no artifacts, cannot treat the 18.9% improvement or 8B approaching Claude as independently established |
| MAVM | Historical CVE root cause, trigger, patch dependency, and repo context can form an effective recurrence-detection/repair process | Tasks start from known-patch-similar candidates, not open-ended discovery; 57.3% excludes FN, and no build/test/exploit evidence, cannot be a full real-vulnerability fix rate |
| VulnResolver | Directed repo context, executable Safety Property, and multi-candidate dynamic selection clearly improve SEC-bench issue resolution | 75%/67.5% are benchmark-verifier resolution; without unified full baselines, repeated variance, public implementation, and comprehensive semantic audit, cannot be a real production fix rate |
| VulnAgent-R2 | "Evidence accumulation, counterexample, validation, calibration, and budget joint optimization" is a reasonable design direction | Before reproduction, cannot treat the .385 PrimeVul F1, significance, and 38.3% token savings as verified facts |
| VulnLLM-R | A dedicated security-reasoning model provides strong cross-language OOD representation on given function/project candidates, and the public 7B weights suit the candidate-reasoning base | ARVO test gives the patch-modified function oracle; five projects only require hitting any golden-patch function, and the paper Agent, raw runs, and 15 zero-day evidences are unpublished, cannot be an open-ended zero-day audit rate |
| HardSecBench | 924 C/Verilog secure code-generation tasks released, with complete file and test assets; the final tasks' actual mean coverage 94.97% and mutation 77.34% recomputable from the repo | The paper writes the 1,170 initial-pass tasks' ~92.7%/70.2% as retained statistics; Verilog coverage is not dynamic coverage, and mutation cannot prove the security harness alone is reliable |
| ShadowProbe | LLM-constructed worst-case input, project dependency recovery, and multi-scale dynamic growth inference turn complexity suspicions into falsifiable runtime evidence; no-Dynamic-ablation collapse supports this boundary | WISE only 10 cases, PyEmailBench human ground truth no kappa/CI, 162/84/41 real findings no per-item evidence, and `$0.006/target` is incompatible with 32K tokens; cannot treat real output volume and cost as recomputed facts |
| Code-Augur | Explicitly turning implicit security assumptions into assertions, then using guided-fuzzing counterexample and iterative revision, is a specification-first direction stronger than pure text judgment; real disclosures show external value | Known recall is not universally ahead, extra PoVs are not maintainer-confirmed vulnerabilities; without source, manifest, PoV, and raw runs cannot independently rely on the 34%–370% gains |
| KNOWDIT | Project semantic knowledge graph, spec generation, PoC execution, and reflection form a strong DeFi audit loop; human quality and κ on knowledge nodes support the KG ontology | 21/57/0 is union@5 over 11 projects, each $200 per run, five runs, not single-run stable performance; the 9 High/36 Medium/72 Low confirmed fixes have no per-item evidence, and artifacts still redacted |
| EvoPoC | The three-layer HKG of Contract Semantics, Failure Mode, and Exploit Primitive with reachability/profitability validation is a strong architecture hypothesis for DeFi exploit synthesis | D1 confusion-matrix unit inconsistent, D2's 85/88 exceeds all single-model 69-79/88 and is likely union/best-of; no code and per-item zero-day evidence, cannot rely on 98%, 96.6%, or the $70.6M headline |
| NeuroLog | LLM typed facts, Datalog composition, SMT witness, and ASan validation are auditable neuro-symbolic responsibility separation; the external libarchive issue/PR suggests at least some real findings are valid | Cannot extrapolate 90×/70× to complete projects or industrial static analysis, nor claim already project-level low false positives, new CVEs, or confirmed precision on the 295 FFmpeg findings |
| From Lab to Reality | Same-distribution high scores collapse on out-of-time Linux vulnerabilities, and vulnerable/patched pair distinction is stricter than one-sided recall | The 20-CVE VentiVul cannot determine universal model ranking; "unmodified function = safe" and GPT-5 out-of-time no-contamination are both not fully established |
| Hybrid-CASR | Time-forward window, replay, and LoRA merging can slightly improve continual vulnerability classification at lower training cost, improving some historical retention | The co-success 37-window only moves .651 to .667, Cliff's delta=.103; Phi-2 may have pretrained seen early CVEs, and single-model function-level results cannot prove repository-level continual audit advantage |
| CWE-Trace | DFI/HDD distinguish correct identification, paranoid all-report, and skeptical all-non-report; time pairs and context ladder suit a calibration diagnostic framework | The "84% contamination" decomposition is arithmetically impossible, abstract exact-CWE Top-1 <1.3% conflicts with body best 14.71%; only adapter public, cannot rely on headline numbers |
| JavaVulBench | Java vulnerability detection should adopt project/time/clone/CWE-family isolation, a unified prediction schema, and simultaneously report detection, localization, and calibration | Cannot directly rely on current sample totals, dedup split, DeepSeek-Coder main table, and contamination clean/risky results; paper and Zenodo snapshot not frozen-consistent |
| T2L | Given crash/trace, AST chunk narrowing and divergence tracing improve project-level line-by-line localization; ~half success also shows the task remains hard | The 50 cases are manually curated and not all homogeneous security vulnerabilities, ATA ablation removes base execution capability, code link 404; cannot be an open-ended discovery rate or full ARVO performance |
| Revisiting Vul-RAG | The Qwen baseline can be essentially reproduced; swapping in larger/newer models does not break through ~.30 PairAcc | Cannot thereby assert that all knowledge-level RAG has a universal .30 theoretical ceiling |
| Phoenix | Gherkin behavior contract is an effective intermediate representation, public results recompute 0.8246 F1; contract clearly beats no-contract Judge | 0.825 is not an open-ended vulnerability-discovery rate; Agent 2 first sees the same vulnerability's vulnerable/fixed pair, and the 17 "real FPs" also lack exploit-level confirmation |
| VulAgent | Splitting trigger hypotheses into precondition, path, counterexample, and final guard verification is a more suitable audit-evidence protocol than direct classification or multi-Agent voting | PrimeVul/SVEN P-C ~26%, FPR still 36.78%-55.42%; context not same-sample ablation, Oracle-CWE lowers FPR while significantly lowering F1, and artifacts now 410 |
| RuleForge | Multi-candidate rule generation, synthetic tests, production-flow enrichment, and human approval improve vulnerability-detection-rule productivity | IP reputation is not exploit ground truth; same-source Claude generator/judge, 100/50 small samples, closed-source, and confidential absolute denominators make the 67% FP reduction and 336% non-generalizable |
| SPECA | Cross-implementation reuse of specification checklists and explicit threat model reduce semantic blind spots and batch false positives in multi-implementation systems | V2 3/11 and top 4% human ranking come from post-hoc re-scoring the same ground truth, cannot be independent generalization evidence |
| MemRepair | Failure trajectories and layered memory are a reasonable repair-Agent design | Cannot treat 58% as a stable leak-free result when task order and online memory leakage are uncontrolled |
| SecureCode | The four-round security data schema, public revision history, and grounding/fix audit process are valuable | No model results, 93.8 is an internal self-score; the current public split conflicts with the zero-contamination/no-CVE-overlap caliber, cannot be called a proven effective production-grade ground truth |
| Taught by the Flawed | analyzer-aware data filtering may change small-model generation behavior, and training-data security governance deserves larger, multi-oracle experiments | Bandit finding is not a real vulnerability; only 5 independent outputs per model, mostly non-runnable, the only clear security difference comes from the SQL prompt, cannot prove universal security gain without harming correctness |
| SECJS | JavaScript audit should simultaneously test whole-project, vulnerable/fixed pairs, extremely low FPR, and semantic-preservation transformation robustness; this is a worthwhile direction to rebuild | RQ1/RQ4, website, and current code are three snapshots; loader truncation, augmentation placeholders, and TP/VD-S implementation do not correspond to the paper, cannot cite current key tables as recomputable evidence |
| CodeCureAgent | The Agent tool loop and build/rescan/tests approval produce a high proportion of buildable, scannable static-alert-handling changes, and public artifacts are per-item verifiable | 96.8% is a plausible-handling rate, not a correct security-fix rate; 303 FP suppressions and only 31 security types cannot prove real-vulnerability repair capability |
| RAVEN | Separating historical-patch retrieval and current-repo Curator, then adding Reviewer/Validator, is a reasonable repair architecture | 83.13% counts functional-regression Partial Fix as success; no execution validation and retrieval base/eval same-source, cannot be a real fix rate or generalization evidence |
| Agent Audit | The Agent/MCP software stack needs tool, prompt, config, secret, and privilege-specific static rules; the public tool can directly connect to CI/SARIF | The current repo cannot reproduce the paper's 22/42 and 0.909; the larger recomputable benchmark is only F1 0.778, cannot directly cite the four-fold recall advantage |
| AgenticSCR | Repo navigation, SAST candidates, and CWE guidelines improve the proportion of generated review comments meeting localization, relevance, and technical correctness simultaneously | 17.5% is a comment-level composite correctness ratio, not a 144-task success rate or 107-CVE recall; independent vulnerability coverage, repeated runs, cost, and public artifacts all missing |
| Security in a Workflow | The role-based flow of Planner/Analyzer/Fixer/Verifier and "CodeQL may help or might mislead" is a reasonable exploratory conclusion | 44%/31%/19%/13% recomputes from the public workbook's 16 cases yet conflicts with the body's 25 cases; the 150/300-run protocol, 69% verifier, and complete logs unclear, cannot rely on absolute effects |
| Vul-R2 | reasoning-data, SFT, and RL improve the model's exact match to dataset reference patches | No compile, test, vulnerability-elimination, or exploit oracle, cannot interpret 24.83% exact match as a security-fix success rate |
| Obfuscation Study | Surface and structural transformations significantly change detection results, and the binary "has vulnerability" metric masks the problem | 80%/75% attack success rates and model-family ranking still depend on whether transformations truly preserve semantics and vulnerability |
| VulReaD | CWE knowledge graph, teacher reasoning, and preference optimization can be a security-semantic supervision layer, and knowledge representation direction is worth independent reproduction | Random function split lacks project/time/clone isolation, Table 4 F1 arithmetic error, CWE-ID hit is not causal fidelity, LFS data and training entry unusable |
| VULSOLVER | source-to-sink paths, branch and project context constraints are a more reasonable vulnerability-evidence representation than function-level binary classification, and ablation shows branch/context contribute | Three classes of regularized OWASP CWE, borrowed baselines, all four backbones 100% recall, no cost/repeated-runs/artifacts, and author-claimed 15 real-problem cases cannot support universal 97.97% F1 |
| AutoTrace | Trigger statement and source-to-sink witness should be an independent, structured Agent tool interface | The current artifacts conflict with the paper on the 744 fixed denominator, VulnHit definition, and strict statement-level hit rate, cannot cite 75.0% as an established result |
| EntailLLM | Logic rules filter LLM paths conflicting with domain knowledge, and provide the first failing step and rule trace | 97.9% is entailment under same-source knowledge constraints, not vulnerability recall, precision, coverage, or exploitability |
| Multi-LLMSecCodeEval | LLM-only collaborative gains are limited; a strong static analyzer with a candidate-repair loop is the main improvement source for a secure-code pipeline | 97.32%/99.06% optimize and reuse CodeQL scoring, do not test function, and exclude uncompilable outputs, cannot be interpreted as real secure-code rate |
| ReVul-CoT | Labeled similar-vulnerability retrieval improves CVSS severity classification of known vulnerabilities, and public prediction files recompute main tables | 87.5% accuracy is not vulnerability-discovery rate; row-level split, same-CVE/near-duplicate, and directly providing neighbor labels limit generalization to new projects and CVEs |
| codebadger | CPG high-level tools let the Agent more effectively navigate directedly and construct executable evidence | Three cases cannot prove overall accuracy, low false positives, or universal improvement across models |
| Antaeus | Both local and project-level context improve known-sample recall for logic vulnerabilities | Cannot treat all discoveries not matching the target CVE as confirmed FPs, nor ignore the ~1.1% precision |
| VulRTex | rich-text issue, attachments, comments, and historical cases can be vulnerability-clue intake, turning external intelligence into downstream source/dynamic-validation hypotheses | 973,572 vs. 1,195,320 scale conflict, Table 4 has mathematically impossible F1, threshold and future-knowledge leakage not isolated and artifacts expired; cannot rely on 88.9 F1/99.2 AUROC as strict generalization |
| OpenAnt | The multi-stage static funnel, attacker simulation, and dynamic environment can be engineered to run | Cannot call the 144 locally dynamically reproduced candidates 144 confirmed new vulnerabilities |
| Adversarial Comments | On 100 short synthetic positives, static comment deception caused no statistically significant universal decline | Cannot generalize to real repos not fearing comment/metadata attacks; 96.9% SAST synergy is affected by samples preselected as SAST-detectable |
| VulTriage | Control/Knowledge/Semantic three-path context is a reasonable typed-evidence design, and the PrimeVul confusion matrix shows directional gain from the three-way combination on pair tasks | FPR 54.25%, public exact-graph extraction actually silently falls back, CWE library only 25 entries, pair side-by-side may be unfair and Kotlin flow broken; cannot attribute main results to the full three-path implementation described |
| iCodeReviewer | Routing vulnerability-family/language prompt experts has a gain signal on the company's 682 internal samples, suitable as a versioned security-review policy | precision/recall/F1 weighted/micro caliber conflict; no public data, prompt isolation, variance, and sufficient production denominator, cannot rely on 63.98 or one-week 84% as universal effects |
| SPEAR | Splitting Planner, Executor, Repairer, coordination protocol, fault recovery, and cost observation into first-class modules is a more mature system design than chat-style multi-Agent | The PFIR figure does not show Retry-only/LLM-only baselines, 18 vs 54 minutes is nearly a single case; without public implementation and raw runs cannot rely on 0.87 F1, 94% repair, or 3× speedup |
| VulnScout-C | A small dedicated MoE can achieve high binary-classification F1 on a fixed C microbenchmark, and scarce-CWE data completion clearly helps the current training flow | GPT verifier knows the target CWE and expected labels, ESBMC is bounded and does not guarantee the target CWE; 250 microbenchmarks, single seed, and unpublished artifacts cannot prove real-project low false positives or high-quality security ground truth |
| CKG-LLM | Compiling natural-language vulnerability specifications into executable KG/SPARQL queries is an inspectable intermediate layer worth connecting to audit Agents | The fixed 553-contract TP+FN varies per model, the same SLM participates in training feedback and test judgment, and Achecker F1 is even higher; cannot rely on 74.9% as a fair, independent detection result |
| Dual-Mode Benchmarks | Separating vulnerability-family methodology Agents, LLM reconnaissance/payload, and programmatic evidence rules is a more reasonable black-box security-testing architecture than direct prompting | Abstract conflicts with Table 4, five apps used simultaneously for training/development/evaluation, ARG no FP reported, private models, 100+ zero-day and cost no raw evidence; cannot rely on absolute scores, zero false positives, or cross-app generalization |
| MultiVer | RAG can suppress both TP and FP, and union/weighted voting exposes the recall-FPR conflict of multi-Agent aggregation; 85% FPR is an important negative result | 82.7% and fine-tuned GPT-3.5's 81.3% come from 202 and 300 different test sets, balanced accuracy below random; cannot claim a zero-shot system exceeds a fine-tuned model |
| ELVul | Different models may complement on language, CWE, and sample type, and dynamic routing deserves re-study with verifier-backed historical capability | Random function split, BigVul denominator conflict, relative best single model only ~1-2 points and no multi-seed; the public DGS five experts all read the same result file, cannot rely on current ensemble results or new-project generalization |
| Traditional CNN Vulnerability Detection | On the cleaned Juliet wide-label task, average AUPRC ~0.66, and hits a few historical Linux vulnerability functions | Merging CWE labels with older fine labels is unfair, positive duplication has split-leakage risk; Linux only reports 1/41, 4/41 positive hit and no clean FP, cannot claim low-false-positive real detection |
| Constitutional SDD | A versioned security constitution, spec/plan/tasks, and bidirectional traceability is a security-governance model worth productizing | n=1, no baseline artifacts, and public code violates CSRF, secret, revocation, rate limit, TLS/encryption, and testing principles; cannot cite 73% reduction or 15/15 fully implemented |
| VCAO | token, static-analysis, fuzzing, GPU, and human budgets in vulnerability discovery deserve dynamic benefit scheduling | Cannot treat 2.7×, 15.1% FPR, 847 CVE replay, or live discovery as reliable empirical results |
| Beyond Single Bugs | Vulnerability density and count bias within the same long file merit being an independent evaluation dimension | Abstract 40,000 conflicts with the body's generation formula ~16,000; same LLM injects/ground-truths, no compile/PoC/human audit and no artifacts, cannot rely on exact model/language rankings |
| AgenticRepair | Structure, runtime, and historical context can be produced in parallel by specialized Agents, and the SEC-Bench official page supports the CVE subset 150/200 verifier resolution | Cannot treat 73.3% as a complete semantic correct-fix rate, nor attribute the 44.5-point difference fully to multi-Agent when budgets are unaligned |
| CodeScan | Designing black-box generation probes for known vulnerability classes can identify directed-poisoning models in the same experimental ecosystem, suitable as a model supply-chain auxiliary health check | Only tests CWE-79/295/200, clean n=18, BAIT aggregation not closed and same-source development; cannot extrapolate average ~0.9814 F1 to unknown backdoors or the real model market |
| ReasonVul | Different evidence roles and conflict-only debate suit security analysis better than simple majority vote, and the paired confusion matrix recomputes | PrimeVul/JITVUL FPR still 40.23%/43.57%; the 389/542 conflict-resolution rate cannot be reliable mechanism evidence without per-example artifacts |
| Co-RedTeam | execution, code browser, validation, and memory significantly improve exploit/PoC success given a task | Bounty detection precision/recall remain low, test-flow evolving memory has order dependence; cannot be written as a mature open-ended discoverer |
| VulInstruct | Layered knowledge representation of general specs and specific CVE root-cause/patch cases suits an audit Agent's specification memory | The paper's 55.8% precision is a Lenient rationale metric, with raw FPR 80.67%; zero-day deployment also has no target-CVE time oracle |
| CryptoScope | Crypto-specific knowledge, semantic retrieval, and vulnerability-pattern retrieval make model output closer to reference and cover more analysis points, suitable as a crypto-memory blueprint | Credibility, cosine, Semantic Match, and Coverage are not detection rates; knowledge base and CVE/CTF benchmark not isolated, 9 findings no external confirmation, cannot be written as real vulnerability-detection gain |
| SecLens-R | Vulnerability model selection should indeed be split by role, cost, throughput, and tool capability, not just a single F1 | Fixed weights/caps, missing-dimension dynamic denominator, and single-run are insufficient to support specific A/B/C grades or procurement rankings |
| Securing the Dark Matter | binary CPG semantic lifting, domain security knowledge graph, and graph reasoning are architecture hypotheses worth independent study | The `n=5` Wilcoxon `p<0.01`, 14/15, 89%-91% F1, and 91.7% reduction all cannot be reliably supported by current public artifacts |
| AdaTaint | project-specific source/sink adaptation and static-alert prioritization learning are important engineering directions | Paper retains 2018 placeholder metadata, project count and ablation percentages self-contradict, missing denominator, split, per-project results, and all artifacts; no headline number should be cited |
| REVELIO | Programmatically separating high-recall hypotheses and sanitizer-confirmed PoVs significantly improves report constructiveness, and the system did produce maintainer fixes and formal CVEs | CyberGym gives the vulnerable file, 0% FP is the PoV-replayable caliber and complete artifacts missing; cannot extrapolate 69% as open-ended whole-repo recall or claim near-solving memory-safety detection |
| LLMVD.js | High-recall Finder, Judge, constraint extraction, PoC execution, and human review form an effective validation funnel on the Node.js benchmark, and 433/517 and 244/259 PoC-effectiveness rates have scale | Automatic side effects still produce 69 invalid exploits, only 3 maintainer responses to the Wild's 36 valid PoCs; cannot treat as 36 confirmed vulnerabilities or an 84% recall for arbitrary npm packages |
| AEGIS | Repo-graph evidence trace, pro/con argumentation, and independent audit are more reasonable than a single text verdict, and top-k/cost/veto recomputation shows components have distinct roles | Most baselines not uniformly reproduced, FPR 36.78%, Audit veto only 56.5% correct and the public repo cannot rerun; cannot rely on SOTA ranking or deployment reliability |
| A2 / Android | On 60 Ghera apps, multi-model aggregation hits 47/60, proving static analysis, device interaction, and validation can compose an Android Agent workflow | The 75 TPs mix in 17 out-of-scope and 2 uninstallable; 104 zero-day, 51/75 actionable, and 82% token reduction cannot be cited as strong facts |
