```text
  -> VULBENCH-CPP-style compile/function/static/sanitizer/formal multi-layer verifier, coverage, and disagreement statistics
  -> RealSec-bench-style joint gate of build/functional/security for the same candidate, and correct that SecurePass@k must not be stitched across attempts
  -> Beyond Single Bugs's multi-vulnerability-density problem is rebuilt on buildable, triggerable, locatable paired variants
  -> obfuscation-paper-style metamorphic robustness testing; SECJS only as a JavaScript direction to be rebuilt after unifying data, code, and metrics
```

### Recommended System Modules

| Module | Primary source | Implementation requirements |
|---|---|---|
| Secure training data governance | Taught by the Flawed + SecureCode | Save analyzer findings and human/execution evidence; compare filter, reweight, repair, and contrastive pair under the same budget, isolate by project/CVE/clone, and measure functional loss |
| Continuous task factory | CVE-Factory | Real source and commit, dynamic PoC, per-item manifest, time and near-duplicate isolation |
| Rebuildable dynamic vulnerability assets | ARVO | vulnerable/fixed images, original/same-root-cause PoCs, patch provenance, environment failure states, and separation of full-volume and sampled success rates |
| End-to-end Agent overall evaluation | CyberGym-E2E | S1-S4 stage denominators, hidden-target PoCs, task-level build/test/PoC, image digests, per-task trajectories, and total cost; S3 must not masquerade as the target-vulnerability success rate |
| Real vulnerability-introducing scenarios | SecureVibeBench | pre-VIC commit, real requirements, gold patch, functional tests, PoV, SAST rules, and repeated runs |
| Cross-language compilable benchmark | RustMizan | vulnerable/safe pairs, file/function context ladder, mutation provenance, compile gate, per-call logs, and multiple runs |
| Vulnerability injection factory | AVIATOR | clean/vulnerable pairs, weakness/trigger/exploit grading, retention of unknown, project-level build/test/PoC verification |
| CVE executable reproduction factory | CVE-GENIE | Knowledge Builder, environment build, exploit, independent verifier, critics, patched replay, and Success@k |
| CVE-to-detector factory | QLCoder | vulnerable/fixed distinction, cross-project variants, rule versions, target-leakage boundaries, and human root-cause confirmation |
| Production detection rule factory | RuleForge | multi-candidate generation, positive/negative synthetic traffic, heterogeneous judges, weak-label enrichment, human approval, versioning, and rollback |
| Project rule and sink hypothesis generation | QRS + Argus (adopted with reduced weight) | Provenance from CWE/CVE to query and source/sink/API hypotheses; public query manifest, patched replay, negative-package sampling, and low-base-rate precision; forbidding construction of ground truth from one's own positives |
| External vulnerability-clue intake | VulRTex | issue/PR/attachment/comment parsing, source URL/hash, time-frozen knowledge base, project/CVE/clone isolation, and negative sampling |
| Security code review contract | SecureReviewer + AgenticSCR | diff span, issue type, support/counter-evidence, attack preconditions, and applicable suggestions; secure negatives, blind double review, and a factual-error taxonomy |
| Three-way context construction | VulTriage | Control/Knowledge/Semantic stored in separate databases, recording extractor mode/failure, versioned CWE corpus, and separate single-function and pair leaderboards |
| Specifications and threat model | SPECA | requirement ID, actor, asset, trust, boundary, scope, and cross-implementation 1→N checks |
| Business-logic invariant knowledge | LogicScan | BSL, source project/commit/time, actor/asset/state, near-duplicate marker, strict isolation and historical-knowledge dual settings |
| Project semantics knowledge graph | KNOWDIT | finding-to-pattern-to-semantic links, human quality sampling, project-specific specification, PoC/reflection write-back, and separation of union@k/single-run results |
| Crypto-logic specialized knowledge layer | CryptoScope (rebuild) | primitive, parameter constraint, protocol invariant, failure mode, standard provenance; time and near-duplicate isolation from target CVE/CTF/project, finally evaluated with blind confusion matrix/PoC |
| DeFi attack knowledge graph | EvoPoC (rebuild) | Contract Semantics/Failure Mode/Exploit Primitive typed nodes, cross-contract provenance, reachable path, gross/net profit, and single-model/union leaderboards |
| Specification knowledge and root-cause cases | VulInstruct | general/project-specific specification layering, time-frozen retrieval without target-CVE oracle, and joint reporting of raw confusion matrix and root-cause matching |
| CWE and project-rule semantic layer | VulReaD + AdaTaint | CWE, source/sink, guard, and sanitizer all bound to program evidence; project/time/clone isolation, with reasoning faithfulness and alert ranking evaluated separately |
| Repository semantic tool layer | codebadger | CPG caching, restricted high-level queries, path provenance, timeout, and resource isolation |
| Repository vulnerability localization layer | Antares | explore-first file navigation, layered candidates, GHSA/task/commit deduplication, layering of human root-cause files and patch-touched files, and freezing of model/CLI versions |
| Cross-procedure context compression | CPRVUL | caller/callee/global provenance, deterministic CPG edges, pre/post-summary token statistics, relevance ranking, and same-backbone function-only ablation |
| Closed-source binary tool layer | SLYP | COM/RPC/function entries, decompilation/cross-references, object layout, debugger, PoC adapter, and vendor status; same tool-permission baseline |
| Binary evidence confirmation | Veritas | witness-backed flow, claim schema, runtime validator, exhaustive/sampled candidate denominator separation, plus a replaceable open implementation |
| Graph semantic repair | VulWeaver | layering of deterministic edges and LLM-inferred edges, confidence, provenance, sampled audit, and incremental updates |
| Context community batching | Heimdallr | dependency graph, business community, function importance, and token-aware batching, not mechanically splitting by file size |
| High-recall candidate funnel | Antiproof + GONDAR + OpenAnt | scope, attack surface, detector versions, candidate coverage, per-level denominators, and failure states |
| Static-guided symbolic execution | Sailor + KLEECopilot | specification/candidate/path-budget/constraint/replay layering; random-path increment, crash, location, root cause, maintainer/CVE grading counts, and reporting token/CPU/disk cost |
| Secure-practice and edit state machine | GRASP + SCAFFOLD-CEGIS | practice provenance, semantic anchor, scope/build/test/security four-layer gate, counterexample backfill; invalid outputs remain in the denominator, and the gate and final oracle are not the same source |
| Multimodal candidate features | MULTIVUL (adopted with reduced weight) | non-code text only as low-trust features; project/CVE/clone isolation, trivial baseline, single-modality ablation, repeated runs, CI, and raw predictions must all be present |
| Project knowledge learning | 2601.19239 | automatically discover project-specific allocator/free, source/sink, guard, sanitizer |
| Historical vulnerability memory | Vul-RAG | preconditions, trigger conditions, dangerous behavior, fix solutions, and counterexamples |
| Recurring-vulnerability governance | MAVM | CVE root cause, trigger, patch dependency, candidate confirmation, interface consistency, and executable repair verification |
| Vulnerability lifecycle multi-artifact ledger | DeepTraVul | issue, PR, commit, diff, CWE anchor, link relations, and timestamps unified into a graph; isolate by project, disclosure time, and artifact hash, with applicable/full-volume denominators separated |
| Root-cause commit history investigation | AgentSZZ | blame-first, cross-refactor tracking, grep/log cross-file investigation, restricted read-only tools, and a counter-evidence role; layer earliest cause, propagation, exposure, and later-related commits |
| Logic invariants | Antaeus | sink, safety condition, trust boundary, project-level counterexample, and state-machine evidence |
| Goal-directed exploration | DREA + VulnResolver CPCAgent | Planner makes falsifiable evidence gaps explicit, Explorer is read-only; issue, symbol, dependency, and call context all keep provenance and total cost is counted |
| Strategy planning and experience screening | Mastermind | separation of strategy from raw trajectories, task-local Curator, failure attribution, equal-budget selection, and project/CVE/family isolation |
| Diff-aware security review | AgenticSCR | comment/task/vulnerability three-level denominators, diff-line binding, repository tools, SAST candidates, and CWE checklist |
| Multi-role reasoning | VulnAgent-R2 | expert roles only handle explicit evidence tasks, avoiding free-chat-style voting, and outputting raw pre-calibration evidence |
| Graph evidence debate and audit | AEGIS | clue, program edges, support/opposition arguments, and audit decision saved in layers; veto sets a reliability threshold and accepts static/dynamic oracle override, with fully reported rejected TPs |
| Hypothesis verification and counter-evidence pruning | VulAgent | TriggerPlanner, AssumptionPruner, FinalValidator; same-sample paired context ablation, per-guard evidence, and low-base-rate FPR gate |
| Multi-perspective conflict resolution | ReasonVul | Deductive/Inductive/Abductive bound to independent evidence sources, auditable conflict trajectories, external verifier decision, and retaining inconclusive |
| Specialized security reasoning base | VulnLLM-R | model, data, Agent scaffold, and project-level raw runs released by version; layering of localization oracle, open search, CWE, path, PoC, and zero-day evidence |
| Counter-evidence and false-positive governance | R2 + Sifting the Noise | Sceptic, cross-file/config verification, TP retention threshold, and inconclusive state |
| Spec-driven counter-evidence | Code-Augur | making input-domain/state/permission/resource assumptions explicit, assertion, guided fuzzing, and splitting spec-invalid from new-PoV |
| Executable invariant generation | FLAMES | score gap localization and guard generation separately; verify compile, semantic equivalence, functional regression, and exploit blocking level by level, and isolate the split by contract/family |
| Alert adjudication | LASAA | true/false/dependent/inconclusive, CC/LRE, trigger positive evidence, pre/post labels, and modification reasons |
| Causal evidence graph | DAGVul | attacker input, propagation, guard, sink, invariant, and impact bound edge-by-edge to program evidence; Lucky Hit listed separately |
| Logic evidence filtering | EntailLLM | per-step program edge and rule trace; knowledge graph as independent ground truth, and entailment must not masquerade as detection rate |
| Trigger point and path evidence | AutoTrace | ExactLine/Statement/Function sub-items, fixed denominator, source-to-sink witness, and failure classification |
| Post-crash line-by-line localization | T2L | structured trace/sanitizer/stack input, and layered scoring of repository/chunk/statement/exact-line/root-cause |
| Path constraint solving | VULSOLVER | source, transform, guard, sink, branch/context constraint, program-edge provenance, and external solving/execution verification |
| Harness and execution artifact quality gate | QuartetFuzz | P1 logic, P2 API protocol, P3 security boundary, P4 entry sufficiency, generate-check-fix, and harness-blame exclusion |
| Directed fuzzing | FuzzingBrain V2 | Suspicious Point, Direction, global/SP fuzzer, sanitizer, minimized PoV, and down-weighted retention of FP seeds |
| Red-team execution orchestration | Co-RedTeam | discovery/exploitation separate queues, isolated execution, payload validator, per-round logs, frozen/online memory separate leaderboards, and cost caps |
| Smart-contract attack synthesis | KASS | typed attack plan, inner/outer loop, Foundry postconditions, target contract bytecode/integrity checks, harness/cheatcode labels, and per-case complete artifacts |
| PoE/PoV generation and memory-safety confirmation | VulnSage + REVELIO | layering of candidates, PoE, sanitizer-reproducible PoV, maintainer fixes, and CVE; save harness, raw trajectory, failure reasons, patched replay, and per-item disclosure manifest |
| Dynamic exploitability confirmation | AXE + GONDAR + Antiproof + V2E + SmartPoC + CyberChainBench | real build, fresh replay, historical state, profit, sanitizer, PoC, Action-State differential, trigger/impact/permission/capability oracle; verification failure must not be treated as safe |
| On-chain security task environment | CyberChainBench | case manifest, archive RPC, source/bytecode, Harbor isolation, attack replay, normal-transaction regression, and raw eval tree |
| Discovery state machine | Antiproof | Hit, PoV, PoE, human confirmation, maintainer confirmation, and CVE grading, forbidding cross-level promotion |
| Alert handling state machine | CodeCureAgent | classify, supplement evidence, patch/suppress, build, rescan, test, human gate, and per-task trace |
| Multi-context vulnerability repair | AgenticRepair | structure/runtime/history parallel producers, patch-integrity preflight, equal-budget scaffold comparison, functional/security dual regression, and multi-file statistics |
| Versioned repair experience library | EvoRepair | success/failure experience schema, quality re-ranking, provenance and applicability conditions, cold/static/evolving protocols, task-order randomization, and Success@k sub-items |
| Repair knowledge pattern | VulKey | Action, Key Element, CWE, precondition, and applicable context searchable; Exact Match only as reference, and candidates must enter build/test/exploit and human semantic gates |
| Multi-repair-Agent orchestration | PatchIsland | heterogeneous repairers, shared build artifacts, failure-aware scheduling, per-agent/per-run/best-of-k leaderboards; plausible patch, correct patch, and vulnerability coverage accounted separately |
| Smart-contract knowledge repair candidates | SCPatcher (adopted with reduced weight) | knowledge graph, retrieval re-ranking, and Slither only generate and pre-screen candidates; final adjudication by functional tests, attack PoC, state invariants, and human review |
| Executable safety-property repair | VulnResolver + Vul4Py + PATCHEVAL | Safety Property generation, instrumentation, exploit/functional paired oracle, vulnerable/patched/negative-sample execution, multi-candidate patches, feedback rounds, and semantic human audit |
| Repair memory and training | MemRepair + SecCoderX + Vul-R2 | source tracking, cold start, failure trajectories; learned judge only as auxiliary, with RL reward mainly from execution/security oracle |
| Heterogeneous-model verification orchestration | Strategic Heterogeneous Multi-Agent (adopted with reduced weight) | strong-model candidates and local verifier strictly independent, equal-budget serial/parallel comparison, GPU/latency accounting, and reporting pre/post-verifier precision, FPR, and wrongly-deleted TPs |
| Multi-model candidate orchestration | Multi-LLMSecCodeEval | equal-call/equal-token budget, multi-model only expands candidates; filtering tool separated from final scoring oracle |
| Cost and task scheduling | VCAO + DREA | unified accounting of token, GPU, fuzzer core-hour, static queries, wall time, and human time |
| Multi-role decision dashboard | SecLens-R | raw metrics first, organization-custom weights, cap/threshold sensitivity, missing items as unknown, low-base-rate precision, and human minutes/TP |
| Context trust isolation | Contextual Bias + Words Speak Louder Than Code | code-only first pass, PR/commit/comment/author/existing-analysis separate channels, forward and reverse attacks, threshold-drift diagnosis, and adaptive replay |
| Domain-prompt drift evaluation | SecDrift | neutral/matched/full-context/placebo, interface and operation-change diff, leave-one-CWE/template-out, refusal splitting, and two-way manual sampling of SAST positives/negatives |
| Algorithmic-complexity dynamic verification | ShadowProbe | worst-case input parameterization, multi-scale repeated timing, JIT/GC/CPU environment recording, growth fitting, attacker controllability, and DoS threat model |
| Black-box vulnerability-family methodology | Dual-Mode Benchmarks (rebuild) | methodology/ARG development application isolated from sealed tests, high-recall lane and evidence lane separate leaderboards, rule precision/FP, and unified budget |
| Model and tool capability routing | ELVul (idea only) | per-expert independent output hash, project/time/family held-out, multiple seeds, and comparison against a fixed strong model and oracle upper-bound |
| Role-based workflow skeleton | Security in a Workflow (concept only) | Planner/Analyzer/Fixer/Verifier responsibilities and artifact contract fixed; explicit arbitration of tool conflicts, and full release of case/model/workflow/repeat manifest and execution oracle |
| AI code-quality calibration | Human-Written vs. AI-Generated Code | human/AI provenance, LOC/AST normalization, parseable/compilable rate, functional completion, and human confirmation of static findings in layers; empty outputs must not dilute the risk rate |
| Reused-component logic audit | SCRUTINEER | reusable security contract, component usage knowledge, RAG candidates, similarity, and snapshot conflict checker; LLM candidates must pass a deterministic gate |
| Call-context multi-role analysis | MAVUL | architect, security analyst, sceptic, caller/callee tools, and new-evidence stopping condition; a judge that knows CVE/label/diff is only allowed offline matching |
| Weak-label training and fusion governance | SPLVD + LibScan | label confidence/self-paced curriculum, project/CVE/clone split, fusion-trainer train/validation/test isolation, and confusion matrix auto-generating all headline metrics |
| Confidence and release gate | Security Calibration | raw/calibrated confidence, False Trust, coverage-risk, refusal rate, and external-evidence strength |
| Vulnerability severity grading | ReVul-CoT | only handle confirmed vulnerabilities; isolate the retrieval library by CVE/project/time, forbidding direct copying of neighbor labels |
| Vulnerability management orchestration | AgenticVM (adopted with reduced weight) | retain asset/package/version/path-level identity, separately reporting record/CVE/asset dedup, CVSS filtering, and ground-truth FP; after suggestion generation, wire in patch, test, approval, and rollout gates |
| Robustness testing | 2512.16538 | executable semantic-equivalence transformations, safe/vulnerability dual samples, and conclusion consistency |
| JavaScript project-level robustness | SECJS (rebuild) | frozen unique data/code snapshot, complete repository loading, real augmentation, vulnerable/fixed pairs, low-FPR threshold scanning, and raw outputs |
| Multi-layer security verification evaluation | VULBENCH-CPP | compile, functional, static, sanitizer, formal, PoC layering; cluster-aware statistics, coverage, timeout, and tool disagreement |
| Calibration-failure diagnostics | CWE-Trace | DFI, HDD, vulnerable/patched pairs, context ladder, contamination-decomposition recomputation, and confidence intervals |
| Time drift and continual learning | Hybrid-CASR | bimonthly disclosure-time windows, full-timeline near-duplicate dedup, window/replay/cumulative equal-budget baselines, IBR retention, effect sizes, and pretraining-contamination audit |
| Repository-level secure code generation | RealSec-bench | same candidate passes build/function/security simultaneously, layered by CWE, multi-oracle disagreement, protocol-semantic exceptions, and raw outputs |
| Multi-vulnerability density stress test | Beyond Single Bugs (rebuild) | 1/3/5/9 density, build/test/PoC, exact-set, instance/location recall, count MAE, and independent verifier |
| Paper evaluation | 2601.19239 + From Lab to Reality + ARVO | real-vulnerability recall, SFDR, PairAcc, out-of-time data, full-volume/sampled reproduction rates, cost, and human time |

### Minimum Paper Acceptance Line

If this project is to be submitted in the future, it should at minimum satisfy:

1. Isolate the known real-vulnerability set by project and time, and report recall, precision/F1, or interpretable partial metrics.
2. Manually re-review alerts on the newest real projects and give confidence intervals for SFDR.
3. Make fair comparisons against CodeQL, Semgrep, RAG-only, single-agent, no-CPG, no-Sceptic, and no-verifier.
4. Report first-index cost, online tokens, tool calls, runtime, and failure rate.
5. The verifier's build failure, timeout, and oracle unavailable must be listed separately as inconclusive.
6. Each high-confidence vulnerability must include file/line number, source, sink, path, guard check, counter-evidence, and dynamic evidence.
7. Check conclusion stability with obfuscation/refactoring variants, and use compile, test, and PoC to confirm semantic and vulnerability retention.
8. Release code, prompts, split, raw predictions, verification traces, and plotting scripts exactly matching the paper version.
9. False-positive filtering must simultaneously report TP retention; high-risk alerts must not be automatically silenced without human review.
10. The online memory system must report repeated results for cold-start, frozen-memory, online-memory, and different task orders.
11. Report False Trust, coverage-risk, refusal rate, and pre/post-calibration confidence; model self-reported confidence must not bypass the execution-evidence gate.
12. Repair papers must at least report build, tests, security regression, or exploit oracle; exact match/CodeBLEU cannot be the main security-success metric.
13. Structured reasoning must distinguish the final label, causal path, and evidence edges; a Lucky Hit with a correct label but wrong path must not count as a high-confidence success.
14. The detect, exploit, and patch subsets must be frozen in a manifest; the paper tables must be recomputable from the public raw eval tree, not just a later-evolved data snapshot.
15. The training reward, data filter, and final security score must not all use the same static analyzer or LLM judge; multi-oracle disagreement must be reported.
16. Secure code generation and repair must count non-compilable, functional failure, vulnerability-still-present, and oracle-unavailable separately, not only report the "passes the scanner" secure rate.
17. The review system must simultaneously report comment-level precision, task-level Success@k, and vulnerability-level recall, and cannot substitute output-comment ratio for vulnerability coverage.
18. LLM-generated verifiers must pass negative samples, patched-version replay, and an independent critic, and retain the verifier error rate.
19. Fuzzer cross-comparison must unify harness, sanitizer, CPU, time, token, and human intervention; results given a ground-truth harness must be listed separately.
20. Production traffic, IP reputation, and SAST alerts can only be weak labels; confirmed conclusions must be bound to exploit, differential execution, maintainer confirmation, or human review.
21. Agent-generated harnesses, PoCs, verifiers, and test scripts must first pass audit of logic, API protocol, security boundary, and entry sufficiency.
22. Binary systems must give the general Agent the same decompilation, debugging, and adapter permissions, separating tool gains from orchestration gains.
23. Multiple runs must report single-run distribution and Success@k, forbidding stitching best-of-run per object and then advertising it as stable recall.
24. Knowledge retrieval must publish a project/commit/time/clone/patched-version leakage manifest, and separately report strict-isolation and allowed-historical-knowledge results.
25. Complexity vulnerabilities must make public the input generator, raw multi-scale timings, runtime environment, growth fitting, and attacker controllability; two-point timing or a model's oral judgment cannot be ACV ground truth.
26. A security review's Non-Issue must not be defined directly by "no comment"; classification, localization, factual correctness, fix applicability, and text quality must be evaluated separately.
27. Issue/attachment/historical-case retrieval must be frozen at the target time, with random sampling of negatives; tracking only model positives cannot estimate real recall.
28. Methodology Agents and ARG must be evaluated on application-level sealed tests; development/training targets must not simultaneously serve as the final benchmark.
29. Dynamic routing must verify that each expert's input, prediction, and probability come from different models, and train the gate on external verifier results rather than model self-reported labels.
25. Automatic repair must separately report benchmark-verifier pass, human semantic correctness, maintainer acceptance, and regression coverage.
26. The multi-layer verifier's parse error, timeout, unsupported, no tests, and unreached paths must count into coverage, not be silently discarded.
27. Repair results must simultaneously report security-only, functionally-regressed plausible, and human semantic correct, and the three denominators must not be merged.
28. The benchmark must save compile status, context level, mutation provenance, per-run logs, and variance; a single model call must not support a stable ranking.
29. Strategy learning must separate training rollouts, inference Best-of-k, and one-shot scores, and prove project/CVE/family-level isolation.
30. The PoC verifier must compare the state before and after a key action; "script exits successfully" or an LLM sanity-check cannot alone be the vulnerability ground truth.
31. Contamination and calibration headlines must give recomputable set relations; diagnostic metrics such as DFI/HDD must not mask exact-CWE, localization, and pair accuracy.
32. The paper, website, code, and data must bind to the same release/commit; if the public implementation cannot recompute the tables, label it clearly as a design reference rather than a reproduction artifact.
33. The full-pipeline success rate and the randomly-controlled-sample success rate must be reported separately, with explicit denominator, resource budget, and failure stage.
34. Static findings must be calibrated by LOC, functional completeness, compilable rate, and unparseable/empty-output rate; without human or dynamic confirmation they must not be written as a real vulnerability rate.
35. An LLM judge that reads labels, CVE descriptions, commit messages, or fix diffs can only be used for offline semantic matching, and must not serve as a deployment verifier or precision oracle.
36. Random Forest, learned gates, thresholds, and round selection must be isolated from the final test labels, and a train/validation/test sample-ID manifest must be released.
37. Accuracy, precision, recall, F1, FPR, and AUC must be generated by the same raw predictions/confusion matrix script, and mathematical constraints and table closure checked in CI.
34. Known recall, additional reproducible PoVs, human confirmation, maintainer confirmation, CVE, and bounty must be graded, and must not be merged into a single "number of vulnerabilities found."
35. If evaluation labels are revised after seeing model outputs, triggers, or verifier results, the pre/post labels, modification reasons, and sensitivity analysis must be frozen.
36. Agent containers must hide the reference patch, patch URL, fix commit, fix function, and private verifier payload before startup, and prove invisibility with automated tests.
37. Multiple runs' union@k, best@k, mean, median, and single-run stable results must be separated; the total budget should include each run's tokens, dollars, and human-confirmation cost.
38. The auditor must adversarially test framing, anchoring, halo, author identity, existing analysis, and forward/reverse prompts, and distinguish threshold drift from true discriminative-power change.
39. Cross-procedure context methods must give function-only, raw-context, compressed-context, and token-matched comparisons under the same backbone and same reasoning supervision.
40. Continual learning must be evaluated forward in disclosure time, with full-timeline near-duplicate dedup, pretraining-contamination audit, historical-retention, and effect-size reporting.
41. Invariant/guard generation must separately report localization, compile, semantic equivalence, functional retention, and exploit blocking; stronger/weaker must not be merged into equivalent.
42. Zero-day or real findings must make public the project, commit, file/line, root cause, PoC/dynamic evidence, maintainer status, and fix link; quantity-only claims must not enter the core effectiveness table.
43. Multi-Agent repair experiments must align total tokens, steps, tool permissions, parallelism, and wall time; a single Agent cannot serve as a causal control at a lower budget.
44. Spec/root-cause judge metrics must be listed alongside the raw confusion matrix; reporting other vulnerabilities on patched code must not automatically count as TN in the deployment caliber.
45. Online long-term memory evaluation must freeze the initial snapshot, randomize task order, and report cold/static/evolving multi-protocol; test-task experience must not silently flow into subsequent independent samples.
46. Role-based total scores must make public the weights and reference caps and do perturbation analysis; missing cost/metrics must be marked unknown, and a hidden advantage must not be gained by deleting the denominator.
47. Statistical tests must be compatible with the sample size and make public the raw observations; if the exact test's minimum possible p-value is higher than the headline, the core significance conclusion directly fails.
48. Reproduction entry points must fail closed when data, models, or core modules are missing; mock/demo output must use separate commands and conspicuous marking, and must not enter the paper's result pipeline.
49. Intermediate-stage success and hidden-target success must be separated; localizing a fixable problem or fixing another vulnerability must not count as end-to-end success on the target vulnerability.
50. Exhaustive and sampled FP must be listed separately, with the verified denominator, sampling method, and confidence intervals public; a sampled discovery count must not masquerade as full-volume FP.
51. When online/test-time memory is transductive learning, it must be listed separately, freeze the initial experience, randomize task order, and report cold/static/evolving and one-shot/Success@k.
52. Context ablations must be paired on the same batch of samples, same model, same budget, and same candidate localization; context-available vs context-missing subsets must not masquerade as causal ablations.
53. Hitting a CWE ID in reasoning cannot replace evidence faithfulness; the causal consistency of source, propagation, guard, sink, impact, and the final label must be verified.
54. All summary percentages must make public the raw numerator, denominator, and recomputable baseline; relative improvement, percentage-point change, and relative percentage must not be mixed.
55. Prompt-drift experiments must note whether the interface, parameters, operations, and output contract changed; only matched tasks can be explained as a framing effect, and placebo and leave-one-template-out must be done.
56. The SAST flag rate must be separated from the real vulnerability rate; zero-alert categories also need false-negative sampling, and positive alerts need false-discovery sampling.
57. Exploit synthesis must distinguish open-ended detection, given finding, given CWE/location, and given report; different oracle information levels must not be mixed in one success-rate table.
58. Foundry/harness/cheatcode evaluation must prove target integrity, distinguishing externally reachable attacks, test-state preparation, direct storage modification, and demonstrations after changing the target semantics.
59. Cross-model union, best-of-model, retry, and single-model Success@1 must have separate leaderboards; the headline must not use a union exceeding any single model's result while omitting the total budget.
60. Knowledge-base benchmarks must be isolated by CVE, CTF, project, commit, near-duplicate, and time; text similarity, Coverage, or LLM Credibility must not masquerade as vulnerability-detection accuracy.

## 5. Usage Recommendations

- If you want to quickly see conclusions: first read this document's "Overall Ranking" and "Four Ranking Lists."
- If you want to design experiments: read 2601.19239 first, then the corresponding limitations and the minimum acceptance line here.
- If you want to evaluate whether industry/domain prompts change security: read SecDrift and fully reproduce the matched baseline, placebo, CWE exclusion, and SAST false-negative audit, not just compare two pooled percentages.
- If you want to do smart-contract attack synthesis: read KASS, borrow the typed plan and inner/outer loop; you must flesh out target integrity, per-case PoC/log, and single-run cost, and not directly cite 94.23%.
- If you want DeFi semantic and economic verification: read EvoPoC, borrow the three-layer HKG, reachability, and profitability; first clarify the D1 statistical unit and the 85/88 union caliber, then build public artifacts from scratch.
- If you want a crypto-logic knowledge base: read CryptoScope, borrow only the crypto-specific dual-path retrieval; redo the evaluation with strict leakage isolation, real negatives, confusion matrix, and dynamic verification.
- If you want secure training-data governance: Taught by the Flawed serves only as pilot motivation; compare filter/reweight/repair over hundreds of tasks, multiple seeds, multiple models, and build/test/security oracles.
- If you want a role-based secure workflow: borrow only Security in a Workflow's Planner/Analyzer/Fixer/Verifier boundaries; the experimental denominators, repeated runs, CodeQL service, and verifier must be rebuilt yourself.
- If you want end-to-end Agent overall evaluation: read CyberGym-E2E, reuse the 920-task build/test/PoC contract; use S4 as the primary metric, and add per-task trajectories, cost, and digests pinned for images.
- If you want stripped-binary evidence-first detection: read Veritas, borrow the Slicer—Discover—Validator boundaries; extend to optimized binaries and verify FP exhaustively or with statistical rigor.
- If you want repair-experience learning: read EvoRepair, borrow the experience schema and quality rerank; redo frozen-memory, random order, chronological, and one-shot comparisons.
- If you want trigger-hypothesis and counter-evidence search: read VulAgent, reproduce TriggerPlanner—AssumptionPruner—FinalValidator first, rather than copying the number of roles.
- If you want to build a CWE/security KG: read VulReaD; after fixing the project/time/clone split and reasoning fidelity, then evaluate the model gain.
- If you want to learn project-specific source/sink: AdaTaint serves only as a requirements checklist; do not cite its current percentages, first build your own recomputable data, alert ground truth, and artifacts.
- If you want a rebuildable real-vulnerability base: read ARVO, directly borrow vulnerable/fixed images, PoC replay, patch locating, and multi-PoC regression; when citing, separate the full-volume 68.8% from the random-100's 81%.
- If you want a specification-first audit Agent: read Code-Augur, turn each secure judgment into an executable assertion, then use guided fuzzing to distinguish real vulnerabilities from wrong specifications.
- If you want a project-semantic knowledge loop: read KNOWDIT, borrow KG—specification—PoC—reflection; when citing effectiveness, separate the single-run result from five-run union@5 and per-project budget.
- If you want smart-contract guard synthesis: read FLAMES, directly borrow the compile—semantic—functional—exploit four-layer oracle; redo the contract/family-disjoint split.
- If you want a real multilingual repair benchmark: read PATCHEVAL, adopt the verified oracle and freeze both the paper and current snapshots; before Agent runs, verify the reference patch is invisible.
- If you want repository-context secure code generation: read RealSec-bench, especially borrow the raw-output pipeline and functional/security joint gate; use multiple oracles to cover the CodeQL candidate-space blind spots.
- If you want static-alert adjudication: read LASAA, borrow CC/LRE/trigger and the dependent state; redo it on real analyzer alerts and freeze pre/post labels.
- If you want to test multi-vulnerability density: borrow only Beyond Single Bugs's problem definition, rebuild on buildable, triggerable paired variants, and do not carry over the 40,000-file headline.
- If you want to design automatic-repair experiments: read Vul4Py and list the four results — security-only, paired-oracle, human semantically correct, and cost — simultaneously.
- If you want a Rust/cross-language compilable benchmark: read RustMizan, especially borrow the context ladder, safe/vulnerable mutation, and per-call artifacts.
- If you want a strategy-learning reproduction Agent: read Mastermind, borrow the strategy Curator and dual-build verifier; redo equal-budget, one-shot, and project/CVE isolation.
- If you want to design a complete platform: first read Antiproof's detector-to-oracle funnel, then use 2601.19239 to check its real-world risk.
- If you want to first solve unreliable dynamic-validation artifacts: read QuartetFuzz and apply P1-P4 simultaneously to harness, PoC, verifier, and test generation.
- If you want to audit closed-source Windows binaries: read SLYP, first copy the tool adapter and debugger-PoC chain, and not the best-of-run headline.
- If you want to compare the real security difference between AI- and human-generated C++: read VULBENCH-CPP, with sanitizer mixed-effects and verifier coverage as primary.
- If you want to automatically consolidate security rules: read QLCoder, but position it as detector synthesis for known patches, not a zero-day finder.
- If you want to bring specifications and trust boundaries into audit: read SPECA, especially absorb the threat-model registry, and do not copy its post-hoc human ranking.
- If you want a pure audit Agent: read VulnAgent-R2 and DREA, and constrain reasoning results with GONDAR/AXE's dynamic-evidence gate.
- If you want graph-evidence support/opposition debate: read AEGIS, borrow the clue/evidence/dialectics/audit layering; the audit veto must be constrained by external oracles and wrongly-deleted-TP metrics.
- If you want repository-level memory-safety discovery—validation: read REVELIO first, borrow the cheap-model broad search, strong-model PoV, and sanitizer gate; do not extrapolate the given-vulnerability-file 69% into full-repo recall.
- If you want post-candidate exploit generation and false-positive confirmation: read VulnSage, borrow the constraint-understanding—generation—reflection—execution loop, but rebuild the PoE/exploit denominators and complete artifacts.
- If you want to automatically generate project CodeQL rules: read QRS, borrow the Q/R/S three-stage quality gate; forbid using the system's own positive set to prove 100% recall.
- If you want a heterogeneous-model verifier: borrow only Strategic Heterogeneous Multi-Agent's responsibility separation, and retest FPR, GPU, latency, and wrongly-deleted TPs on real low-base-rate data.
- If you want to extend static-analysis sinks with historical vulnerabilities: Argus only suits as a hypothesis-intake design reference, and its current vulnerability count or zero-day conclusion cannot be cited.
- If you want to choose a specialized security reasoning base: study VulnLLM-R's public weights and cross-language training, but do not write its given-target-function results as open-ended repository zero-day capability.
- If you want a causal evidence structure: read DAGVul, but must redo the experiments with independent ground truth, cross-model judge, and executable semantic perturbation.
- If you want to enhance repository context: read VulWeaver, and record LLM-inferred graph edges separately from deterministic program-analysis edges.
- If you want exploitability confirmation: read AXE first; it solves post-alert validation, not upstream open-ended discovery.
- If you want smart-contract alert verification: read V2E, especially absorb the trigger/impact/MC tri-state, and do not copy "no profit means low risk."
- If you want to upgrade smart-contract PoCs from "runs" to state evidence: read SmartPoC, borrow the Action-State DV, and do random human audits on both the confirmed and rejected sides.
- If you want a real on-chain Agent benchmark: read CyberChainBench, first reuse the historical fork, profit oracle, attack replay, and normal-transaction regression; first freeze the paper's 94/200 subset.
- If you want a smart-contract audit Agent: read Heimdallr, especially borrow the Profiler and independent Verifier, and do not reuse its FPR naming or closed-source absolute metrics.
- If you want trigger localization: borrow AutoTrace's witness interface, but must recompute the fixed denominator and strict statement-level metrics from artifacts.
- If you want logic-evidence verification: read EntailLLM, but must additionally report real-vulnerability ground truth, coverage, and exploitability.
- If you want to protect the PR audit pipeline: read Contextual Bias and set PR descriptions, commit messages, and comments as low-trust inputs.
- If you want to systematically do audit cognitive attacks: read Words Speak Louder Than Code, reproduce the forward/reverse framing/anchoring/halo matrix, and simultaneously report delta Recall, delta FPR, precision, and overall miss rate.
- If you want to reduce false positives: read Sifting the Noise, focus on the 22.25% true vulnerability wrongly deleted, not just the 6.3% remaining FPR.
- If you want to build the engineering base: study codebadger's CPG tool layer and OpenAnt's end-to-end stage funnel.
- If you want alert handling and CI repair orchestration: study CodeCureAgent's complete log and approval state machine, but forbid automatic NOSONAR on high-risk alerts.
- If you want training and continuous evaluation: study CVE-Factory, but rebuild the recomputable success rate and strict time isolation.
- If you want to generate hard vulnerability-detection data: study AVIATOR, but separate the 34-function local-weakness results from project-level exploitability.
- If you want to govern recurring vulnerabilities: study MAVM, rewrite 57.3% as a report-level correct-fix ratio, and add build/test/exploit coverage.
- If you want automatic repair: absorb MemRepair's failure-trajectory memory, while also doing cold-start and order control.
- If you want issue-semantics and executable-property driven repair: read VulnResolver, split 75% into the three layers of verifier, semantics, and maintainer.
- If you want crypto-protocol and library semantic audit: read Chai, first use multi-implementation differencing to produce hard signals, then check downstream assumptions along the dependency graph; finding counts must be layered by triage maturity.
- If you want Node.js package-level taint verification: read LLMVD.js, reproduce the Finder-Judge-PoC-execution-human funnel, and add public-API reachability and attacker control to the oracle.
- If you want to migrate PoCs to other affected versions: read Diffploit, borrow causing/supporting diff and annealing search; use a per-task manifest to automatically check the paper tables against raw results.
- If you want to prevent malicious issues from inducing repair Agents: read SWExploit, set issue, traceback, and reproduce code as low-trust inputs, and add least-privilege and independent security diff review.
- If you want a vulnerability-repair benchmark: read VulnRepairEval, but expand the original PoC failure into a four-layer oracle of vulnerable-pass, patched-fail, regression-pass, and variant-fail.
- If you want to evaluate cross-procedure context: use 2604.08417 as a negative baseline, add real clean samples, FPR/PR-AUC, and semantic slicing, and forbid using all-positive F1 to prove project-level detection precision.
- If you want execution-driven automatic repair: read ContraFix first, and bring same-crash/oracle-passing, RepairSpec, and human semantic correctness into acceptance together.
- If you want real Web vulnerability reproduction: read 2510.14700, and score environment, authentication, localization, PoC execution, and trigger by stage.
- If you want to batch-convert CVEs into executable exploit tasks: read CVE-GENIE, especially borrow verifier/critic/feedback; also verify the patched version, and cannot trust the LLM verifier's self-report.
- If you want to push static candidates into directed fuzzing: read FuzzingBrain V2, especially borrow Suspicious Point, Direction, global/SP fuzzer, and sanitizer; do not copy the unfair AIxCC ranking.
- If you want a production detection rule factory: read RuleForge, borrow multi-candidate, positive/negative tests, and the human release gate; IP reputation can only be a weak label.
- If you want path-constraint-style auditing: read VULSOLVER, borrow branch/context constraint; redo recall and false positives beyond the three classes of OWASP on real low-base-rate repositories.
- If you want commit/PR security code review: read AgenticSCR, report comment precision, task success rate, and vulnerability recall simultaneously, and cannot report only 17.5%.
- If you want to evaluate whether Agents reintroduce real vulnerabilities: read SecureVibeBench, separate functionality, PoV, and new static alerts, and run each configuration multiple times.
- If you want to turn historical CVEs into rules: read Phoenix, use vulnerable/fixed pairs to generate Gherkin contracts; do not treat the pair-condition F1 as open-ended recall.
- If you want AI Agent/MCP static auditing: directly study Agent Audit's rule/SARIF/CI engineering, but take its current recomputable F1 0.778 as the standard.
- If you want secure training data: borrow SecureCode's four-round schema and revision flow, and redo family-aware split, execution verification, and version freezing before use.
- If you want historical-patch retrieval and a repository Curator: borrow RAVEN's module division, force exclusion of target CVE/commit, and redo the Validator with build/test/exploit.
- If you want repair-model RL: Vul-R2 only suits borrowing the training framework, and the exact-match reward must be replaced with build/test/exploit/static regression.
- If you want secure-code model RL: SecCoderX's code, data, and checkpoints are most complete, but the vulnerability reward can only be a candidate signal.
- If you want a multi-model secure-code pipeline: Multi-LLMSecCodeEval only proves the external analyzer matters; must add functional tests and equal-budget comparison.
- If you want a knowledge base: reuse Vul-RAG's causal knowledge schema, but add a project graph, evidence citations, and knowledge-quality scoring.
- If you want a smart-contract business-logic knowledge base: read LogicScan, borrow BSL and invariant coverage, and strictly isolate targets, clones, and post-fix versions.
- If you want precise vulnerability localization after crash/trace: read T2L, evaluate chunk, exact line, root cause, and patch site separately.
- If you want confidence and automatic release gates: read Security Calibration, especially test False Trust, coverage-risk, and refusal rate.
- If you want to diagnose why a model "reports everything" or "reports nothing": borrow CWE-Trace's DFI/HDD, but the contamination set, exact-CWE, and localization numbers must be recomputed yourself.
- If you want vulnerability severity grading: ReVul-CoT can only be placed in post-confirmation triage, and isolate the retrieval library by CVE, project, and time.
- If you want AI-generated code-quality governance: read Human-Written vs. AI-Generated Code, reuse the paired design and public data, but recalibrate Semgrep hits by code area, functional pass rate, and generation-failure rate.
- If you want a library/framework logic-misuse Agent: first take apart SCRUTINEER's component knowledge and snapshot gate, then combine MAVUL's caller/callee and sceptic; all LLM candidates must be bound to deterministic evidence.
- If you want weak-label training or LLM+static fusion: borrow SPLVD/LibScan's problem definition, and re-experiment from project/CVE/clone isolation, independent fuser training, and metric closure, without citing existing headlines.
- If you want cost optimization: borrow only VCAO's problem definition, and re-implement and release per-task runs, hardware, and human-audit evidence.
- If you want paper innovation: obfuscation robustness is easiest to expand into a strong experimental chapter, provided you complete executable semantic-equivalence verification.
- If you want JavaScript project-level robustness: treat SECJS as a requirements checklist rather than a directly reproducible artifact, and rebuild after unifying data, loader, augmentation, TP definition, and low-FPR metrics.
- If you want to add realistic evaluation: read From Lab to Reality, and force in out-of-time vulnerabilities, full-file low base rate, and vulnerable/patched pairs.
- If you want to continually update vulnerability models: read Hybrid-CASR, adopt time-forward windows, replay/retention, and effect sizes; the 0.016 Macro-F1 shows complex systems do not necessarily bring large gains.
- If you want to compress cross-procedure context: read CPRVUL, borrow the CPG caller/callee/global ranking and summarization, but use the same-backbone 1.56-2.42-point ablation as the real gain reference.
- If you want multi-Agent scheduling and runtime self-healing: read SPEAR, borrow same-logic centralized comparison, failure class, recovery time, and coordination overhead; PFIR must add a semantic oracle and real baseline results.
- If you want low-cost vulnerability candidate ranking: read VulnScout-C, but first change the label-aware verifier to a blind verifier and report precision/FPR on real low-base-rate repositories.
- If you want to compile security specs into graph queries: read CKG-LLM, borrow typed SPARQL/KG execution; the training reward model and final judge must be separated.
- If you want multi-Agent verdict aggregation: treat MultiVer as a counterexample, forbid any-agent-positive union, use calibrated evidence voting, and report balanced accuracy.
- If you want a security constitution: borrow Constitutional SDD's versioned spec/plan/tasks and traceability, but each principle must be bound to executable checks, negative tests, and independent evidence.
- If you want a traditional lightweight baseline: 2602.23121 only suits as a CNN historical comparison; split by project/CVE/clone, and simultaneously report real clean-file false positives.
- If you want vulnerability-repair context engineering: read AgenticRepair, especially reproduce the three context producers, patch-integrity, and equal-budget ablation.
- If you want multi-perspective security reasoning: read ReasonVul, keep the three independent evidence classes and conflict trajectories, then decide with an external oracle.
- If you want exploit/PoC orchestration: read Co-RedTeam, especially borrow execution, validation, code browser, and the frozen-memory protocol.
- If you want a security-spec knowledge base: read VulInstruct, distinguish raw labels, root-cause matching, and real unknown-vulnerability retrieval conditions.
- If you want model procurement/role-based evaluation: read SecLens-R, borrow only the metrics catalog and do your own weight, cap, and missing-value sensitivity analysis.
- If you want industrial binary semantic graphs: you can read Securing the Dark Matter's architecture, but the experiments and artifacts must be rebuilt from scratch.
- If you want continuous vulnerability-repair orchestration: read PatchIsland, especially borrow heterogeneous repairers, shared environment, and failure-aware scheduling; the primary metric must be split into plausible, correct, coverage, and best-of-k cost.
- If you want an SZZ/root-cause commit investigation Agent: read AgentSZZ, borrow blame-first and cross-refactor tool investigation; redo the unified data manifest, calibration, and high-confidence error audit.
- If you want a vulnerability lifecycle evidence graph: read DeepTraVul, borrow the issue/PR/commit/diff/CWE link structure; first unify time splitting, dedup, and the applicable/full-volume denominator.
- If you want to add knowledge constraints to a repair Agent: read VulKey and SCPatcher, borrow only Action/Key Element, the knowledge graph, and retrieval re-ranking; Exact Match and Slither-alert disappearance must not be the final repair ground truth.
- If you want a vulnerability management workflow: read AgenticVM, borrow the deterministic parser, authoritative-source completion, prioritization, and approval interface; do not write CVE-ID dedup, threshold filtering, or count consistency as false-positive elimination and triage F1.
- If you want to write related-work history: 2308.10345 can be cited as early exploration, but cannot be used to prove GPT-4 is better than Snyk.

## 7. Final One-Sentence Judgments

The thirty-third batch further clarifies: PatchIsland's practical value lies in heterogeneous repair orchestration, but the AIxCC results prove that plausible patches must undergo official/manual correctness review; AgentSZZ and DeepTraVul are suited to building historical investigation and multi-artifact evidence layers, yet both expose the governance problems of the number register and time-split handling; VulKey/SCPatcher's knowledge representations are worth borrowing, but Exact Match and Slither alert-dismissal cannot be called repair; AgenticVM's modular pipeline is usable, but CVE deduplication and threshold filtering cannot be called false-positive elimination.

The thirty-fourth batch further clarifies: MPC-Patch-Bench and CHAINTRIX respectively provide blueprints for patch dual-verification and multi-pipeline audits, but the verifier itself must have human ground truth, and gold-file scope and open-ended localization must be ranked separately; prompt-syntax research cannot wrap the removal of security semantics as pure syntax causality; the IaC out-of-distribution ablation, the NFT positive-label rate, and the unrecoverable statistical register of the context-attack paper all cannot serve as real security capability.

The thirty-fifth batch further clarifies: HoF-Bench proves that when the target file is known, a small model can rediscover many real CVEs, but this cannot be extrapolated to open-ended recall or false positives; LibHalluBench's rich artifacts simultaneously expose the risks of malformed prompts, dynamic PyPI oracles, and no-code scoring; SecPI/MulVul's internalization of security reasoning and category routing are worth implementing, but the current numbers are limited respectively by a mathematically impossible table and missing experiment registers; PromptAudit should retain only the prompt-regression method, and the malicious-package paper retains only the dynamic-behavior candidate entry.

The thirty-sixth batch further clarifies: KLEECopilot is the most credible real-world experiment in this batch, but out of 87 independent sanitizer locations, random-path already covers 81, so the true method increment is only 6; Sailor's static–LLM–symbolic-execution method system is the most directly inspiring to the project, but its 421 crashes, 379 locations, and the "previously unknown vulnerabilities" must be strictly stratified, and the 2.29B token cost cannot be omitted; Antares proves repository localization can be trained into a specialized small-model capability, yet its benchmark duplication, patch-file ground truth, and unpublished 3B require a redo on an independent list; GRASP and SCAFFOLD-CEGIS respectively contribute the security-practice graph and the security-aware editing state machine, but the current experiments and artifacts are insufficient to substantiate the headline; MULTIVUL's OOD F1 is lower than the all-positive naive baseline, which is the standard counterexample that "being better than a relatively complex baseline does not equal true generalization".

The thirty-seventh batch further clarifies: SecCodeBench-V2's 98 tasks, dual oracle, Docker, and per-round register are the most reliable assets of this batch, but function-targeted tasks cannot masquerade as open-ended auditing; TPL-Benchmark proves Android version-level dependency identification is still difficult, and a candidate-set containing ground truth and the open world must be ranked separately; the LLM/SAST comparison holds only under the small-scale protocol that relaxes location; CoTDeceptor's adaptive red-teaming idea is strong, but its rollout, 500 samples, and semantic-preservation register are not closed; SecureRAG-RTL's all-positive examples cannot produce precision/FPR; LiquiLM's 90%+ F1, 238 alerts, and 10 CVEs all need to be re-substantiated from the public manifest, same-budget ablation, and executable verification.

The thirty-eighth batch further clarifies: DIG and PAGENT jointly prove that "program-analysis evidence + repeated input generation + dynamic feedback" is clearly stronger than a bare Agent, but known targets, arbitrary crashes, patched-build crashes, and new vulnerabilities must be stratified; A.S.E's repository-level tasks are worth integrating into the project, but `v_after < v_before` and `:latest` cannot carry repair ground truth and reproduction; EduPluginBench's P0-P4 domain contracts are a plugin/skill admission blueprint, and 100% same-spec mutant recall does not represent naturally generated security; Bastet's DeFi taxonomy needs frozen annotations and a disagreement register; BitCoder's most important conclusion is that structural priors may fail in the real world after synthetic saturation, rather than precise percentage drops across CWE proxies.

The thirty-ninth batch further clarifies: ClawHub proves that agent-skill security must retain multi-scanner typed signals and disagreement, and automatic registry verdicts can only serve as silver labels; VulAgentRL's introduction of CPG node/path evidence into the RL reward is valuable, but teacher nodes are not independent root-cause ground truth and baseline tool permissions must be fair; PRISM's NL/PL boundary summary is the most important new abstraction in this batch, but Good-Turing completeness, file clustering, and the 12-pair CVE extrapolation must be redone; CWEEP's public workbook and PDF can close precision and auto-fix negative results, but lack recall; ORACAL's antonym/deleting-critical-path attacks change semantics and cannot support semantic-preserving robustness, and Tool-Guided Repair remains a WIP missing ablation, missing artifacts, and with a miscalculated clean rate.

The twenty-eighth batch further clarifies: PHOENIX uses project historical defects and SBIR to form a cross-layer semantic bridge, and substantiates real-world value with 26 maintainer confirmations and 20 merged patches; ALIBI proves that natural-language evidence such as comments must be downgraded to low-trust input, but its 90%+ ASR is limited by synthetic ground truth and table conflicts; SecureForge's continuous red-team prompt optimization and public pipeline are worth dissecting, but the MH and SAST oracle conclusions must be downgraded; RepBench supports structured program-evidence compression, but does not isolate scope mixing; the AI refactoring-PR research supports a diff-aware security gate, but finding churn cannot be treated as a vulnerability rate; SAFuzz should retain only the risk-budget scheduling direction, and the existing data splitting and recall numbers need redoing.

**PVBench uses PoC+ to reject fake patches, AgentFlow turns harnesses into searchable typed programs, DrillAgent closes PoV with execution state, JavaVulBench provides multiple splits but artifacts must first be frozen, NeuroLog chains facts, Datalog, SMT, and ASan into an evidence chain, EvoHunt turns procedures into evolvable playbooks; REVELIO/VulnSage push high-recall hypotheses forward to PoE, PoV, and sanitizer-execution evidence, AEGIS layers clue, graph evidence, supporting/opposing arguments, and independent audits, QRS/Argus provide only rules and sink hypotheses, Strategic Heterogeneous Multi-Agent retains only the role separation of a strong-model candidate and a small-model verifier; ShadowProbe uses worst-case inputs and a dynamically growing curve to complete the complexity-vulnerability oracle, SecureReviewer/VulRTex define diff review contracts and external-clue intake, Dual-Mode/VulTriage/ELVul retain only vulnerability-family methodology, three-way typed context, and verifier-backed routing ideas; ARVO provides a reconstructable dynamic vulnerability asset, CyberGym-E2E uses S1-S4 and a hidden-target PoC to define the end-to-end gate, SecDrift uses matched/placebo/CWE sensitivity to constrain prompt-drift conclusions, Code-Augur/KNOWDIT/EvoPoC/CryptoScope/CKG-LLM/VulInstruct/VulReaD turn implicit assumptions, protocol semantics, cryptographic constraints, and exploit primitives into specifications and knowledge graphs, Taught by the Flawed retains only the analyzer-aware data-governance motivation, AdaTaint retains only the project source/sink adaptation direction, Veritas/VulAgent/VulnAgent-R2 converge witness, hypothesis, counterexample, and guard validation into an evidence protocol, KASS/Co-RedTeam compose plan, PoC, inner repair, outer replan, and execution validation into an attack loop, Security in a Workflow retains only the role-responsibility skeleton, SPEAR/ReasonVul define multi-agent scheduling, fault recovery, and multi-view conflict resolution, AgenticRepair/EvoRepair/MemRepair define multi-context repair and versioned experience; then by FLAMES/QuartetFuzz auditing the execution artifacts, Antiproof defining a discovery–validation funnel, PATCHEVAL/Vul4Py/ContraFix defining paired repair oracles, RustMizan/SecureVibeBench/VULBENCH-CPP defining compilable multi-layer evaluations, LogicScan/VulWeaver/codebadger/Heimdallr/VULSOLVER defining knowledge, graphs, and constraints, DREA/Mastermind/VulnResolver/DAGVul/EntailLLM defining planning and evidence verification, AutoTrace/T2L/FuzzingBrain V2/AXE/GONDAR/V2E/SmartPoC/CyberChainBench defining the dynamic validation chain; a real project should not copy any single paper, but rather turn the whole chain into a system that is recomputable, rejectable, and incapable of cross-level overclaiming.**

The fortieth batch further clarifies: OpenVul's pair-aware post-training and fine-grained reward are the strongest method assets of this batch, but the CVE/patch-aware judge and cross-split project structure mean P-Pass cannot be directly taken as deployment accuracy; TraceLLM's trace-first evidence chain suits on-chain investigation, but still requires a fair baseline and a transparent expert protocol; the reentrancy paper's dual benchmarks are valuable, but 432/436 data drift, missing line-level error denominators, and mixed weighted/binary F1 overstate the cross-group gap; MALF's industrial fuzzing division of labor is borrowable, but the statistics and zero-day evidence are insufficient; VulnGraph's 30,946 missing embeddings and label-word leakage, and AI Code in the Wild's ground-truth-less authorship attribution, are both evaluation shortcuts that must be blocked at the data entry.

The forty-first batch further clarifies: PBFuzz's typed constraints, program analysis, and property-based search are the strongest method assets of this batch, but `25.6x`, the single main experiment, and the cost table cannot be advertised across measuring standards; FuzzingBrain CRS is most worth dissecting for large-scale engineering and failure governance; Esim's SSG is suitable as candidate-similarity retrieval, but the 16-case manual judgment cannot be extrapolated as whole-chain vulnerability precision; npm slicing should retain the source/sink slice, and first fix the evaluator, the empty-slice fallback, and the per-package token ledger; BugSweeper's test-parameter tuning and replicated p-values, the firmware paper's denominator-less main table, and the artifact secret leak must all be blocked by the Evaluation Integrity Gate and the Artifact Secret Gate before publication.

The forty-second batch further clarifies: Re-Evaluating EVMBench's cross-scaffold, exploit execution, and grader adversarial framework is still the strongest asset of this batch, but the fixed artifacts directly overturn the 22-event contamination-free claim; STELP is suitable as an additional layer for secure execution, but cannot replace system sandboxing; DVDR's multi-model disagreements should trigger evidence validation rather than selecting secure patches by text similarity and complexity; Who Said CVE? proves that the time window, deduplication keys, and hand-written graph data can materially change the ratios; FPEval and the vulnerability-analysis PoC require the Evaluation Integrity Gate to automatically check all-tests-passed, fixed denominators, paired analysis, table closure, counterexamples to conclusions, and secret-safe logging.

The forty-third batch further clarifies: EvoVuln's executable policy, execution trace, batch knowledge evolution, and regression gate are the strongest Agent-method assets of this batch; ProfMalPlus's static/dynamic/cross-component malicious-package evidence chain is worth dissecting, but the execution surface must first be strongly isolated; GiAnt should retain the 7,711 real audit corpus and redo the benchmark; DeFiScreener retains only historical-pattern recall and call-graph validation; Verify Before You Fix and Code Provenance jointly prove that a detector flag or a disappearing regex cannot serve as a security-repair oracle, and the Evaluation Integrity Gate must also block in-library self-matching, ground-truth Top-K, task leakage, mixed statistical units, and non-closure of the paper's original tables.

The forty-fourth batch further clarifies: VIPER-MCP's static anchor, prompt evolution, runtime oracle, and tiered validator are the strongest dynamic-evidence architecture of this batch, INFRASCOPE's reference-vulnerability semantics and module routing are the strongest variant-hunting direction, and VulnGym's EP-Trace-CO is the most benchmark structure worth retaining; but candidate-union recall, duplicate SARIF results, KLEE path errors, wrapper compilation, sink_reached, potential vulnerability, and real maintainer confirmation must be strictly stratified. The Evaluation Integrity Gate must also require an open-world candidate universe, per-project/CVE grouping, API/compilation/solver failures entering the denominator, isolated execution of unknown repositories, per-item fix commit/CVE status, and main-table version-drift auditing.

The forty-fifth batch further clarifies: SEC-bench Pro's three-mirror judge, KVerus's dependency-scoped knowledge, Semantic Trap's paired benchmark, and HarnessAgent's active retrieval/compilation gate are the four routes most worth retaining from this batch; but target-conditioned PoC, best-of-k proof, paired classification, harness coverage, scanner detection, patch classification, simulated fuzzing, and real open-world vulnerability discovery must be strictly stratified. The Evaluation Integrity Gate must also automatically validate confusion matrices, prevalence, F1, confidence intervals, t-tests, the candidate ledger, and label semantics, and require that data augmentation occurs after the project/CVE split, that dynamic validation preserves real execution logs, and that production-CVE headlines provide per-item fix evidence.

The forty-sixth batch further clarifies: EVMbench's three-mode execution benchmark, PoCo's patch-blocked PoC, FuzzySQL's grammar/repair/replay, Reflexion's structured feedback, Okara's TLS flow/ART TI, VISION's paired counterfactual, and RampoNN's reachability-guided falsification can each yield useful modules; but known-target, open-world, alerts, PoC, Fixed, author-Verified, vendor confirmation, and CVE must be strictly stratified. The Evaluation Integrity Gate must also cluster statistics by repository, penalize false positives outside the report, freeze the test set, isolate the feedback and scoring oracle, verify functional correctness and semantic equivalence, automatically close denominators and metrics, and scan for credentials before artifact release.

The forty-seventh batch further clarifies: Locus's progress predicate, LASiR's state slicing and symbolic paths, PILOT's effective-input toolchain, SSR/GoAT-X's business property graph, FORGE's L0-L3, 2605.11163's counterfactual evaluator, and 2509.13023's templated Forge/Kontrol can all be split into project modules; but verifier timeouts/errors must fail closed, speedup reports use robust aggregates and survival analysis, properties pass schema/type checks, target-conditioned/open-world/PoC/maintainer-confirmation/CVE are stratified, the model judge is calibrated on an independent holdout, synthetic scaffolds are not attributed to real CVEs, and no counterexample found does not equal security.

The forty-eighth batch further clarifies: real vulnerability chains must preserve report/confirm/fix/CVE status, and fuzz comparisons must be evaluated on equal budgets and repeated seeds; static replay and sampled extrapolation must not masquerade as dynamic validation; crashes must pass caller/entry constraints; repair must go through targeted/full scan, compile/validate, plan/test, behavior diff, and intent adjudication; repository Agents simultaneously report prediction volume, predictions/TP, human burden, and cost. The best engineering combination is Hound's long-term evidence graph + FORGE's task forest + Following Dragons/MASFuzzer's dynamic search + FalseCrashReducer's entry constraints + Automated V&V's A/B execution + TerraProbe's multi-layer oracle.

The forty-ninth batch further clarifies: all ranking evaluations must save per-query qrels/ranks and automatically check Recall/Hit/ME and F1/confusion-matrix closure; ground truth must not enter the runtime scope/filter/scorer; missing outputs and parser/tool errors must be counted in coverage; multi-labels must not be silently first-labelized; model candidates, rule confirmation, manual confirmation, maintainer confirmation, and real prevalence must be in separate accounts. The best engineering combination is ASSEMBLAGE-DEEPHISTORY's cross-build regression set + PCodeTrans's executable pseudo-source layer + PatchSeeker's patch memory + ConneX's structured examiner + SecretLoc's two-phase review + SCD's dual-stream context isolation.

The fiftieth batch further clarifies: multi-language repair must be isolated by CVE/project/URL/clone/time and use executable oracles; SAST labels and real vulnerability ground truth are in separate accounts; the CVE-CWE-CAPEC/ATT&CK chain is verified item by item; taxonomy reward selects models only on validation and runs independent training seeds; feedback, reward, evaluator, and the deployment verifier are mutually isolated; fuzzing and SZZ treat timeout/unknown/failure as fail-closed. The combination most worth implementing is Cross-Domain's CI feedback loop + MAS-SZZ's historical evidence chain + AutoDecompiler's dynamic counterexample correction + hierarchical CWE reward.

The fifty-first batch further clarifies: all functional, security, and repair metrics must state the oracle type; reward/label/evaluator/verifier are isolated; multilingual and RAG data are cleaned by project/CVE/commit/clone/time; per-instance tuning reports additional training budget and parameter changes; training/inference shapes are fixed with assertions; code attribution supports open-set and anti-collapse; Agent automatic actions must have a run ledger and rollback. The combination most worth implementing is MulVuln's language routing + PSSec's analyzer/repair loop + SmartCoder-R1's security training framework + the Llama paper's classification-head QLoRA/RAG.

The fifty-second batch further clarifies: multi-step Agents take the cumulative workspace and the final PoC as ground truth; the retained stress set does not extrapolate ordinary-task base rates; all BROKEN/ERROR/invalid/tool failures enter the denominator; stratified sampling is restored by overall weights; attention/router naming must be proven by tensor shapes and input dependencies; corpus and knowledge bases are isolated by overlap/family/time; all headlines are auto-generated from the immutable raw manifest. The combination most worth implementing is MOSAIC's compositional tasks and final-state oracle + R+R's security knowledge injection + MARD's tool forensics/verifier + PYPILINE's supply-chain knowledge base.
